<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h3 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:1.05em">✅ 0.2 · Stage 2 · Tidying</h3></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

**Description.** Semicolon insertion, preallocation, `find`→logical indexing, deleting a provably unused assignment. Checked by a whitelisted-pattern diff **plus** a runtime golden-output match. A closed list of four: anything bigger is a Speed item (0.5), not a widening of this stage.

**Status.** Rewritten 2026-09-21 now that the data-path fix (S2.4) has landed and `run_demo.m` has proven, end to end, that `+lib/texture_patch.m` + the root decision-variable functions run correctly on real (small, git-tracked) Brodatz patches with no dependency on the ~19 GB natural-image set. Golden coverage is therefore extended, ahead of running any tranche, to every function whose only real-data need is Brodatz: `+lib/texture_patch.m`, `+lib/edge_props_stim.m`, and the root cluster-C decision-variable functions `re.m`, `rs.m`, `rs_new.m`, `rp.m` (see 0.2.0 below). `+experiment` stays pattern-check-only — Psychtoolbox/a live display, not data, is the blocker there, and that's unchanged. `+general` stays pattern-check-only too, but the reason has changed: the data resolves fine now, the remaining blockers are B2.12 (three unresolvable legacy functions), B2.13 (two `.mat` files absent from the store), and S2.5 (script, not function — no callable entry point to golden-test at all). `setup.m` and `texture_grouping.m` stay pattern-check-only (path-modifying / full-driver scripts, per section 1); `config()` was already covered and is unaffected. **The harness extension (0.2.0) landed 2026-09-21** — `texture_patch`, `re`, `rs` and `rp` are covered by real output checksums, while `edge_props_stim` and `rs_new`, which turned out to have no runnable path at all, are covered by the identity of the error each raises.

Note `rs_new` is covered by an error identity, not an output (B3.13). **Done 2026-09-21** — patterns 1-3 a documented negative; two `ASGLU`s fixed in `re.m` and one `NASGU` fixed in `rs.m`; golden replay passes.

<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h3 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:1.05em">🚧 0.3 · Stage 3 · Structure</h3></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

**Description.** Signature changes, `arguments` blocks, merging duplicate functions, reordering statements, float/RNG-order changes, renaming a config struct field or a frozen `save`/`load` name, acting on the Stage 1 commented-out-code triage — not behaviour-preserving, done after Stage 1/2, and not Bugs (0.4) or Speed (0.5) even if a fix here happens to speed something up. **Rating:** 🔴 clear win, low risk · 🟠 real cost (design decision or wide call-site surface) · 🟡 marginal, defer — a joint call, not a formula, say which factor drove it. Id `S<tier>.<n>`, a flat sequence sorted by tier then discovery order, never renumbered.

**To-do.**

- [ ] 🔴 **S1.1.** Merge the byte-identical duplicate files across `+experiment/+discriminate/+run/` and `+experiment/+grouping/+run/` (`display_level_start.m`/`give_feedback.m`/`save_current_level.m`/`stimulus_interval.m`) into one shared implementation. As of S2.1 the destination already exists: `+experiment/+run/`, the shared-hook package, holding `fixation_interval.m` and `response_interval.m`. These four go there the same way — note they are no longer *byte*-identical, only code-identical: Stage 1's tranche 6 gave each copy a tree-specific header and `See also`. Three of the four also read fields the grouping tree never sets (B2.9), so the merged copy's header has to say so once rather than twice.
- [x] 🔴 **S1.2.** `+grouping/find_xy.m:25-65` inlines `check_xy.m`'s eight-neighbour boundary test verbatim — same comparisons, same order, same variable roles. The whole `while` body reduces to `is_boundary = grouping.check_xy(x, y, trial, maps, grid_size);`, which draws the same random numbers in the same order, so it is output-identical. Clear win, low risk, one call site. **Done 2026-10-05** — exactly that one-line replacement, plus the header sentence that described the inline copy. See section 3.3.
- [x] 🟠 **S2.1.** Merge the near-duplicate files in the same two trees that differ by a handful of lines (`response_interval.m`, `load_current_session.m`, `fixation_interval.m`, `+prep/setup_subject.m`, `run_experiment.m`, `load_stimuli.m`), deciding how to parametrize the discriminate-vs-grouping difference. **Done 2026-10-05** — `fixation_interval.m` and `response_interval.m` merged into a new shared package `+experiment/+run/`, each taking one logical argument that keeps each tree's own behaviour. The other four were deliberately not merged: `run_experiment.m` is each tree's wiring, `load_stimuli.m` differs structurally, and `load_current_session.m`/`setup_subject.m` wait on B2.9/B3.14 (now S3.10). See section 3.3.
- [ ] 🟠 **S2.2.** Repoint `+lib/downsample.m`/`downsample_old.m` to `vislab.lib.downsample` and delete the local copies (both also currently shadow the Signal Processing Toolbox's `downsample` builtin).
- [ ] 🟠 **S2.3.** Repoint `+lib/power_dv.m` and `rp.m` (formerly `Rp_win.m`) to `vislab.nat_stat_bayes.dv_power`, which `simulate_discrimination.m` already calls directly; update the remaining callers (`texture_grouping.m:72`, `rs_new.m:49-50`).
- [x] 🟠 **S2.4.** Unify the paths used for the same Brodatz texture set onto `config.m`'s `cfg.paths.textures`. Three spellings, none of which resolves from the repo root: `'img_data/brodatz/'` (`+general/simulate_discrimination.m:82,410,416` — no `img_data/` exists anywhere), `'vislab-common/data/textures/brodatz/'` (missing the `../`), and — added in tranche 9 — **a bare filename with no directory at all**, `append('B', num_str, '.gif')` at `texture_grouping.m:~91`, whose `imread` is the driver's first data access. Filed here rather than as a bug, following tranche 8's treatment of the identical `img_data/` case: with the right (undocumented) working directory it does run, and the fix is this item's. **Done 2026-09-21** — widened in the doing to every data path in the repo that bypassed `config()`, not just the Brodatz ones, and to four more bare-`B<n>.gif` sites in `+grouping` that the recorded site list had missed. See section 3.3.
- [ ] 🟠 **S2.5.** Convert the `+grouping`/`+general` script files with no callable entry point (`mk_texseg_stim_points.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m`, `find_tex_regions.m`, `tex_regions.m`, `demo_mk_trl_points.m`, `compute_exp_error_mat.m`, `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m`, `simulate_discrimination.m`) into proper functions, callable through their package.
- [x] 🟠 **S2.6.** Deduplicate the three call sites that each independently build the same `mk_dist`/`mk_mecc`/`mk_decc`/`mk_bindex` grid for the same `grid_size` (`mk_texseg_session.m:75,80`, `mk_texseg_stim_points.m:64,69`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m:53,58` — line numbers as of tranche 4, up from `:37,40`/`:36,39`/`:29,32` before that tranche's headers and renames; re-verify again before acting) into one shared setup call. **Done 2026-09-21** — new `+grouping/mk_pair_geometry.m` returns all four as four outputs; each consumer's four calls become one. See section 3.3.
- [x] 🟠 **S2.7.** `+lib`'s `inputParser` parameter-name strings still carry the abbreviations the rest of the repo is moving away from (`'patch_sz'`, `'lum'`, `'cont'`, `'ppd'`, `'pd'`, `'w'`, `'ncolr'`, and `'filter'`, which shadows a builtin at every call site). Stage 1 could not touch them — a parameter name is a string literal, so renaming it is both `canon`-visible and caller-visible — so the receiving local variables were deliberately left spelled identically to the parameter (see tranche 2's rule B-1). Stage 3 should rename the parameter, its local, and the call sites together.
- [ ] 🟠 **S2.8.** `+general/nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m` is three near-identical ~60-line blocks, one per CPS image set, differing only in the loop bound (`n_img9`/`n_img10`/`n_img12`), the filename prefix (`'Set9_16_'`/`'Set10_16_'`/`'Set12_16_'`), the output variable suffix and the slice bounds. Collapsing them into one loop over a `{name, count}` table would delete ~120 duplicated lines — the largest single duplication left in the repo after the two `+experiment` trees — and would dissolve both B1.3 (the slice-bound arithmetic exists three times, and is wrong once) and B3.19 (`size_x`/`size_y`/`dist_min` would be computed once per image by construction). Filed 🟠 rather than 🔴 because the *benefit* is unambiguous but the *cost* is not: the three frozen output-name triples (`ptchn9`/`ptchf9`/`pcnt9` and friends) have to survive the merge, the file has no runnable path on which to verify the result (B2.12/B2.13), and it is tangled with S2.5's script-to-function conversion. Do this together with S2.5's entry for this file, not separately.
- [ ] 🟠 **S2.9.** `config.m`'s `cfg.color.rgb_to_lms` is a hardcoded copy of a lab-global calibration that already has a canonical home: it is **element-for-element identical** to the `lms` variable in `../vislab-common/data/cps_rgb2lms.mat` (verified by `isequal` in R2024b, tranche 9), which is the file `vislab.lib.rgb2lms` loads and caches for itself — and whose header says in as many words that callers should not load the matrix themselves. Two copies of one calibration that can silently drift apart. Filed 🟠 rather than 🔴 because the fix is a genuine design decision, not a mechanical repoint: either `config()` loads the `.mat` (giving a zero-argument config function disk I/O and a dependency on the data store being present), or the field is dropped and every future caller routes through `vislab.lib.rgb2lms`. Either way `tools/golden_harness.m` checksums this field, so the harness has to move with it.
- [ ] 🟡 **S3.1.** Decide whether `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m` — a third variant alongside `mk_texseg_stim_shape.m`/`mk_texseg_stim_points.m` with no recorded reason it's still kept — should be deleted, once its Stage 1 commented-out-code triage and S2.5/S2.6 have landed.
- [ ] 🟡 **S3.2.** `+lib/edge_props_stim.m` returns 29 positional outputs, of which callers name 9 and discard the rest in long `~,~,~` chains (`+general/simulate_discrimination.m:96-101,146,335-340`). One returned struct would replace the lot. Tangled with the disabled contour block that owns outputs 16-29, so only worth doing once that block's fate is settled.
- [ ] 🟡 **S3.3.** Decide the fate of `+grouping/effective_distance.m` and `+grouping/find_tex_regions.m` — two callerless scripts that S2.5's list missed. Neither finishes its job: `effective_distance.m` cannot run at all (B3.9), and `find_tex_regions.m` builds a patch-pair table whose distance column duplicates what `grouping.mk_dist` already returns, fills three of its five columns, and then ends without reading it. Either finish them as functions (the S2.5 treatment) or delete them; the duplication with `mk_dist` argues for deletion.
- [ ] 🟡 **S3.4.** `+grouping/check_tlst.m` is misnamed: it never touches a patch list (`tlst`), it tests one side of one patch against the label map, and its signature is `(side, x, y, map, grid_size)`. Something like `check_side` or `find_free_side` describes it. A bare function rename is mechanically checkable, so this is only Stage 3 because the Stage 1 rename tranche (0.1.1) has already closed; two call sites (`mk_masks.m`, `tex_regions.m`) plus the golden harness.
- [ ] 🟡 **S3.5.** Split the multi-role loop counters Stage 1 could not split, because `check_stage1`'s rename map must be a per-file bijection and one old name cannot map to two new ones (see tranche 3's rule C-6). Sites: `mk_masks.m`'s `jj` (region index in two loops, grid x-coordinate in a third) and `kk` (patch-try index, then grid y-coordinate); `mk_texs.m`'s `ii`/`jj` (pool position and within-permutation position, then trial and region); `find_tex_regions.m`'s `index1`/`index2` (grid coordinates, then patch numbers); and, added in tranche 9, `texture_grouping.m`'s `ii`/`jj` (region row/column, then histogram bin index, then the two patch indices of a pair — four roles for `ii`), its `xx`/`yy` (a 2-element log-response vector, then a patch's pixel row/column origin), and `setup.m`'s `ii` (candidate-folder index in `locate_folder`, add-on-folder index in `ensure_addon_on_path` — two roles in two different local functions, which the textual rename map cannot tell apart). Each becomes two role-specific names once a non-Stage-1 edit is allowed.
- [ ] 🟡 **S3.6.** `config.m`'s `cfg.paths.data` and `cfg.paths.data_root` name two unrelated folders — `data` is this repo's own `data/`, `data_root` is the *shared* store at `../vislab-common/data` — and the shared suffix implies, wrongly, that one is the root of the other. Nothing reads either field yet, so the whole call-site surface is `tools/golden_harness.m` (which records path field *names*, so a rename does change the golden reference). Something like `cfg.paths.shared_data` vs `cfg.paths.repo_data` would end the confusion. Deferred here rather than done in Stage 1 purely because a config field is dot-qualified and `check_stage1` is blind to it (section 3.1.1, zone 2); 🟡 because the confusion is real but currently harmless.
- [ ] 🟡 **S3.7.** The repo seeds its RNG four different ways and none of them goes through `config()`: `texture_grouping.m`'s live `rng(2)` (a literal, and the only live seed that is not 0), `+general/nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m`'s live `rng(0)` with a commented `rng('shuffle')` beside it, `+general/simulate_discrimination.m`'s commented-out `rng(0)`, and `cfg.seed = 0`, which only `tools/golden_harness.m` reads. Route all of them through `cfg.seed`, which is what that field is for. Picks up the three *alternative* triage verdicts in section 3.1.4 that each say "Stage 3 should handle this together with the others so the whole repo seeds one way" — but note `texture_grouping.m`'s is live code, not a commented block, so no triage row owns it. 🟡: small, and changing `rng(2)` to `rng(0)` changes that script's output, so it needs a deliberate call rather than a sweep.
- [ ] 🟡 **S3.8.** `texture_grouping.m` hardcodes the 2-cue quadratic that turns its two raw decision variables into one (`q2` 2x2, `q1` 2x1, `q0` scalar, 16 significant digits each) — exactly the shape of the repo's shipped `fabric_bd.mat`, which holds a `fabric_bd` struct with those same three fields, but with different values (`q0` 78.1079 vs 26.9060; verified by loading both in R2024b, tranche 9). So a fitted model boundary lives inline in the driver while a sibling artifact of the same shape sits in the repo root, and nothing in the code records which fit either came from. 🟡 because the benefit (load the boundary instead of freezing it in the driver) is real but small, and the provenance question — which fit is current, and does `fabric_bd.mat` even correspond to this pipeline — cannot be answered from the code and has to be answered before anything is changed.
- [ ] 🟡 **S3.9.** The repo's *own* `exp_files/` store is reached by bare relative string at eight sites, the same convention gap S2.4 just closed for the shared store: `['exp_files/' exp_type]` and friends in both `+experiment/+{discriminate,grouping}/+prep/setup_experiment.m`, `+prep/setup_subject.m`, `+run/load_current_session.m` and `+run/save_current_level.m`. All eight resolve today, but only with the repo root as the current folder, and `config()` already exposes `cfg.paths.exp_files`. Found 2026-09-21 by the exhaustive path grep S2.4's fix ran; deliberately left out of that fix, which was scoped to the shared data store. 🟡 because nothing is broken — it is a consistency and robustness change across eight call sites in two near-duplicate trees, so it is also cheaper to do after S2.1 merges them. Note B3.1 is the one `exp_files` path that does *not* resolve, and its fix should route through this field too.
- [ ] 🟡 **S3.10.** Merge the two trees' `+run/load_current_session.m` and `+prep/setup_subject.m` into `+experiment/+run/` and a new shared `+experiment/+prep/`, once Stage 4 has decided B2.9 and B3.14. Every difference between each pair comes from one choice: the grouping tree's subject file carries no randomized stimulus index `idx`. Its `setup_subject.m` has the `idx` build commented out (triage verdict *alternative*) and makes `response` logical rather than `int8`, and its `load_current_session.m` therefore reads `n_levels` from `response` (B3.14) and has the `stimuli`/`diffpair` slicing commented out (triage verdict *unfinished*, the cause of B2.9). Merging now would need a flag whose only job is to keep B2.9 alive. Once Stage 4 decides whether the grouping experiment gets an `idx`, the two copies of each file either become the same code (a trivial merge, like S1.1) or the difference becomes a real design choice. Found 2026-10-05 while doing S2.1. 🟡 because it is blocked on Stage 4 and the trees are not runnable until B2.10 is fixed anyway. Note B3.24 lives in the `idx` build and should be fixed first.

</div>

</details>

<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h3 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:1.05em">⬜ 0.4 · Stage 4 · Bugs</h3></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

**Description.** Bugs found anywhere during Stage 1/2/3, fixed here after Structure so a later structural change can't clobber or duplicate a fix. **Rating:** 🔴 severity 1, silently wrong results on a path that has actually been run · 🟠 severity 2, reachable but not silent — errors out, or touches only plots/logging/display · 🟡 severity 3, latent, not reachable with the current config but a hazard for plausible future use. Id `B<severity>.<n>`, a flat sequence sorted by severity then discovery order, never renumbered.

**Status.** Three of these (B2.1, B2.2, B2.3) are pre-existing and already acknowledged in the README's "Status & caveats" section, which used to track them in a now-deleted `CLEANUP.md`; they're re-filed here rather than left undocumented.

**To-do.**

- [ ] 🔴 **B1.1.** `+lib/edge_props_stim.m:30` — a bare `randi(10)` with no semicolon, on the per-trial call path from `simulate_discrimination.m` (called `nchoosek(n_tex,2)*n_samp` times), prints to the console and silently consumes an extra draw from the RNG stream on every call, shifting every later random draw in the simulation.
- [ ] 🔴 **B1.2.** `re.m:103` — `for k = 1:nc1` inside the "patch 2" block (lines 85-119, mirroring "patch 1" at 48-82) almost certainly should be `for k = 1:nc2`; as written it iterates the wrong count for patch 2's labelled regions whenever `nc1 ≠ nc2`, on a live decision-variable function actually used in analysis.
- [ ] 🔴 **B1.3.** `+general/nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m` reuses a *count* as a cumulative *offset* when slicing out image set 12's patch pairs. Set 10's block (`:260-262`) is correct (`patches_near(..., pcnt9+1:patch_count)`, where `pcnt9` is a cumulative index), but set 12's block (`:340-342`) reads `patches_near(..., pcnt10+1:patch_count)` where `pcnt10 = patch_count - pcnt9` is the *number* of set-10 pairs, not the index the set ends at. With the file's own constants (104/90/197 images × 10 samples × 2 orientations) the set-12 slice should be `3881:7820` but is written as `1801:7820`, so `ptchn12`/`ptchf12` are saved 6020 slices deep instead of 3940 and their first 2140 entries are set-9/set-10 data; `pcnt12 = patch_count - pcnt10` is likewise 6020 instead of 3940. Silent — no error, just a wrong `.mat` on disk. Filed 🔴 because it is the script's whole product and the fix is one term, even though no in-repo evidence proves this script has been run since the constants changed; it is currently masked by B2.12/B2.13 throwing earlier (same masking relationship as B2.8/B2.9, which per tranche 6's precedent does not lower the tier).
- [ ] 🟠 **B2.1.** `edge_dv.m:2-3` calls `lib.edge_contour_props(...)`, which does not exist anywhere in `+lib` — errors if reached.

(b) Line 31 calls `abs(i1, k2)`, and `abs` takes one argument; from the surrounding code it was plainly meant to be `a(i1, k2)`, matching the outer test. (c) The inner test compares `d(k1, k2)` but the assignment beneath it takes `d(i1, k2)` — the two name different pairs, and only one of them can be right. (d) `dist_max` is computed and never used. - [ ] 🟡 **B3.10.** `+grouping/mk_mecc.m` and `mk_decc.m` measure eccentricity from the lattice point `(grid_size/2, grid_size/2)`, but with patch indices running `1:grid_size` the grid's true center is `(grid_size+1)/2`. On the live `grid_size = 16` grid the center is taken as patch 8, so patch 1 sits at eccentricity 7 and patch 16 at eccentricity 8 along each axis — an eccentricity field that is asymmetric by a whole patch. This feeds `find_bin`'s `bin_bounds_min_ecc`/`bin_bounds_delta_ecc` lookups on the live `mk_texseg_session` path, so pairs near a bin edge can land in the wrong bin. **Flagged with low confidence: this may be a deliberate choice of a lattice point over the true half-patch-offset center**, which is why it is filed 🟡 rather than higher; the tier reflects the small magnitude and the real chance it is not a bug at all, not a claim that it is unreachable. Stage 4 should make the call deliberately rather than inherit it.

- [ ] 🟡 **B3.12.** `re.m:59,64` (`for` loop over `grad_dir1`/`grad_dir2`) — when a zero-crossing pixel fails the gradient-magnitude threshold, `grad_dir2(ii,jj)` is set to plain `0`, but `grad_dir1(ii,jj)` is set to `0*n_kernels` instead. Both evaluate to `0` for any finite `n_kernels`, so the two branches are silently equivalent in every call this tranche could find — but `n_kernels` is otherwise completely unused in the function (it appears nowhere else), so its value has no effect on the result regardless. If `n_kernels` were ever `NaN` or `Inf`, `grad_dir1` would silently diverge from `grad_dir2`'s treatment. Latent: `re.m` has no in-repo caller (see the tranche 5 findings-log note), and no plausible caller would pass a non-finite kernel count.

Since `rp.m`'s very first live line is `patch1 = patch1.*win;`, any call through `rs_new.m` would error immediately with "Not enough input arguments." Latent because `rs_new.m` itself has no caller anywhere in the repo (confirmed by grep; also true of `re.m` and `rs.m` — see the tranche 5 findings-log note on B1.2).

Harmless in fact — every image in all three CPS sets is 2844x4284 (verified with `imfinfo` over the first and last file of each set) — but the whole point of measuring per image is lost, and any set whose images differ in size would either silently under-sample (smaller bounds than the image) or error on an out-of-range index. The two missing lines are exactly the two set 9 has.
- [ ] 🟡 **B3.20.** `+general/simulate_discrimination.m`'s first cell now fits **5**-dimensional gradient boundaries (`grad_m_same`/`grad_o_same`/`grad_p_same` are `nan(n_all, 5)`, filled from `edge_props_stim`'s five scales), but its third cell requests only four scales (`[grad_m_1, grad_m_2, grad_m_4, grad_m_8, ~, ...]` at `:437-447`) and evaluates the boundary on a 4-vector at `:453,456,459`. The shipped artifact agrees with the *third* cell, not the first: `data/model/texture discrimination boundaries.mat` holds `grad_m_bd`/`grad_o_bd`/`grad_p_bd` with `q2` 4x4, `q1` 4x1 (and `all_bd` with `q2` 9x9, which does match cell 1's nine-cue call) — verified by loading it in R2024b. So cell 3 works today against the 4-scale boundaries someone fitted earlier, and cell 1 has since drifted to 5 scales; the moment cell 1 is re-run and its currently-commented `save` is re-enabled, cell 3 errors on a 5x5-times-4x1 product. Latent in exactly that sense. Stage 4 has to decide which scale count is canonical (and the four-scale output names `grad_m_1/2/4/8` suggest the scales were once 1/2/4/8 px, with the fifth a later addition).
- [ ] 🟡 **B3.21.** `+general/compute_exp_error_mat.m:56-61` treats the two halves of the same matrix differently: the same-pair (diagonal) branch **accumulates** (`err_mat(tex_pair, tex_pair) = err_mat(tex_pair, tex_pair) + correct(...)`) and is then divided by the number of same trials per texture to give a proportion, while the different-pair branch **assigns** (`err_mat(tex_pair(1), tex_pair(2)) = correct(...)`) and is never normalized. If every different-texture pair is presented exactly once across the concatenated sessions the two agree (a single trial's proportion correct is just its 0/1 outcome), but if any pair repeats, only the last trial's outcome survives off the diagonal while the diagonal averages over all of its repeats — the same plotted matrix then carries two different quantities. **Filed with low confidence**: whether a pair can repeat depends on the real session design, which is in the out-of-scope `exp_files/` data, so this may be correct as written. Stage 4 should check the trial counts before changing anything.
- [ ] 🟡 **B3.22.** `config` is not a unique name across the lab's repos: `../camouflage_detection/config.m` defines a zero-argument `config()` too, and it is on this machine's saved MATLAB path. Whichever comes first wins, so a bare `config()` call resolves to *this* repo's file only when the repo root is ahead of `camouflage_detection` on the path — which `setup.m` does arrange (`addpath` prepends), but which is silently untrue if `setup.m` was not run, or if some other code `cd`s elsewhere first. Found 2026-09-21, empirically and by accident: the S2.4 fix's own verification script called `run()` on a file in a scratch folder, `run` cd'd to that folder, and `config()` duly returned camouflage_detection's struct, which errors on `cfg.paths.stimuli`. Ten files now call `config()` (up from zero before S2.4), so the exposure is new and repo-wide. Mostly it errors rather than lying — `paths.stimuli` and `paths.model` do not exist in the sibling's struct — and the three fields that do overlap by name (`data_root`, `natural_images`, `textures`) happen to hold *identical* values in both, since both point at the same shared store. But `paths.data` and `paths.exp_files` would silently name camouflage_detection's own folders. 🟡: latent, needs an unusual path order to trigger, and no current caller reads the two silent fields. The clean fix is to make the call unambiguous — package the config function, or have callers resolve it relative to their own `mfilename('fullpath')` — not to rely on path order.
- [ ] 🟡 **B3.23.** `+experiment/+{discriminate,grouping}/+run/response_interval.m`'s `response = -1;` (line 40/41, flagged by `checkcode`'s `NASGU`) is provably never read: the header documents `-1` as the "no response" outcome, but the `while true` loop right below it only exits via `break` (after `response` is reassigned to `0` or `1`) or, in the discriminate tree, an `error(...)` on ESC — there is no timeout branch, so the loop cannot exit any other way and return the initial `-1`. Deleting the line would be behavior-preserving by the letter of "unused assignment," but it would also erase the only surviving trace of a response-interval timeout that the docstring promises and the code never implements — a missing-feature bug, not dead code, so fixing it means adding the timeout, not deleting the initializer. Left in place here for that reason (see the findings-log entry below). Latent: reachability depends on getting past `load_stimuli.m`'s `B2.10` failure first, which currently blocks both trees before this function is ever called. Since S2.1 the two copies are one file, `+experiment/+run/response_interval.m`; the bug is unchanged.
- [ ] 🟡 **B3.24.** `+experiment/+discriminate/+prep/setup_subject.m` builds the randomized stimulus index with `for i_session = 1:n_sessions; idx(:, i_session) = randperm(n_trials*n_sessions); end`, but `idx` is `zeros(n_trials*n_sessions, n_levels)`, so its columns are *levels*, not sessions. The loop should run over `1:n_levels`. With `n_sessions < n_levels` the last level columns stay all-zero, and those zeros later index `exp_settings.stimuli` in `load_current_session.m` and error. With `n_sessions > n_levels` the assignment silently grows `idx`, and the `reshape(idx', n_levels, [], n_sessions)` below it then errors or scrambles the table. Latent because the discriminate tree's `setup_experiment.m` sets `n_levels = 4` and `n_sessions = 4`. The grouping tree's commented-out copy of the same block has the same loop, and its settings are `n_levels = 5`, `n_sessions = 6`, so re-enabling that block as written would hit the second case. Found 2026-10-05 while diffing the two `setup_subject.m` copies for S2.1.

</div>

</details>

<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h3 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:1.05em">⬜ 0.5 · Stage 5 · Speed</h3></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

**Description.** Optimization candidates found during the survey (section 2) or anywhere in Stage 1–4, never made at the time — done last, once the code is structurally settled and correct. Every item needs a before timing, golden-output equality, and an after timing; if the speedup isn't worth the diff, the change is reverted and the null result recorded. **Rating:** 🔴 large win, low risk · 🟠 real cost (moderate gain, or a large one that perturbs float/RNG/`parfor`-reduction order and needs a tolerance decision) · 🟡 small, speculative, or unverified — most items start here. Id `O<tier>.<n>`, same non-renumbering rule as 0.4. Separate axis, **verification status** — Proven, Perturbing, or Unprofiled (not implementable as recorded until profiled).

**Status.** No timing data exists anywhere in this repo — no `tic`/`toc`, no logged runtimes, no README note — so every item below starts life Unprofiled; nobody currently knows where this repo's wall-clock actually goes.

**To-do.**

- [ ] 🔴 **O1.1.** `+lib/steerable_filter.m:12-18` and `+lib/local_sd.m:18-24` rebuild their kernel/mask from a double loop on every call, for the same handful of `kernel_sd`/size values reused across the whole trial loop (`edge_props_stim.m:106-107` alone calls `steerable_grad`→`steerable_filter` 10 times per trial for 5 fixed scales, plus 10 more `local_sd` calls); `steerable_grad` already accepts an unused `'filter'` parameter (line 8) built for exactly this hoist. Unprofiled.
- [ ] 🔴 **O1.2.** `+experiment/+discriminate/+prep/generate_stimuli.m` → `+lib/texture_patch.m:31` re-`imread`s a Brodatz `.gif` on every patch draw — roughly 2400 calls over only 60 distinct files, each also rebuilding a fresh `inputParser`. Unprofiled.
- [ ] 🔴 **O1.3.** The `+grouping` texture-region pipeline (`mk_trl_points.m:37,40,43`, `mk_texseg_stim_points.m:202`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape.m:77`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m:205`, `tex_regions.m:100`) re-`imread`s the same Brodatz files every trial instead of once — same pattern as O1.2, different call path. Unprofiled. **Corrected in tranche 9: `texture_grouping.m` is not an instance of this and has been struck from the site list.** Its `imread` sits in a 4x4 region loop that advances a running counter, so it reads 16 *distinct* sheets exactly once each — there is no repetition to hoist.
- [ ] 🔴 **O1.4.** `+lib/edge_props_stim.m:80` calls `lib.target_mask()` unconditionally, at parse-argument time, on every single call — just to supply the *default* value of the `'target_normal'` parameter. That call builds a 256x256 mask with a double loop of 65536 `norm` evaluations, a second such loop for the normals, and a full `lib.steerable_grad` pass for the boundary ribbon. On the live `'tex'` path none of it is used: `bd_strip` is read only in the `'camo'` branch and `target_normal` only in the disabled contour block. Making the default lazy is a large expected win at low risk on a per-trial path (`simulate_discrimination.m` calls this `nchoosek(n_tex,2)*n_samp` times). Unprofiled.
- [ ] 🟠 **O2.1.** `+lib/edge_props_stim.m:152-153,188-249` unconditionally opens/redraws figures and plots on every call (1 `figure`/`imshow` + 15 `subplot`/`bar` calls across 5 scales); removing this, along with B1.1's stray `randi(10)`, changes the RNG draw sequence for the rest of the run, so it needs an explicit tolerance decision before it can be verified. Unprofiled.
- [ ] 🟠 **O2.2.** `+grouping/mk_texseg_stim_points.m:213-226,263-276,277-290` (line numbers as of tranche 4, up from `:169-178,219-228,233-242` before that tranche's headers and renames; re-verify again before acting) calls `figure; image; set(gcf); pause; close all` three times inside the triple trial loop, blocking on `pause` every trial; gating behind a debug flag changes interactive behaviour, not a pure no-behaviour-change win. Unprofiled.
- [ ] 🟠 **O2.3.** `+general/simulate_discrimination.m`'s different-texture rejection loop (`while true`, current lines 184-231) calls the expensive `lib.edge_props_stim` *before* testing whether the trial is accepted — but the accept test at `:223` reads only `pow_diff_this` and `hist_diff_this`, which are computed at `:203-205`, 15 lines earlier and cost a tiny fraction as much. Every rejected draw therefore pays a full `edge_props_stim` pass (which per O1.1/O1.4 is itself the repo's most expensive per-trial call) for a result that is immediately overwritten. Moving the `edge_props_stim` call below the accept test is a pure reorder with identical numerical output — **but** while B1.1's stray `randi(10)` is still in `edge_props_stim`, skipping the call on rejected draws changes the RNG draw sequence for the rest of the run, so this is Perturbing and needs a tolerance decision. Fix B1.1 first and it likely becomes Proven. How large the win is depends entirely on the rejection rate, which nobody has measured. Unprofiled.
- [ ] 🟡 **O3.1.** `+grouping/mk_dist.m:6-16`, `mk_decc.m:6-18`, `mk_mecc.m:6-18` are 4-deep loops over `sz²×sz²` of pure arithmetic on `x1,y1,x2,y2` (vectorizable via `meshgrid`), and `mk_bindex.m:12-19`'s 3-deep loop is exactly `reshape(1:N,...)` with a permute — was tangled with S2.6, which landed 2026-09-21: the three consumers now reach all four through `grouping.mk_pair_geometry`, so the loops are unchanged but there is now one place that drives them. Unprofiled.
- [ ] 🟡 **O3.2.** `+grouping/find_bin.m:11-25` does three linear-scan loops to find one bin, called `sz⁴` times from three call sites — replacing the whole pattern with one `discretize` call over the full matrices at once is a large potential win, entirely unmeasured. Was also tangled with S2.6, which landed 2026-09-21 — but note S2.6 only merged the *setup* calls: `find_bin` itself is still called separately from each of the three consumers' own bin-table loops, so this item's three call sites are unchanged. Unprofiled.
- [ ] 🟡 **O3.3.** Remaining small vectorizable loops: `thresh.m:20-28`, `nlsame.m:17-23`, `mk_win.m:23-32,34-47`, `re.m:54-67,77-86,116-125` (tangled with B1.2 — fix the bug first), `mk_contour.m:32-38` (its pairwise 8-connectivity test over all `n_pixels^2` pairs, vectorizable via `pdist2`/thresholding; the traversal walk below it is inherently sequential), `+grouping/mk_masks.m:75-88`, `tex_regions.m:66-76`, `mk_trl_points.m:18-28`'s block-mask construction, and (added in tranche 3) `+grouping/mk_texs.m`'s two double loops — the first is `tex_pool(ii:ii+n_tex-1) = randperm(n_tex)`, the second a `reshape`/transpose of the pool. Line numbers for `thresh.m`/`nlsame.m`/`mk_win.m`/`re.m` as of tranche 5 (up from `:3-12`/`:5-11`/`:6-15,25-30`/`:32-45,55-64,92-101` before that tranche's headers and renames). Unprofiled.
- [ ] 🟡 **O3.5.** `+lib/target_mask.m:77-82` and `:121-133` each loop over all `bg_size^2` pixels calling `norm` per pixel — once to draw the circular mask, once to fill the normals — where the blob branch of the same function already does the equivalent with `meshgrid`/`cart2pol`. Same shape of finding as O3.1, and tangled with O1.4: if the unconditional `target_mask()` call goes away, this loop stops being hot and the item may close itself. Unprofiled.
- [ ] 🟡 **O3.4.** `parfor` candidates: `+general/simulate_discrimination.m:93-99`'s image-load/OTF-filter loop (was `:30-36` before tranche 8's header and renames) is cleanly sliced with no reduction variable — re-confirmed in tranche 8: it writes only `imgs(:, :, i_file)` with `i_file` the loop variable, reads only `files(i_file).name`, and makes no RNG draw; `generate_stimuli.m:32-66`'s first loop (was `:13-46` before this tranche's headers and renames) is similarly sliceable — confirmed on rereading: it only writes disjoint `stimuli(:,:,:,idx)`/`seeds`/`coords` slices with `idx` computed purely from `i_tex`/`i_trial`, no cross-iteration reads. The second loop (different-texture pairs, now `:68-93`) is not: each outer `i_tex` iteration reads `tex(...)` entries written by every prior iteration, via `prev_tex = cell2mat(tex(n_tex*n_trials+1:idx))`, to avoid sampling the same pair twice — a genuine sequential dependency, not parfor-sliceable as written; `+general/nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m`'s three per-image loops carry `pcnt9/10/12` counters and `while dflg==0` rejection sampling, needing restructuring first — re-confirmed in tranche 8, and now more specific: the blocker is `patch_count`, a single running index incremented twice per sample inside the loop, which is a genuine sequential dependency. It is removable (the write index is `(i_img-1)*2*n_samples + 2*(i_sample-1) + 1`, computable from the loop variables alone), but doing so is S2.8's restructuring, not a `parfor` keyword change. Unprofiled.
- [ ] 🟡 **O3.7.** `texture_grouping.m`'s `clear all` (the repo's only `checkcode` `CLALL` hit, and the only lint message in tranche 9's three files) clears compiled functions and MEX links from memory as well as variables, so the next run re-parses every function the script calls. Plain `clear` does what the script actually wants. Left untouched in Stage 1 deliberately: dropping `all` is a behaviour change, and a script that assumes a clean workspace is making a correctness assumption, not a stylistic one. 🟡 and Unprofiled — certainly small next to this script's real cost (16 `imread`s, then 65536 patch-pair decision variables, each an FFT pair plus a histogram).
- [ ] 🟡 **O3.8.** `texture_grouping.m` computes both decision variables for **all** `n_patches^2` = 65536 ordered patch pairs, including the 256 self-pairs and both orderings of every distinct pair. `rp` and `Rh` are symmetric in their two patches by construction (`rp` compares two power spectra, `Rh` two histograms), so a little over half the work is duplicated: 32640 distinct unordered pairs would do, with the matrix mirrored. The self-pairs are needed by nothing either — the diagonal of `dv` is deliberately left at 0 and `thresh` forces the diagonal link regardless. Potentially close to a 2x saving on the script's dominant loop, but the symmetry claim needs checking against `rp`'s windowing before it can be trusted, and nothing here has been timed. Unprofiled.

<details>
<summary><h2 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:1.15em">1. How an agent should execute this cleanup plan</h2></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

`mtree` parses a `.m` file into MATLAB's own syntax tree, and `tree2str` prints it back in canonical form: comments stripped, whitespace/bracket spacing normalised, missing `end` supplied.

```matlab
canon = @(f) tree2str(mtree(f,'-file'));
```

This gives a fingerprint blind to exactly what stage 1 is allowed to change, sensitive to everything else. Verify it succeeds on all 74 in-scope files before relying on it in a tranche.

Reverse-rename check: clean a file (renames, header, reflow), then undo the rename map on the canonical form of the new file and assert it's identical to the canonical form of the old one:

```matlab
c_new = canon(new_file);
for k = 1:size(renames,1)   % renames = {new_name, old_name}
    c_new = regexprep(c_new, ['(?<![A-Za-z0-9_.])' renames{k,1} '(?![A-Za-z0-9_])'], renames{k,2});
end
isequal(canon(old_file), c_new)   % must be true
```

The lookahead `(?![A-Za-z0-9_])` matters: without it a short name (`patch`) can wrongly match inside a longer one (`patch_size`). The rename map itself must be a bijection with disjoint domain/range — assert this before using it:

```matlab
dom = renames(:,1); ran = renames(:,2);
assert(numel(unique(dom)) == numel(dom));
assert(numel(unique(ran)) == numel(ran));
assert(isempty(intersect(dom, ran)));
```

Caveat: `mtree`/`tree2str` are undocumented MATLAB APIs — pin the MATLAB version (R2024b, per the survey) in every commit that depends on this check.

**Stage 2's extra requirement: runtime golden output — narrower than the data alone would suggest, but wider than the original survey assumed.** This repo has no single runnable pipeline: `+experiment` needs Psychtoolbox and a display — that part of the original assessment holds. But the original assumption that the ~19 GB natural-image set was simply absent from this machine was wrong: the shared store `../vislab-common/data` is present (391 CPS images, 60 Brodatz sheets, verified on disk 2026-09-21), and since Stage 3's S2.4 fix every consumer reaches it through `cfg.paths.*` rather than a relative string. `run_demo.m` (added post-Stage-1) proves this in practice — it calls `lib.texture_patch`, `lib.power_dv`, `lib.hist_dv`, and the `+grouping` helpers on real Brodatz patches, headlessly, with no dependency on the natural-image set at all (only `+general`'s two scripts need that). The golden harness therefore covers two tiers: the pure, self-contained functions callable with small synthetic inputs (`config()`, the `+grouping` geometry helpers `mk_dist`/`mk_decc`/`mk_mecc`/`mk_bindex`/`mk_masks`/`find_bin`/`check_xy`/`check_tlst`, and the `+lib`/root utilities `steerable_filter`/`steerable_grad`/`local_sd`/`mk_win`/`mk_contour`/`thresh`/`nlsame`) — and, as of the Stage 2 rewrite, functions whose only real-data need is the small, git-tracked Brodatz set (`+lib/texture_patch.m`, `+lib/edge_props_stim.m`, and the root decision-variable functions `re.m`/`rs.m`/`rs_new.m`/`rp.m`), called directly with a real (small) patch and an explicit seed. What's still pattern-check-only, and why: all of `+experiment` (Psychtoolbox/display, not data); `+general`'s two scripts (B2.12's unresolvable legacy functions, B2.13's two absent `.mat` files, and S2.5's script-not-function shape — none of which is a data problem); `edge_dv.m`/`contour_blur_estimation.m` (not yet added to the harness — revisit if either turns out to be a clean Brodatz-only entry point); `setup.m`/`texture_grouping.m` (path-modifying / full-driver scripts, per the skill's rule to call entry points directly rather than through a driver). State the pattern-check-only reason explicitly in each such tranche's findings-log entry rather than skipping the question. No `parfor` loops exist anywhere in the repo yet, so there's nothing to check for sliced-vs-reduction safety.

Semicolon insertion is stage 2, not stage 1: `tree2str` preserves display-vs-suppress, so the canonical diff is exactly `X` → `X;` — trivially whitelisted, but not invisible. One thing to watch here specifically: `+general/simulate_discrimination.m:69,114` and `+lib/edge_props_stim.m:30` have *missing* semicolons that are almost certainly leftover debug prints rather than deliberate interactive output (all three are deep inside per-trial loops, not top-level script lines) — ask before "fixing" if in doubt, per the skill's rule, but the loop-nesting context here argues they're accidental.

Speed is its own stage rather than a corner of Structure because the verification differs: a Structure item is reviewed and reasoned about, a Speed item is not done until a stopwatch says it worked. Being last also makes it the one stage that can be dropped entirely without touching anything else.

Stage 3 matters most. The tempting changes are all stage 3 (see section 0.3's list) — merging the near-duplicate `+experiment/+discriminate` / `+experiment/+grouping` trees is the single largest one here. Every one of them changes output, call sites, or validation behaviour, so none of them belong in the behaviour-preserving pass — keeping them out is what makes that pass's diff skimmable instead of line-by-line.

**All three of Structure, Bugs, and Speed rate their items on the same three-colour scale** — 🔴 highest, 🟠 middle, 🟡 lowest — though what the tier means differs by list: how bad a bug is if left unfixed, versus overall cost/benefit for a structural item, versus overall risk/benefit for an optimization. See sections 0.3, 0.4, and 0.5 for each list's specific criteria.

When the pass uncovers a *structural opportunity* — record it in 0.3 with its tier emoji and do not make the change. When it uncovers an *optimization* — record it in 0.5 and do not make it, however small it looks. When it uncovers a *bug* — record it in 0.4 first, marked with its severity emoji; the fix comes later, as a stage 4 item.

Statement reordering is kept out of stage 1/2 even though it looks cosmetic, purely because the check cannot distinguish a safe reorder from an unsafe one.

**Before working section 0.3, check whether section 2 is still current.** If meaningful time has passed since it was measured, or the file set has changed, re-measure it.

**Golden-reference maintenance, Stage 3 and Stage 4.** Run `golden_harness('replay', ...)` after every Stage 3 or Stage 4 item that touches a file the harness actually covers (see the coverage note above — most `+experiment`/`+general` items have no harness to run against, so their log entries say so explicitly instead). If the item was meant to be output-preserving and replay still passes, nothing to do. If replay fails, decide which happened before touching the reference: the item was *expected* to change output — recapture deliberately and log what changed — or it was supposed to be output-preserving and something moved that shouldn't have, which is a new bug for section 0.4, not a reason to recapture.

**Stage 6 (Closing), once Stages 1 through 5 are done:** re-run the full check suite end to end (section 0.6's checklist) rather than trusting the sum of the per-item checks.

</div>

</details>
