<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h5 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:0.92em">Step 2 — `+lib`</h5></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

Run 2026-09-20, **MATLAB R2024b**. Baseline: HEAD (`7c0f3a479`), working tree confirmed clean with `git status` before the first edit, so `git show HEAD:<path>` supplied the `old` copy of all 14 files. Scope: exactly the 14 `.m` files in `+lib/`, plus a new `+lib/Contents.m`; nothing outside `+lib/` was touched.

**`check_stage1` over all 14 files — verbatim:**

```
PASS  +lib/compute_p_clipped.m
PASS  +lib/create_pink_noise_line.m
PASS  +lib/downsample.m
PASS  +lib/downsample_old.m
PASS  +lib/edge_props_stim.m
PASS  +lib/gamma_correct.m
PASS  +lib/hist_dv.m
PASS  +lib/local_sd.m
PASS  +lib/monitor_degrees_to_pixels.m
PASS  +lib/power_dv.m
PASS  +lib/steerable_filter.m
PASS  +lib/steerable_grad.m
PASS  +lib/target_mask.m
PASS  +lib/texture_patch.m

check_stage1: 14 / 14 pass, 0 fail
```

Run with a **per-file rename map**, not one repo-wide map: the maps are not jointly a bijection (`filt` is a *new* name in `steerable_filter.m` and an *old* name in `steerable_grad.m`), so a single combined map would fail `assert_rename_bijection` or, worse, reverse-rename a name that never changed. Later tranches in this cluster should do the same.

**No diff-reading workaround was needed.** The package-qualified call sites inside `+lib` — `lib.steerable_filter`, `lib.local_sd`, `lib.target_mask`, `lib.compute_p_clipped`, `lib.create_pink_noise_line`, `vislab.lib.otf_filter` — all name functions this tranche did **not** rename, so `check_stage1.m:30`'s lookbehind never had to reverse-rename anything after a `.`. Every one of the 14 passes on the unmodified checker.

**Golden-harness replay: passes unchanged.** `golden_harness('replay', 'tools/golden_ref.mat')` asserts clean (its `isequal` at `golden_harness.m:243` did not throw) for the three entry points this tranche touches — `steerable_filter`, `local_sd`, `steerable_grad`. Expected: these are renames and reformatting only. The reference was **not** recaptured.

**`checkcode`: 25 messages before, 25 after — identical, file by file.** `compute_p_clipped` 2, `downsample` 5, `edge_props_stim` 18, everything else 0, measured on the HEAD copies and again on the edited files. This tranche neither introduced nor removed a lint message, which is the expected result for a Stage 1 pass: every remaining message is a Stage 2 (`NASGU`) or Stage 3/4 (`STOUT`, see B2.4) item.

**Decisions made here that bind cluster B (tranches 5, and the `+lib`-adjacent parts of 8).**

1. **Header shape — the exemplar, followed exactly.** `%NAME  Summary sentence.` (shout-caps self-name, two spaces, sentence ending in a period), then the **package-qualified** signature line `%   out = lib.name(in)`, then optional prose, then labelled `Inputs` / `Name-value inputs` / `Output`(s) with each argument's unit, then `%   See also LIB.OTHER_NAME.` The `See also` follows the exemplar's own casing — package lowercase, function name in caps. Step 1's open question about whether to keep the shout-caps self-name is hereby **settled: keep it**, because the exemplar uses it and MATLAB's own `help` renders it as the topic line.
2. **A `Name-value inputs` block is its own labelled section**, separate from `Inputs`, since every `inputParser` file in `+lib` has both. Each entry states its default in prose.
3. **Rule B-1 — `inputParser` mirrors keep the parameter's spelling.** A local whose only job is to receive `parser.Results.<name>` is spelled exactly `<name>`, so the body and the documented API read the same. The parameter *string* cannot be renamed in Stage 1 (it is a string literal, so `canon`-visible, and it is caller-visible), so expanding only the local would leave `patch_size = parser.Results.patch_sz;` — a mismatch that reads worse than the abbreviation. Renaming both together is **S2.7**, Stage 3. One exception: when the parameter name shadows a builtin, the local takes a non-shadowing form — `filter` → `apply_filter` in `downsample`/`downsample_old`, following the precedent `steerable_grad.m` already set with `filt = parser.Results.filter`.
4. **Never introduce a bare `patch` identifier.** Section 3.1.1 proposes `ptch` → `patch`, but `patch` is a Graphics builtin, and the whole point of the shadow sweep is not to create new ones. Use a qualified form instead: `patch1`/`patch2` (`hist_dv`), `patch_a`/`patch_b` (`power_dv`, already so named), `patch_img` (`texture_patch`'s output). **This amends section 3.1.1's proposed expansion for `ptch`** and binds every later tranche.
5. **The `*_dv` functions return `dv`.** `hist_dv`'s `Rh` and `power_dv`'s `Rpout` both became `dv` — one name for the decision variable these functions exist to compute, and it removes the last TitleCase leftovers in `+lib`. Tranche 5 should do the same to `re.m`/`rs.m`/`rs_new.m`/`rp.m`'s `Reout`/`Rsout`/`Rpout`, which step 1 deliberately left mismatched.
6. **Function bodies are indented four spaces and every file ends in `end`.** This is the skill's "four spaces per level, including the function body", and both halves are invisible to `canon` — verified empirically on the first three files before committing to it, since 11 of the 14 previously had neither. Continuation lines get a four-space hanging indent from the statement's own indent, never alignment under the opening delimiter.
7. **Two spacing exceptions to the general "space around binary operators, space after commas" rule**, both because the alternative reads worse in MATLAB and both applied consistently across all 14 files: (a) **a subscript list made of bare colons and simple indices stays tight** — `grad(:,:,1)`, `grads(:,:,:,i_scale,2)`, `grad_m_bins(i_scale,:)`, not `grad(:, :, 1)` — while a subscript list holding a range or a bracketed expression is spaced like any argument list (`stim_sd_padded(pad_len+1:end-pad_len, pad_len+1:end-pad_len)`, `mask_edge(:, [1 end])`), and a subscript list of named variables is too (`nhood(i_row, i_col)`). Function-call argument lists are always spaced. (b) **index and range arithmetic stays tight** — `2:bg_size-1`, `pad_len+1:end-pad_len`, `stim_size+1:2*stim_size` — since spacing a colon expression reads as a typo. Statement-level arithmetic is spaced normally.
8. **Renames are propagated into commented-out code when, and only when, the commented code is in the same scope and still refers to live variables** — so it would still run if uncommented. `epsl` → `eps_offset` and `stim_sz` → `stim_size` were therefore rewritten inside `edge_props_stim.m`'s disabled blocks, and `filter` → `filt` inside `steerable_filter.m`'s. Where a commented block refers to a loop or variable that no longer exists (`target_mask.m`'s "old method" block, which uses an `i`/`j` loop that is gone), it was left verbatim. Nothing was deleted, converted, reflowed or re-worded in any commented block; the only other change made to one is the uniform four-space body indent from decision 6, and a trailing-whitespace strip.
9. **`N_a`/`N_b` became `n_elem_a`/`n_elem_b`, not `n_a`/`n_b`.** The obvious lowercase form would have collided with `n_a`/`n_b` inside the disabled contour block, where they mean the *number of contours*, not the number of elements. Renaming into a collision would have quietly broken the block if it is ever revived.
10. **Units are stated in every header, and where a unit could not be verified from the code it is named as such rather than guessed.** Three that needed care: `steerable_grad`'s `'normalize'` is documented as being in the same squared gray levels as `sd^2`, since it is added under a square root to `stim_sd.^2`; `power_dv`'s `noise_const` is documented as dimensionless because it is added to a power spectrum already normalized to unit mean; and `edge_props_stim`'s LLR outputs are documented as "log-nats", since the function takes `log` of a quantity that is itself a log-likelihood ratio in nats.

**Header-doc corrections made along the way** (all comment-only, so invisible to `canon`): `gamma_correct.m` and `monitor_degrees_to_pixels.m` both carried a copy-pasted `Example:` block naming `SAMPLEPATCHESFOREXPERIMENT` / `MONITOR_DEGREES_TO_PIXELS(ImgStats)`, neither of which matches the function's actual signature — replaced with real signatures; the `v1.0 … Steve Sebastian` attribution lines were kept. `compute_p_clipped.m`'s comment called its result a percentage; the arithmetic returns a fraction, and the header now says so. `power_dv.m`'s header named its arguments `ptch1`/`ptch2` while the code calls them `patch_a`/`patch_b`. **Two of section 2's header counts were wrong for this directory, and are corrected here.** One of the four headerless files it found is in this tranche — `target_mask.m`, as predicted — but section 2 also lists `edge_props_stim.m`, `local_sd.m` and `steerable_grad.m` among the only three files in the repo with a labelled `Inputs`/`Output` block, and **none of the three had one**: `local_sd.m` and `steerable_grad.m` opened with a single unlabelled description line, and `edge_props_stim.m` had no header whatsoever, going straight from its signature to `%% parse inputs`. So this tranche wrote all 14 headers from scratch rather than extending three existing ones, and the repo's true "labelled Inputs/Output" count before this step was **zero**, not three. Section 2 should be re-measured at Stage 6 (0.6.4) rather than trusted on this point.

**Line-length exceptions — 14 lines over 100 characters, every one of them inside a commented-out block** (`edge_props_stim.m:150, 151, 177, 347, 432, 456, 549, 569, 575, 582, 587, 592, 597, 602`). Reflowing them would mean rewriting dead code, which step 2c forbids until its Stage 3 verdict lands; several only crossed 100 because of the four-space body indent from decision 6. No live line in the tranche exceeds 100. `target_mask.m`'s 6-output signature and `edge_props_stim.m`'s 29-output signature were both wrapped with `...` to stay under.

**Commented-out code.** All five blocks the survey located in this tranche were triaged, plus ten more it missed — 15 rows total in section 3.1.4, covering `edge_props_stim.m` (6), `target_mask.m` (4), `steerable_filter.m` (2), `steerable_grad.m` (2) and `downsample.m` (1). Verdicts: 2 superseded, 1 scaffolding, 7 alternative, 3 unfinished, 2 unknown. Nothing was deleted or converted. The two *unknown* verdicts are genuine: `downsample.m:55`'s upsample-back line and `edge_props_stim.m:129-131`'s scale-5 bin override have no recoverable purpose in the surrounding code, and no `git log` was consulted.

**Bugs found — five new, all recorded and none fixed.** 🟠 **B2.4** (`edge_props_stim`'s 14 unassigned outputs, which its live caller requests — the source of the file's 15 `STOUT` messages), 🟡 **B3.4** (`create_pink_noise_line`'s `exist('alpha','var')` guard silently ignoring the caller's exponent), 🟡 **B3.5** (the live PCA branch discarding `grads_pca`, and hardcoding `[256, 256, 5, 2]`), 🟡 **B3.6** (the `'camo'` path indexing a fourth dimension `grad_mags` does not have — and `'camo'` is the *default* `stim_type`), 🟡 **B3.7**/🟡 **B3.8** (`downsample_old`'s unassigned `img` on RGB input, and its call to the non-existent `aply_otf`). B1.1's `randi(10)` at what is now `edge_props_stim.m:89` was already recorded by the survey and is untouched, semicolon and all — adding the semicolon is Stage 2, removing the line is Stage 4.

**One suspected bug checked and cleared.** `hist_dv` normalizes its bin counts by `n_bins`, the *number of bins*, where the structurally identical code in `edge_props_stim.m:237-249` normalizes by the number of elements — which looks like a straightforward mix-up. It is not: writing out `sum_num - sum_den`, every `log(n_bins)` term cancels and the result is `Σ N₁ log N₁ + Σ N₂ log N₂ − Σ(N₁+N₂)log(N₁+N₂) + T log 2`, independent of `n_bins` entirely. The normalizer's value cannot affect the output, so there is nothing to fix. The header now says so, so the next reader does not have to redo the algebra.

**Optimizations spotted — two new, neither made.** 🔴 **O1.4** (`edge_props_stim.m:80` calls `lib.target_mask()` unconditionally at parse time, on every trial, to supply a *default* that the live `'tex'` path never reads — two 65536-iteration loops plus a full `steerable_grad` pass, wasted) and 🟡 **O3.5** (`target_mask`'s own two pixel loops, vectorizable the way its blob branch already is). Both Unprofiled. O1.1's hoist of the `steerable_filter`/`local_sd` kernel rebuild was re-confirmed while reading those two files and needs no change to its write-up.

**Structural items — two new.** 🟠 **S2.7** (the `inputParser` parameter strings that rule B-1 deliberately left alone) and 🟡 **S3.2** (`edge_props_stim`'s 29 positional outputs). S2.2's premise was also re-confirmed by reading: nothing in the repo calls `lib.downsample` or `lib.downsample_old`, which is what keeps B3.7/B3.8 latent.

**Deliberately left alone.** `compute_p_clipped.m`'s two `LOGSUM` lint suggestions (`sum(logical)` → `nnz`) — a real AST change, not one of Stage 2's four patterns, and too marginal to be worth a Stage 3 item; noted here so the next reader knows it was seen. The hardcoded Brodatz path in `texture_patch.m:58` — already S2.4. Every `save`/`load` string literal and zone-3 frozen name: `+lib` contains none, a documented negative.

**`Contents.m`.** `+lib/Contents.m` created — the repo's first — listing all 14 functions in four groups (stimuli and masks, gradients and local statistics, decision variables, display and image utilities) with the one-line summary from each file's own header, so the two cannot drift apart silently.

**Baseline note for later tranches.** The working tree is clean again after this step's commit; HEAD remains a valid `check_stage1` baseline, and no `+lib` path changed in this step.

</div>

</details>

<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h5 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:0.92em">Step 3 — `+grouping` pure helpers</h5></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

Run 2026-09-20, **MATLAB R2024b**. Baseline: HEAD (`34584357a`), working tree confirmed clean with `git status` before the first edit, so `git show HEAD:<path>` supplied the `old` copy of all 12 files. Scope: exactly 12 `.m` files in `+grouping/`; nothing outside them was touched. The directory listing was re-checked on disk first — the tranche table says "~11 files", but all 12 named files are present, including `mk_texs.m`.

**`check_stage1` over all 12 files — verbatim:**

```
PASS  +grouping/check_tlst.m
PASS  +grouping/check_xy.m
PASS  +grouping/effective_distance.m
PASS  +grouping/find_bin.m
PASS  +grouping/find_tex_regions.m
PASS  +grouping/find_xy.m
PASS  +grouping/mk_bindex.m
PASS  +grouping/mk_decc.m
PASS  +grouping/mk_dist.m
PASS  +grouping/mk_masks.m
PASS  +grouping/mk_mecc.m
PASS  +grouping/mk_texs.m

check_stage1: 12 / 12 pass, 0 fail
```

Run with a **per-file rename map**, following tranche 2's precedent: the maps are not jointly a bijection (`i` is an old name in nearly every file but maps to a different new name in each, and `np` is an old name in three files while `n_patches` is a *new* name in two others), so one combined map would fail `assert_rename_bijection`.

**Package-qualified call sites — no diff-reading workaround was needed, and here is why.** `check_stage1.m:30`'s lookbehind only matters when a *renamed* name appears after a `.`. The only package-qualified call inside this tranche's files is `grouping.check_tlst(...)` at `mk_masks.m:95`, and `check_tlst` was **not** renamed by this tranche — its arguments were, and those are bare identifiers inside the call's parentheses, which the lookbehind does not block. So all 12 files pass on the unmodified checker. The renamed arguments at that one call site (`side_order(i_side), x, y, map, grid_size`) were also read in the diff and confirmed to be positional substitutions only, with the argument order unchanged. The three out-of-scope callers (`mk_texseg_session.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_points.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m`) call every one of these functions positionally, so **no call site anywhere needed editing** — this tranche renamed nothing but file-local identifiers.

**Golden-harness replay: passes unchanged.** `golden_harness('replay', 'tools/golden_ref.mat')` returns without throwing (`REPLAY PASSED`), covering `mk_dist`, `mk_mecc`, `mk_decc`, `mk_bindex`, `mk_masks`, `find_bin`, `check_xy` and `check_tlst` — eight of this tranche's twelve files, more coverage than any tranche so far. `mk_texs`, `find_xy`, `effective_distance` and `find_tex_regions` are not covered (the first two consume RNG with no fixed reference; the last two are scripts). The reference was **not** recaptured.

**`checkcode`: 1 message before, 1 after — identical.** `find_bin.m` alone, `NASGU` on the dead `err = 1` that is part of B3.2; every other file in the tranche is clean, before and after. No line in any of the 12 files exceeds 100 characters, so there are no line-length exceptions to document; no tabs, no trailing whitespace.

**Decisions made here that bind cluster C (tranches 4 and 5).** Header shape, `See also` casing, four-space bodies, the tight-vs-spaced subscript rule and the "never a bare `patch`" rule all carry over unchanged from tranche 2's decisions 1, 6 and 7 — only cluster C's own vocabulary is settled below.

1. **C-1. `grid_size` is the patch-grid side length; `n_patches` is the total patch count.** This **amends section 3.1.1**, which proposed `sz`→`grid_size` *and* `np`→`n_patches` while glossing both as the per-side count. They are the same quantity — `mk_texseg_session.m:34` passes its `sz` into `mk_masks`'s `np` — so calling them different things was the whole problem. `grid_size` therefore takes over every per-side use of both `sz` and `np`, and `n_patches` is freed for `grid_size^2` (`npt` in `find_tex_regions.m`, `s_gtr_img.m`, `tex_regions.m`).
2. **C-2. `np` is not one thing repo-wide, so it must be expanded per file, not swept.** Three distinct meanings were found: the grid side length (`mk_masks.m`, `check_tlst.m`, `find_tex_regions.m`, and in tranche 4 `tex_regions.m`, `s_gtr_img.m`) → `grid_size`; the number of patch *pairs* in a geometry bin (`mk_texseg_session.m:95`, `mk_texseg_stim_points.m:94`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m:87`, each `np = bintbl(1,bin)` followed by `randi(np)`) → `n_pairs`; and the number of sub-patches per patch side (`rs_new.m:1,7,15`) → `n_subpatches`. Tranches 4 and 5 must check each occurrence rather than apply one map.
3. **C-3. The eccentricity words are spelled out everywhere, including inside the bin-bounds names.** `mecc`→`min_ecc`, `decc`→`delta_ecc`, and therefore `bbmecc`→`bin_bounds_min_ecc`, `bbdecc`→`bin_bounds_delta_ecc`, `bbdist`→`bin_bounds_dist`. This **amends section 3.1.1**, which proposed keeping the abbreviation inside the bin-bounds names (`bin_bounds_mecc`); half-expanding one vocabulary word reads worse than either extreme. Also `bindex`→`bin_index`, `bintbl`→`bin_table`, and a per-quantity bin number is `i_dist`/`i_min_ecc`/`i_delta_ecc`, matching the array dimension it indexes.
4. **C-4. A patch's linear number is `patch_index1`/`patch_index2`, never `patch1`/`patch2`.** Cluster C numbers patch `(x, y)` as `(x-1)*grid_size + y` and uses that number to index both dimensions of the pair matrices. Tranche 2's decision 4 already gave `patch1`/`patch2` to cluster B, where they hold actual image arrays; reusing them here for integers would collide across clusters. `patch_index1`/`patch_index2` is used in `mk_dist`, `mk_mecc`, `mk_decc` and `find_bin` (the old `i0`/`j0`).
5. **C-5. Flags get a semantic predicate name, not a `*_flag` suffix.** Section 3.1.1's `flg`-family entry is read as "make these readable", not "append `_flag`": `flg`→`is_boundary` (`check_xy`, `find_xy`), `chkflg`→`is_free` (`check_tlst`, `mk_masks`), `sflg`→`seed_repeat`, `xyflg`→`in_radius`. Use a bare `flag`/`check_flag` only where the surrounding code gives no better word — none of this tranche's flags did. Tranche 4's `dflg`/`sflg` and tranche 5's flags should be read the same way.
6. **C-6. When one old identifier serves several distinct roles in a file, it gets a single neutral non-shadowing name, not a descriptive one.** `check_stage1`'s rename map must be a per-file bijection (`assert_rename_bijection.m:20` rejects a duplicated old name), so Stage 1 physically cannot map one old name to two new ones — and a descriptive name that is right in one role is actively wrong in the others. Three files hit this: `mk_masks.m`'s `j` (region index twice, grid x-coordinate once) and `k` (patch-try index, then grid y-coordinate) became `jj`/`kk`; `mk_texs.m`'s `i`/`j` became `ii`/`jj`; `find_tex_regions.m`'s `j`/`i` became `index1`/`index2`. Every other counter in the tranche has one role and got a real name (`i_region`, `i_side`, `i_bin`, `i_dist`, `i_patch`, `i_pair`, `n_in_region`, `trial`). Splitting the neutral ones is **S3.5**. Note the related trap: a name already live in the file cannot appear on the right of the map either (`x`/`y` are live in `mk_masks.m`, so no counter could be renamed *to* `x`), since `assert_rename_bijection.m:22` requires disjoint domain and range.
7. **C-7. The rest of the settled cluster-C vocabulary**, all confirmed against each function's actual signature rather than taken from the proposal list: `trl`/`ntrl`→`trial`/`n_trials`; `ntr`→`n_regions` (texture regions per image) and `ntexr`→`n_tex_regions` where the file also carries `ntex`→`n_tex`; `tlst`→**`patch_list`**, not section 3.1.1's `tile_list` — the array holds patch coordinates with a running count in column 1, and cluster C calls these things patches everywhere else; `msks`→`masks`; `dprm`→`side_order` where it is the `randperm(4)` ordering of a patch's four sides, and `side` for the single side `check_tlst` receives (so `dprm` is "direction permutation", now settled); `iprm`→`region_order`; `nprm`→`patch_order`; `rnum`→`region`; `xout`/`yout`→`x_out`/`y_out`; `pw`→`patch_width`, `imw`→`image_width` (both pixels, as section 3.1.1 already has them).
8. **C-8. A function's `mk_*` name and its output variable are allowed to disagree.** `mk_mecc` returns `min_ecc`, `mk_decc` returns `delta_ecc`, `mk_bindex` returns `bin_index`. Step 1 decision 4 settled that the `mk_*` filenames stay; expanding the output variable anyway is the same deliberate, temporary mismatch step 1 decision 5 created for `re.m`/`rs.m`. Renaming the files to match is not queued — it would be a wide, low-benefit tranche, and step 1 already closed that question.
9. **`check_tlst`'s missing terminating `end` was supplied.** The file's last `end` closed its `if`/`elseif` chain; the function itself had none, which MATLAB accepts. Per tranche 2's decision 6 every file now ends in `end`, and this is invisible to `canon` because `tree2str` supplies a missing `end` anyway — confirmed by the file's `PASS` above.
10. **Scripts get the exemplar header too, minus the parts that do not apply.** `effective_distance.m` and `find_tex_regions.m` have no `function` line, so their headers carry the summary, a `grouping.<name>` invocation line, the prose, `Inputs` (stated as *none*, with the hardcoded constants named) and `Output`, plus `See also`. A script is not exempt from documenting its units.

**Units, as written into the headers.** `grid_size` is an integer count of patches, stated in every header that takes it, with "not pixels, not degrees" spelled out. `dist`, `min_ecc` and `delta_ecc` are all **in patches** as well, and this is now confirmed from the code rather than assumed: `mk_dist` takes `sqrt((x1-x2)^2 + (y1-y2)^2)` straight from grid indices, and `mk_texseg_session.m:23-25`'s bin bounds (`[1,2,4,8,16,32]`, `[0,1,2,4,8,12]`, `[0,2,4,6,8,12]`) bracket exactly the ranges a 16-patch grid produces. `patch_width` and `image_width` are pixels. `seed_radius_frac` and `fill_fraction` are dimensionless fractions.

**Two argument names whose documented meaning was wrong, corrected in the header only.** `mk_masks`'s `pixp` was commented "proportion of pixels taken up by texture regions", but the loop it controls is `sum(patch_list(:,1)) < fill_fraction*grid_size^2`, and `patch_list(:,1)` counts *patches*, not pixels — hence `fill_fraction`, documented as a fraction of the grid's patches. `find_tex_regions.m:6` labelled the patch count "(pixels)", copied from the line above it. Neither is a behaviour change; both were silently misleading.

**Commented-out code.** Four blocks, all in `mk_masks.m`, all triaged in section 3.1.4: two `figure`/`image`/`axis` stanzas (**scaffolding**), the `while min(min(map)) == 0` stop condition (**superseded** by the `fill_fraction` line below it), and `lmsks = cast(msks,'logical')` (**unfinished** — the logical output the header promises). The survey had located only the two figure blocks, so this tranche adds two rows it missed. **The other eleven files carry no commented-out code at all** — a documented negative, checked file by file while reading. Nothing was deleted or converted. Per tranche 2's decision 8, `ntr`→`n_regions` was propagated into the two figure stanzas (live variable, same scope, would still run), while the `lmsks` line was left verbatim because it names `msks`, which does not exist in the function.

**Bugs found — two new, neither fixed.** 🟡 **B3.9** (`effective_distance.m` cannot run: undefined `a` and `d`, a two-argument `abs` call, and an inner test and assignment that name different pairs) and 🟡 **B3.10** (`mk_mecc`/`mk_decc` measure eccentricity from `grid_size/2` rather than `(grid_size+1)/2`, giving an eccentricity field asymmetric by a whole patch — filed with explicit low confidence, since a lattice-point center may be deliberate).

**The two bugs already on record were re-read, and both descriptions hold — with one refinement each.** **B3.2**: confirmed in place, untouched, and it is still the file's only lint message. Refinement: the guard is dead in a second way as well — it tests `id == 0`, so it cannot catch the `ime`/`ide` failures at all, which throw *before* it is reached whenever `min_ecc` or `delta_ecc` falls outside its bounds. **B3.3**: confirmed, and the function's own header supplies the evidence — its original comment line read `nmsks = number of masks = ntr*ntrl`, i.e. the author wrote down the correct third dimension and then preallocated a different one. The new header states the intended size and points at B3.3 rather than fixing it.

**Optimizations — nothing new beyond one addition to an existing item.** O3.1 (`mk_dist`/`mk_decc`/`mk_mecc`/`mk_bindex` vectorization) and O3.2 (`find_bin`'s three linear scans) were both re-confirmed by reading the actual code and need no change to their write-ups; `mk_bindex` really is `reshape(1:n, ...)` with a permute, and `find_bin`'s three loops really do keep scanning after a match. `mk_texs`'s two double loops were appended to **O3.3**'s enumerated list. S2.6's premise was re-confirmed too: the three call sites each rebuild the same four matrices for the same `sz`.

**Structural items — four new.** 🔴 **S1.2** (`find_xy` inlines `check_xy`'s eight-neighbour test verbatim — the single clearest duplication in this cluster), 🟡 **S3.3** (the fate of the two callerless scripts, which S2.5's list missed), 🟡 **S3.4** (`check_tlst` is misnamed — it never touches a patch list), 🟡 **S3.5** (splitting the multi-role counters rule C-6 could not split).

**`Contents.m` — deliberately deferred to tranche 4, and 0.1.3's checklist item is left unticked for it.** `+grouping` holds 21 files; this tranche cleaned 12. A `Contents.m` listing only 12 of them is worse than none, because `help grouping` presents whatever is in that file as the package's complete contents, so a partial one would silently misrepresent the package for the length of one tranche. The cost of deferring is nil: every one of these 12 files now carries the one-line summary its `Contents.m` entry needs, so tranche 4 can lift all 12 verbatim and add its own 9 — the same "one source, no drift" discipline `+lib/Contents.m` follows.

**Deliberately left alone.** `check_tlst`'s four `else chkflg = 0;` branches, which re-assign a value the function already initialized — removing them is a real AST change, not Stage 1. `find_tex_regions.m`'s unused `image_width` and `effective_distance.m`'s unused `dist_max` — `checkcode` does not flag either, and deleting an assignment is Stage 2. `mk_masks`'s `patch_list` row width, `round(3*grid_size^2/n_regions) + 1`, which caps a region at about 1.5 times its fair share before MATLAB silently grows the array: not a bug (growth is correct, just unpreallocated) and too marginal to be worth an item, noted here so the next reader knows it was seen. Every `save`/`load` string literal and zone-3 frozen name: this tranche's files contain none, a documented negative.

**Baseline note for later tranches.** The working tree is clean again after this step's commit; HEAD remains a valid `check_stage1` baseline, and no `+grouping` path changed in this step.

</div>

</details>

<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h5 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:0.92em">Step 4 — `+grouping` stimulus scripts</h5></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

Run 2026-09-20/21, **MATLAB R2024b**. Baseline: HEAD, working tree confirmed clean with `git status` before the first edit, so `git show HEAD:<path>` supplied the `old` copy of every file. Scope: the 8 files section 0.1.4 named (`mk_texseg_session.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_points.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m`, `mk_trl_points.m`, `tex_regions.m`, `s_gtr_img.m`, `demo_mk_trl_points.m`), plus one file the tranche's own file list missed: **`+grouping/shape_cue.m`**. The directory listing was re-checked on disk first, per this tranche's standing instruction not to trust the file list blindly — `+grouping` holds 21 `.m` files total (matching section 2's count), and 12 (tranche 3) + the 8 named here = 20, one short. `shape_cue.m` is called by three of this tranche's own files (`mk_texseg_stim_points.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m`) and was in neither tranche 3's nor this tranche's dispatch list — folded into this tranche's scope rather than left for a later one, since three of this tranche's own files already depend on its vocabulary and it is the only remaining uncleaned file in the package.

**`check_stage1` over all 9 files — verbatim:**

```
true s_gtr_img.m
true mk_trl_points.m
true demo_mk_trl_points.m
true mk_texseg_session.m
true mk_texseg_stim_points.m
true mk_texseg_stim_shape.m
true mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m
true tex_regions.m
true shape_cue.m

check_stage1: 9 / 9 pass, 0 fail
```

Run with a **per-file rename map**, following tranches 2 and 3's precedent (`assert_rename_bijection` on a single repo-wide map would fail: e.g. `condition` is a *new* name in most of these files but a pre-existing live name in `mk_texseg_stim_points.m` and `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m`, so `cond` had to stay `cond` in those two files specifically rather than expand — see decision 3 below).

**Package-qualified call sites — only one file has any, and it needed no workaround.** `mk_texseg_session.m` is the only file in this tranche that calls its helpers package-qualified (`grouping.mk_texs(...)`, `grouping.mk_masks(...)`, `grouping.mk_dist(...)`, `grouping.mk_mecc(...)`, `grouping.mk_decc(...)`, `grouping.mk_bindex(...)`, `grouping.find_bin(...)` ×2, `grouping.find_xy(...)` ×2, `grouping.check_xy(...)` ×2) — none of those function names were renamed by this tranche, and every renamed identifier in this file is a local variable or a positional argument inside parentheses, never immediately preceded by a `.`, so `check_stage1.m:30`'s lookbehind never had reason to fire. Confirmed by reading the diff: every rename site in `mk_texseg_session.m` is inside an argument list, not a callee name. The other 8 files in this tranche have **zero** `grouping.`-qualified calls anywhere — they call `mk_texs`, `mk_masks`, `shape_cue`, `mk_dist`, `mk_mecc`, `mk_decc`, `mk_bindex`, `find_bin`, `find_xy`, `check_xy` and `check_tlst` unqualified, which is itself a new bug (B2.6, below), not a Stage 1 finding to correct.

**Golden-harness replay: passes unchanged, and covers none of this tranche's files.** `golden_harness('replay', 'tools/golden_ref.mat')` returns clean. None of the harness's 15 entry points call into any of this tranche's 9 files — a documented negative, consistent with section 1's coverage note: none of these files are pure/self-contained enough for a synthetic-input entry point (`mk_texseg_session.m` draws real Brodatz-adjacent randomness and returns a large struct; the rest are scripts with no callable signature, several already broken as written per B2.5/B2.6/B2.7). The replay's only job here is confirming this tranche did not disturb anything the harness *does* cover, which it didn't.

**`checkcode`: 5 messages total, unchanged in kind from the survey.** `mk_texseg_session.m` 1 (`NASGU` on `n_pixels`, a pre-existing dead assignment - the script never reads it after computing it), `s_gtr_img.m` 4 (`STOUT` on the unassigned `simg` output, two `INUSD` on unused inputs, one `NASGU` - all four are B3.11, below), all other 7 files clean.

**Line-length exceptions — 2 lines over 100 characters, both inside a commented-out block** (`mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m:211-212`, the leftover point-cue lines triaged in section 3.1.4). Two live lines that would otherwise have crossed 100 (`mk_texseg_stim_shape.m`'s and `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m`'s `else` branch of the shape-cue call) were wrapped with `...` instead of being left long. No other line in the tranche's 9 files exceeds 100.

**Decisions made here that bind nothing further** (this tranche does not set new cluster-C vocabulary; it applies tranche 3's):

1. **`grid_size`/`n_patches`, the eccentricity spellings, `patch_index1`/`patch_index2`, `patch_list`, the `*_dv`-style predicate flags, and the `mk_*` filename-vs-output mismatch all applied exactly as tranche 3 settled them (C-1 through C-8).** Confirmed against each callee's actual current signature by reading `mk_dist.m`, `mk_mecc.m`, `mk_decc.m`, `mk_bindex.m`, `find_bin.m`, `mk_masks.m`, `check_tlst.m`, `check_xy.m`, `find_xy.m`, `mk_texs.m` before writing any caller.
2. **New vocabulary this tranche had to settle, not present in tranche 3's files:** `tex_nums` (mk_texs's actual output name, used identically by every caller), `cue_locations` (the `cuelocs` field/argument, expanded per section 3.1.1's original proposal — no tranche 3 file used it), `cue_img`/`tex_img`/`feedback_img`/`patch_img`/`img_in` (section 3.1.1's original `cimg`/`timg`/`fimg`/`pimg`/`imgin` expansions, applied for the first time here), `mean_lum` (the `m0` argument/field), and `n_pairs` for the bin-table "how many pairs are in this bin" count (`np = bin_table(1,bin)`), matching C-2's rule that `np` is expanded per its actual meaning rather than swept.
3. **C-6's neutral-name rule (one old identifier, several roles, one new name) came up in every multi-loop script in this tranche, not just `mk_masks.m` and `mk_texs.m`.** `mk_texseg_stim_points.m` and `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m` each reuse bare `i`/`j` for both "linear patch index" (the bin-table build/retrieval loops) and "region index"/"grid row" (the per-trial mask and texture loops); both became `ii`/`jj`/`kk` throughout, with an inline comment at first use pointing at C-6. `tex_regions.m`'s `i` (region index and permutation-position counter) and `j` (permutation target, grid row, *and* a seed-duplicate-check counter) both needed the same treatment. `shape_cue.m`'s `i`/`j` swap row/column role between its four bounding-box searches and became `ii`/`jj` for the same reason.
4. **`cond` could not become `condition` everywhere, even though section 3.1.1's abbreviation table and this tranche's own early drafts assumed it could.** `mk_texseg_stim_points.m:163` and the equivalent line in `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m` already contain the *literal word* `condition` in live code (a pre-existing, unrelated naming drift from the same file's own `cond` variable used a few lines above) — expanding `cond`→`condition` in those two files would have collided with that live name, exactly the "domain must not intersect a name already live in the file" trap `assert_rename_bijection` exists to catch. `cond` was therefore left as `cond` in those two files specifically; every other file in the tranche (which has no such pre-existing `condition`) expands it normally. Caught by `check_stage1` failing on the first attempt, not by inspection - a concrete instance of why the checker, not a global find-and-replace, is the source of truth for a rename map.
5. **`msks`/`masks` cannot always both become `masks`.** In `mk_texseg_stim_points.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m` and `tex_regions.m`, the per-trial pixel-level mask array (old `msks`) became `trial_masks`, not `masks`, in every file where a session-level `masks` (from `[masks, maps] = mk_masks(...)`) is *also* live in the same script scope - reusing `masks` would collide with that pre-existing name the same way `cond`/`condition` did. `mk_trl_points.m` has no such pre-existing `masks` (it never calls `mk_masks` itself), so its per-trial array is named `masks` directly, matching the settled vocabulary without a workaround.
6. **`shape_cue.m` folded in, not deferred** — see this step's opening note. Cleaned to the same header/formatting/vocabulary standard as the rest of the tranche; its `cue`/`cue_map` outputs match the names `mk_texseg_stim_shape.m`/`mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m` already use at the call site.

**Header-doc corrections made along the way.** `mk_texseg_session.m`'s original header comment named itself `mk_texseg_session_points.m` - a stale copy-pasted filename, replaced with a real summary. `mk_texseg_stim_shape.m` and `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m` both opened `% mk_texseg_shape.m` / `% mk_texseg_stim_shape.m` respectively - neither matches its own filename; both replaced. `s_gtr_img.m`'s and `tex_regions.m`'s headers previously described arguments as pixel counts that are patch counts (`np`/`ntr`), corrected in the new headers using the units glossary from section 3.1.1/tranche 3.

**Commented-out code.** 11 blocks triaged, all newly found or newly split out from the survey's 3 approximate entries - see section 3.1.4. Verdicts: 5 scaffolding, 4 unfinished, 2 alternative, 1 unknown (the `tex_regions.m:150` dangling `s_pimg` reference). Nothing was deleted or converted. Per tranche 2's rule 8, renames were propagated into the two commented display-loop blocks (their variables - `i_trial`, `cue_locations`, `n_tex_regions` - are all still live in the enclosing scope, so the blocks would still run if uncommented) and into `mk_texseg_stim_points.m`'s trailing save comment only where the same name is used elsewhere in the *live* struct-building code (it wasn't - see the frozen-names note below); the zone-3 frozen `save`/`load` argument lists in `mk_texseg_session.m`'s and `mk_texseg_stim_points.m`'s trailing comments were left completely verbatim, per section 3.1.1's existing note that these are frozen if ever revived.

**A frozen-name decision this tranche had to make that section 3.1.1 didn't anticipate: session struct field names.** `mk_texseg_session.m` returns `session.cntrst`, `session.cuelocs`, `session.m0`, `session.maps`, `session.pw`, `session.sz`, `session.texs`, `session.tex_set`, `session.tperm` - none of these are `save`/`load` string literals (section 3.1.1's zone 3 only lists those), but they are read by name, dot-qualified, from `+experiment/+grouping/+prep/setup_experiment.m:38-58` (tranche 7's scope, not yet cleaned) and from this tranche's own `demo_mk_trl_points.m`. Confirmed by grep across the whole repo. Since a struct field is dot-qualified the same way a config field is, and `check_stage1`'s regex cannot see it either way, these field names were treated the same as section 3.1.1's zone-3 names for this tranche: **the local variable that feeds each field was renamed freely (e.g. `mean_lum` feeds `session.m0 = mean_lum;`), but the field name itself was left exactly as it was** (`cntrst`, `cuelocs`, `m0`, `pw`, `sz`, `texs`, `tex_set`, `tperm`). This is the same B-1 pattern tranche 2 used for `inputParser` parameter strings. Renaming these fields properly (updating every reader at once) is Stage 3 territory, not recorded here as a new structural item since it would just be S2.7's `inputParser` treatment applied to a struct instead of a parser - noted here so Stage 3 knows to look for it.

**Bugs found — four new, none fixed.** 🟠 **B2.5** (`demo_mk_trl_points.m` calls `mk_texseg_session()` with no arguments, and separately reads a `session.texset` field that does not exist - `session.tex_set` does), 🟠 **B2.6** (four of this tranche's scripts call their helpers unqualified, and none of those names resolve - confirmed empirically with `which`, see the package-qualified-calls note above), 🟠 **B2.7** (`mk_texseg_stim_points.m`'s contrast-block loop is missing `i_group`'s offset in its middle bound, so every block past the first is empty), 🟡 **B3.11** (`s_gtr_img.m` never assigns its declared output `simg`; callerless). S2.5, S2.6 and O2.2's line-number references were re-verified against this tranche's edited files and updated in section 0.3/0.5 (headers and renames shifted every line number; the underlying findings are unchanged). O3.1/O3.2/O3.3's premises were re-confirmed by reading `mk_texseg_stim_points.m`'s and `tex_regions.m`'s loops again - no change to their write-ups.

**Deliberately left alone.** `mk_texseg_stim_points.m` computes the same `image_width` value twice under two different names (`img8b_width` early, for sizing `img_stack`, and `image_width` later, for the render loop) - a pre-existing redundancy, not a bug (both compute the same correct value) and too marginal for a Stage 5 item on its own; noted here so the next reader knows it was seen. `check_tlst`'s and `find_tex_regions.m`'s pre-existing "deliberately left alone" items from tranche 3 are unaffected by this tranche. Every `save`/`load` string literal and the session-struct field names (see above): confirmed present but frozen, not a documented-negative this time since they are genuinely there.

**Structural items — none new.** S2.5's and S2.6's premises were both re-confirmed by reading every file in this tranche; S3.1 (whether `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m` should be deleted) gained direct support from this tranche's header work - the file's own new header now states plainly that it has no caller and no recorded reason for existing alongside its two siblings.

**`+grouping/Contents.m` created**, covering all 21 files: the 12 tranche 3 lifted its summaries from verbatim, plus this tranche's 9 (including `shape_cue.m`). Grouped into four sections (geometry/bin helpers, region growing and patch tests, session/trial stimulus scripts, unfinished callerless scripts), each entry the file's own header summary, flagged inline with a bug id where a file is broken as written (B2.6/B2.7 for the stimulus scripts, B3.11 for `s_gtr_img.m`) so `help grouping` does not silently claim a broken script works.

**Baseline note for later tranches.** The working tree is clean again after this step's commit; HEAD remains a valid `check_stage1` baseline, and no `+grouping` path changed in this step beyond the 9 files above and the new `Contents.m`.

<summary><h5 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:0.92em">Step 5 — root cluster-C files</h5></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

Run 2026-09-21, **MATLAB R2024b**. Baseline: HEAD, working tree confirmed clean with `git status` before the first edit, so `git show HEAD:<path>` supplied the `old` copy of every file. Scope: exactly the 10 files section 0.1.5 named (`re.m`, `rs.m`, `rs_new.m`, `rp.m`, `thresh.m`, `nlsame.m`, `mk_contour.m`, `mk_win.m`, `edge_dv.m`, `contour_blur_estimation.m`). The root directory listing was re-checked on disk first, per the standing instruction not to trust the file list blindly — it holds exactly these 10 files plus `config.m`, `setup.m`, `texture_grouping.m` (tranche 9, out of scope here).

**`check_stage1` over all 10 files, per-file rename maps — verbatim:**

```
true  re.m
true  rs.m
true  rs_new.m
true  rp.m
true  thresh.m
true  nlsame.m
true  mk_contour.m
true  mk_win.m
true  edge_dv.m
true  contour_blur_estimation.m

check_stage1: 10 / 10 pass, 0 fail
```

Run with a **per-file rename map**, following tranches 2-4's precedent (`ii`/`jj`/`kk` are old names in some files and new names in others, so a single repo-wide map would fail `assert_rename_bijection`). `contour_blur_estimation.m` is a script, not a function, but `canon`/`check_stage1` handle it the same way tranche 3 handled `effective_distance.m`/`find_tex_regions.m`.

**Package-qualified call sites — one file has one, and it needed no workaround.** `edge_dv.m:37-38` calls `lib.edge_contour_props(...)` (the function B2.1 already flags as not existing anywhere in `+lib`). This tranche renamed nothing about that call — `edge_contour_props` isn't touched, and the arguments (`patch_a`, `patch_b`) were already correctly named before this tranche — so `check_stage1.m:30`'s lookbehind never had reason to fire, and `edge_dv.m` passes on the unmodified checker. Confirmed by reading the diff. No other file in this tranche calls anything package-qualified; `mk_contour` is called unqualified from `re.m` (both root-level files, no package involved), and `rp` is called unqualified from `rs_new.m` for the same reason.

**Golden-harness replay: passes, and covers 4 of this tranche's 10 files.** `golden_harness('replay', 'tools/golden_ref.mat')` returns without throwing. Per section 1's coverage note, `mk_win` (`mk_win_radial_checksum`, `mk_win_separable_checksum`), `mk_contour` (`mk_contour_checksum`, `mk_contour_lnks_checksum`, `mk_contour_n_con`), `thresh` (`thresh_checksum`) and `nlsame` (`nlsame_value`) are covered with small synthetic inputs and all match the captured reference unchanged. `re.m`, `rs.m`, `rs_new.m`, `rp.m`, `edge_dv.m` and `contour_blur_estimation.m` are **not** covered — a documented negative, consistent with section 1's note that these six need real image data (or, for `edge_dv.m`, a `+lib` function that doesn't exist) and are pattern-check-only for Stage 2. The reference was **not** recaptured.

**`checkcode`: 4 messages total, identical in kind and count to the pre-edit baseline.** Measured both ways: on the HEAD copies (backed up before editing) and on the edited files. `re.m` 2 (`ASGLU` on `links`, the second output of `mk_contour` that `re.m` never reads — pre-existing, at the renamed lines 89/126, was 67/104), `rs.m` 1 (`NASGU` on the final `p1s = circshift(...)` reassignment, pre-existing, at renamed line 38, was 25), `edge_dv.m` 1 (`NASGU` on `edge_dens_dv_diff`, pre-existing, at line 46, was 11), all other 7 files clean before and after. The apparent "before" totals from a naive `checkcode` run on the backup copies are inflated by an `FNDEF` artifact of the backup filenames themselves (`re_old.m` etc. disagreeing with the `function re(...)` line inside) — that message is not a real pre-existing lint hit and is excluded from the count above; it is a byproduct of the backup-naming scheme, not of anything in the repo.

**Line-length exceptions — 2 lines over 100 characters, both inside `edge_dv.m`'s disabled normal-distribution block** (`edge_dv.m:53,56`, part of the block triaged as *alternative* in section 3.1.4). Reflowing them would mean rewriting dead code, which step 2c forbids until its Stage 3 verdict lands. Every other line that crossed 100 after renaming (`re.m`'s two `mk_contour` calls and two orientation-difference sums, `edge_dv.m`'s output signature and its live `edge_length_dv` formula) was either wrapped with `...` or had its trailing comment shortened; none of those are exceptions since they're live code, not string literals.

**Decisions made here that close out cluster C's vocabulary (no further tranches use it).**

1. **The `*_dv`-family output rule (tranche 2's decision 5) applied exactly as anticipated.** `re.m`/`rs.m`/`rs_new.m`/`rp.m`'s `Reout`/`Rsout`/`Rsout`/`Rpout` all became `dv`, removing the repo's last TitleCase leftovers. `rs.m` and `rs_new.m` use `dv` for both the loop's running accumulator and the final result (the same dual role `Rsout` played before the rename), matching their pre-existing structure exactly.
2. **`sz`/`np` had to be read per-file again, per C-2, and neither was `grid_size`/`n_patches` here.** `thresh.m`'s `sz` is *not* a patch-grid side length — reading its caller (`texture_grouping.m:95`, `thresh(dv, nij, critl)`, where `nij = ni^2` is the *total* patch count) shows it is the side length of a patch-*pair* response matrix, i.e. `n_patches`. This **amends the tranche's own first draft**, which had proposed `grid_size` by pattern-matching the abbreviation table rather than checking the call site — a concrete instance of C-2's rule that `np`/`sz` must be verified against actual usage, not swept. `mk_win.m`'s `sz` (confirmed via `texture_grouping.m:44`, `mk_win(psz, r, shp)`) is a *pixel* size matching `psz`/`patch_size` elsewhere in this cluster, not a patch-grid count either — renamed to `patch_size` for that reason, distinct from `thresh.m`'s `n_patches`.
3. **The `mk_contour(npx,y,x)` builtin shadow (section 2's `contour` shadow at `Re.m:67,104`) is fixed on both ends.** `mk_contour.m`'s own output `contour`→`contour_pts`, and separately `re.m`'s local variable of the same name (from `[contour,lnks,ncon] = mk_contour(...)`) also →`contour_pts` — two independent, unrelated renames (function return-variable names are internal documentation only; MATLAB dispatches outputs positionally), both landing on the same new name by coincidence of shared vocabulary, not because one drove the other.
4. **`i`/`j` shadowing the imaginary unit, fixed in every file that had it (`re.m`, `mk_win.m`, `mk_contour.m`, `thresh.m`, `nlsame.m`, `contour_blur_estimation.m`).** Applying C-6: `re.m`'s `i` served two genuinely different roles (patch row index in three double loops; contour-vertex index in the orientation-difference loops) and became the neutral `ii`, with `j` (always patch column index, a single role) becoming `jj` for pairing consistency in the same nested loops, and `k` (region/contour index, single role, but chosen neutral to avoid tempting a "fix" near B1.2's bug line) becoming `kk`. `mk_win.m`'s `i`/`j` are single-role throughout (always row/column of the window) and got descriptive `i_row`/`i_col`. `mk_contour.m`'s `i` has one consistent role — "a pixel index" — across both the adjacency-building loop and the start-pixel scan, so it took the descriptive `i_point`, with `j`→`j_point` for the paired role ("a candidate connected pixel index"). `thresh.m`'s and `contour_blur_estimation.m`'s `i`/`j` are single-role row/column indices, →`i_row`/`i_col`. `nlsame.m`'s `i`/`j` are single-role indices into `links_a`/`links_b` respectively, →`i_a`/`i_b`.
5. **`re.m`'s `thresh` parameter became `threshold`, distinct from the sibling file `thresh.m`.** The two are unrelated (a scalar gradient-magnitude cutoff vs. a whole response-matrix thresholding function) and live in different files, so there is no rename-map conflict — but keeping the parameter named `thresh` would have read as a reference to the other file. `rs_new.m`'s local `thresh` (a different, always-zero constant local to that file) got the same treatment for the same reason.
6. **`rp.m`'s `b` and `rs_new.m`'s `b` both became `noise_const`**, matching `+lib/power_dv.m`'s already-settled name for the same "noise suppression constant added to a unit-mean-normalized power spectrum" role (tranche 2, decision 10) — confirmed by reading `rp.m` and `power_dv.m` side by side: they compute the identical measure, `rp.m` just windows each patch first. This is also what licenses `rp.m`'s `See also LIB.POWER_DV` and vice versa.
7. **Header cross-references were checked against actual shared computation before being written, not assumed from filename similarity.** `re.m`/`rs.m`/`rs_new.m`/`rp.m` cross-reference each other (`See also`) because all four are called the same way in `texture_grouping.m` (two patches in, one scalar `dv` out) and `rs_new.m` literally calls `rp` internally. `rp.m` additionally cross-references `LIB.POWER_DV` for the reason in point 6. `re.m`/`mk_contour.m` cross-reference each other since `re.m` is `mk_contour`'s only caller anywhere in the repo. `thresh.m`/`nlsame.m` cross-reference each other since both are called together, back to back, from `texture_grouping.m`'s link-finding step. `edge_dv.m` cross-references `LIB.HIST_DV`/`LIB.POWER_DV` as the same general family of two-patches-in/decision-variables-out functions, **not** because it shares their actual math (it doesn't — it's contour-based, like `re.m`, and already cross-references nothing to `re.m` since the two use unrelated per-contour statistics beyond the shared "contour" vocabulary). `contour_blur_estimation.m` cross-references `MK_WIN` only because both live in this same small cluster and touch circular geometry — noted here as the weakest of this tranche's `See also` links, kept because nothing stronger exists in scope.
8. **A refinement to B1.2, found while confirming no other caller could mask or trigger it differently.** Grepping the whole repo for `re(`, `rs(` and `rs_new(` as call expressions (not just the `function` line) finds **zero in-repo callers for any of the three** — `texture_grouping.m` calls `rp`, `thresh`, `mk_win` and `nlsame` directly but never `re`, `rs` or `rs_new`. B1.2's description ("on a live decision-variable function actually used in analysis") most likely refers to use from outside this repository (an interactive analysis script the user runs directly, not committed here) rather than a call site this repo contains — noted so a later reader doesn't go looking for an in-repo call that doesn't exist. This doesn't change B1.2's tier or the instruction to leave the bug untouched; `for kk = 1:n_contours1` at what is now `re.m:127` (mirroring the "patch 1" block's identical loop at `re.m:88`) still reuses patch 1's contour count inside the "patch 2" block, exactly as before the rename — confirmed by re-reading the finished file end to end after all edits landed.

**Header-doc corrections made along the way.** `thresh.m` and `edge_dv.m` had no header at all (both named in section 2's list of 4 headerless files); both now have the full exemplar shape. `re.m`'s original header undercounted its own arguments' meaning (`thres` — a typo for `thresh` — and no unit for `ks`, which is actually a LoG *sigma* in pixels, not a "kernal filter size" as originally commented; confirmed against MATLAB's own `edge(I,'log',threshold,sigma)` signature). `mk_win.m`'s header gained real `Inputs`/`Output` labels; its `sz` argument's true units (pixels, matching `psz` at its one real call site) are stated explicitly rather than left implicit.

**Commented-out code.** 5 blocks/groups triaged (2 in `re.m`, 1 spanning `rs_new.m`, 2 in `edge_dv.m` — one of which is itself 5 separate one-line sketches counted together), all in section 3.1.4: 1 unfinished (`re.m`'s `eps2` section), 1 scaffolding (`re.m`'s trailing figure calls), 1 alternative (`rs_new.m`'s whole raw-correlation-sum variant), 1 alternative (`edge_dv.m`'s normal-distribution length formula), 1 unfinished (`edge_dv.m`'s five per-property mean-difference sketches). The survey's approximate `edge_dv.m:16-22` (6 lines) undercounted this file — the real content is 3 lines of normal-distribution formula plus 5 separate one-line sketches scattered through the rest of the file, found only by reading it closely as this tranche's instructions required. `rs.m`, `rp.m`, `thresh.m`, `nlsame.m`, `mk_contour.m`, `mk_win.m` and `contour_blur_estimation.m` carry **no commented-out code at all** — a documented negative, checked file by file while reading. Nothing was deleted or converted. Per tranche 2's rule 8: renames were propagated into `re.m`'s trailing figure-display block (all six referenced variables — `patch1`, `patch2`, `zero_cross1`, `grad_mag1`, `zero_cross2`, `grad_mag2` — are still live in the enclosing scope) and into `rs_new.m`'s final `% dv = s12/s11 + s21/s22;` line (`dv` is live; `s11`/`s12`/`s22`/`s21` are internal to the same self-consistent disabled unit, never live outside it, so nothing else needed propagating); renames were **not** propagated into `re.m`'s `eps2` block, which is left with every pre-tranche-5 identifier verbatim, because part of it (`eps1`) references a name that exists nowhere in the file, live or commented — the same "would not run even if fully uncommented" test tranche 3 applied to `mk_masks.m`'s `lmsks`/`msks`.

**Bugs found — two new, neither fixed.** 🟡 **B3.12** (`re.m`'s asymmetric `0*n_kernels` vs. plain `0` when a zero-crossing pixel fails threshold — harmless for any finite `n_kernels`, and `n_kernels` is otherwise entirely unused) and 🟡 **B3.13** (`rs_new.m` calls `rp` with 4 arguments where `rp.m` requires 5, so any call through `rs_new.m` would error immediately on `win`; latent since `rs_new.m` has no caller). B1.2 was re-read and confirmed unchanged in shape and effect (see decision 8 above); B2.1 (`edge_dv.m`'s dangling `lib.edge_contour_props` call) was also re-confirmed by reading, untouched.

**One suspected inconsistency checked and found to be nothing.** `rs.m:26`'s `dv = 1/dv;` (inverting the best-aligned similarity so smaller means more similar, opposite of every other function in this cluster) looked at first read like it might be a leftover sign error, but re-reading `texture_grouping.m` shows `rs`/`rs_new` are never actually called anywhere in this repo either (same finding as decision 8), so there is no live convention to be inconsistent with — left exactly as written, no header claim made about "larger means more different" the way `re.m`/`rp.m`/`rs_new.m`'s headers do, since that would be asserting something this tranche could not verify against a real caller.

**Optimizations — one new, tangled with existing items.** 🟡 addition to **O3.3**: `mk_contour.m:32-38`'s pairwise 8-connectivity test over all `n_pixels^2` pixel pairs is a small vectorizable candidate (`pdist2` plus a threshold), in the same shape as this item's other small loops; the traversal walk immediately below it is inherently sequential (each step depends on which link the previous step consumed) and is not a candidate. O3.3's `re.m` line numbers were updated to match this tranche's renamed/reformatted file (`:54-67,77-86,116-125`, up from `:32-45,55-64,92-101`); `thresh.m`/`nlsame.m`/`mk_win.m`'s line numbers were updated the same way. No other new optimization candidates found; none of this tranche's files contain a `parfor`, an `inv`/`pinv`/`det` call, unpreallocated growth, or repeated file I/O.

**Structural items — none new.** S2.3's premise (`rp.m` should repoint to `vislab.nat_stat_bayes.dv_power`, matching `+lib/power_dv.m`) was re-confirmed by reading `rp.m` and `power_dv.m` side by side (see decision 6); its cited caller line numbers (`texture_grouping.m:72`, `rs_new.m:49-50`) have shifted with this tranche's renames — `rs_new.m`'s two `rp` calls are now at `:65-66` — noted here so Stage 3 re-verifies before acting, per that item's own standing instruction.

**Deliberately left alone.** `re.m`'s `sd1`/`sd2`, `rs.m`/`rs_new.m`'s `p1`/`p2`/`p1k`/`p2k`/`p11`/`p12`/`p21`/`p22`(`c`) family, and `rp.m`'s `power1`/`power2` internals — all single-purpose, already-clear short names per section 3.1.1's "expand only what's actually unreadable" rule, left as originally spelled (`rp.m`'s were already expanded by tranche 2's `power_dv.m` naming and carried over unchanged where the two files' code is parallel). `rs_new.m`'s dead `xsz`/`pszc`/`a` (short algebraic names for cross-correlation crop bounds) were left un-expanded for the same reason. `contour_blur_estimation.m`'s hardcoded `inner_radius = 64` with `image_size = 64` (so the annulus condition `radius >= inner_radius` is only satisfied in the image's far corner, given the `+ inner_radius` offset baked into `x`) was checked by hand-computing a few `(i_row, i_col)` values — it does produce a non-empty, if corner-clipped, ring, not an always-empty image as an initial skim suggested; not a bug, and too marginal/uncertain (is the corner-clipping deliberate, to view a magnified arc of the ring? no way to tell from the code alone) to be worth an item — noted here so the next reader knows it was checked, not missed. Every `save`/`load` string literal and zone-3 frozen name: this tranche's files contain none, a documented negative.

**`Contents.m`.** Not applicable — these are root-level loose files, not a package (packages are `+lib`, `+grouping`, `+experiment`, `+general`), per this tranche's own scope note.

**Baseline note for later tranches.** The working tree is clean again after this step's commit; HEAD remains a valid `check_stage1` baseline, and no root-level path changed in this step (all 10 edits were in place, no renames).

<summary><h5 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:0.92em">Step 6 — `+experiment/+discriminate/+run` + `+experiment/+grouping/+run`</h5></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

Run 2026-09-21, **MATLAB R2024b**. Baseline: HEAD, working tree confirmed clean with `git status` before the first edit, so `git show HEAD:<path>` supplied the `old` copy of every file. Scope: exactly the 18 files section 0.1.6 names — the 9 files in `+experiment/+discriminate/+run/` and the 9 in `+experiment/+grouping/+run/`, re-checked on disk first (an untracked `+experiment/+grouping/+run/runExperiment.asv` autosave also sits in that directory; it's already gitignored/untracked, not one of the 9, and was left alone). File and function names were already settled by tranche 1 (`displayLevelStart.m`→`display_level_start.m` etc., in both trees) — this step's job was file-local content only: camelCase variables and the `SessionSettings`/`ExpSettings`/`SessionData`/`SettingsOut` TitleCase structs to snake_case, header shape, formatting, and commented-out-code triage.

**`check_stage1` over all 18 files, per-file rename maps — verbatim:**

```
PASS  discriminate/display_level_start
PASS  discriminate/fixation_interval
PASS  discriminate/give_feedback
PASS  discriminate/load_current_session
PASS  discriminate/load_stimuli

PASS  discriminate/stimulus_interval
PASS  grouping/display_level_start
PASS  grouping/fixation_interval
PASS  grouping/give_feedback
PASS  grouping/load_current_session
PASS  grouping/load_stimuli
PASS  grouping/response_interval
PASS  grouping/run_experiment
PASS  grouping/save_current_level
PASS  grouping/stimulus_interval

check_stage1: 18 / 18 pass, 0 fail
```

Run with a **per-file rename map** (18 maps — one per file per tree; `load_stimuli.m`'s two trees needed genuinely different maps since the grouping copy has a single `stimPosPix`/`stimPosDeg` where the discriminate copy has `stim1`/`stim2` pairs), following tranches 2-5's precedent. `assert_rename_bijection` passed for every map (embedded in `check_stage1`); no map's domain and range overlapped, and no new name collided with a name already live in its file. Only fields that are dot-qualified (`session_settings.fixPosPix`, `subject_file.levelCompleted`, etc.) were left untouched — per this tranche's own reading of section 3.1.1 zone 2/3, the struct *fields* of `SessionSettings`/`ExpSettings`/`SettingsOut` are cross-file vocabulary shared with the byte-identical/near-identical sibling file in the other tree (and, for `exp_settings`/`subject_file`, are themselves zone-3 frozen `.mat`-backed names) — only the struct *container* variables (`SessionSettings`→`session_settings`, `ExpSettings`→`exp_settings`, `SessionData`→`session_data`, `SettingsOut`→`settings_out`) were renamed, matching the instruction that TitleCase structs convert but leaving fields for Stage 3, consistent with tranche 4's session-struct-field precedent.

**Package-qualified call sites — present in every file, none needed a workaround.** Every one of these 18 files calls at least one of its 8 siblings package-qualified (`experiment.discriminate.run.display_level_start(s)`, `vislab.psychframework.run_experiment(exp_settings, hooks)`, `lib.gamma_correct(...)`, `lib.monitor_degrees_to_pixels(...)`), and none of those callee names were renamed by this tranche (they were already renamed in tranche 1) — so `check_stage1.m:30`'s lookbehind never had reason to fire, and all 18 files pass on the unmodified checker. Confirmed by reading every diff: the only tokens changed adjacent to a `.` are struct-field accesses on the *left* of the dot (`session_settings.fixPosPix`), never the callee name on the right.

**Golden-harness replay: passes, and covers none of this tranche's files.** `golden_harness('replay', 'tools/golden_ref.mat')` returns without throwing. Per section 1's coverage note, all of `+experiment` needs Psychtoolbox and a live display, so none of the harness's 15 entry points touch these 18 files — a documented negative, not a gap this tranche introduced. The replay's only job here is confirming the tranche didn't disturb anything the harness *does* cover, which it didn't (nothing outside `+experiment` was touched).

**`checkcode`: 16 messages total, identical in kind, count, and file to the pre-edit baseline (measured on the HEAD backups and again on the edited files).** `load_current_session.m` 3 (`LOAD` ×2, `USENS` ×1 — both trees), `response_interval.m` 2 (`STOUT` on the never-assigned `rt`, `NASGU` — both trees, see B3.16), `save_current_level.m` 2 (`LOAD`, `NODEF` — both trees; the `NODEF` is the "possibly used before defined" hit this tranche's dispatch flagged in advance, now at line 32/33 after the rename, was line 17), `stimulus_interval.m` 1 (`ASGLU` on the first, overwritten `stimulus_onset_time` assignment — both trees). The other 5 files per tree are clean, before and after. `load_stimuli.m`'s undefined `bit_depth_out` in the grouping tree (B2.8) is **not** flagged by `checkcode` — its static analysis does not catch this particular undefined-variable use, so the checker's silence here is not evidence the file is correct.

**Line-length exceptions — 2 lines over 100 characters, both inside the commented-out `settings_out.stimuli`/`settings_out.diffpair` lines in `+experiment/+grouping/+run/load_current_session.m:85-86`** (triaged *unfinished* in section 3.1.4 — the direct cause of B2.9). Reflowing them would mean rewriting dead code, which step 2c forbids until Stage 3's verdict lands. Every other line that would have crossed 100 after renaming — the two `display_level_start.m` `CenterRectOnPointd`/`DrawFormattedText` calls (both trees), `load_current_session.m`'s function signature and its two `settings_out.stimuli`/`.diffpair` *live* lines (discriminate tree only), `load_stimuli.m`'s `struct(...)` call (both trees), `run_experiment.m`'s function signature, its `load_current_session` call, and its `DrawFormattedText` call (both trees), and `stimulus_interval.m`'s header signature line (both trees) — was wrapped with `...` instead. No other line in any of the 18 files exceeds 100 characters.

**Decisions made here.**

1. **Struct containers convert to snake_case; their fields don't.** `SessionSettings`/`ExpSettings`/`SessionData`/`SettingsOut` (the bare variable/parameter names) became `session_settings`/`exp_settings`/`session_data`/`settings_out` everywhere they appear as a container. Every `.field` access on any of these (or on the zone-3 frozen `exp_settings`/`subject_file` loaded from disk) was left exactly as spelled — `fixPosPix`, `stim1PosPix`, `targetOutline`, `diffpair`, `currentLevel`, `bgPixValGamma`, `levelCompleted`, `binIndex`, `idx`, `response`, and the rest — because these are cross-file vocabulary shared with the sibling tree's byte-identical/near-identical copy of the same file (exactly tranche 4's session-struct-field precedent, now applied to `+experiment`'s own struct convention) and, for `exp_settings`/`subject_file`, genuinely `.mat`-backed. Renaming a struct field is Stage 3 territory (dot-qualified, `check_stage1` can't see it, and a half-renamed set of fields across 9+9 files would be exactly the "half-done rename is worse than none" trap the skill warns about).
2. **`S`/`w` (single TitleCase/bare letters standing for the session struct or window handle) became `s`/`win`, not left as-is or padded further.** `S` in `run_experiment.m`'s anonymous-function hooks and local functions is a single letter used dozens of times for the one struct threaded through every hook; lowercasing it to `s` is the direct TitleCase→snake_case conversion, not an expansion. `w` in `display_level_start.m` (a local copy of `session_settings.window`) became `win` rather than `window`, specifically to avoid shadowing the Signal Processing Toolbox's `window` function — the same class of avoidance section 2's builtin-shadow sweep already applies to `filter`/`diff`/`contour` elsewhere in the repo, just not previously triggered by anything in `+experiment`.
3. **A same-named local can't reuse the struct-field spelling when the two would collide.** `load_stimuli.m` builds a local `target_outline` (the raw, unclipped array, matching the file's own existing snake_case name for it) and separately needs a name for the *gamma-corrected* result that feeds the `targetOutline` struct field — reusing `target_outline` for both would silently make the second assignment overwrite what the first still needs. Named the gamma-corrected copy `target_outline_gamma` instead. The analogous split doesn't arise for `fixationTarget`→`fixation_target`, since that file only has one local by that role.
4. **Historical/dead-code references to a struct that no longer exists were left completely alone, not partially renamed.** `SubjectExpFile` (a struct with fields `condition`/`luminance`/`contrast`) appears only inside three commented-out lines across `load_current_session.m` (both trees) and does not exist anywhere live in the file — only lowercase `subject_file`, with entirely different fields (`idx`/`response`, `levelCompleted`, `binIndex`), does. Per rule 8 (tranche 2), a commented block only gets its live renames propagated when it would still run, referring to live names, if uncommented; since `SubjectExpFile` itself isn't live under any name, these three lines were left byte-for-byte as found. See section 3.1.4 for the verdicts.
5. **Renames were propagated into a commented-out block only when every name it references is still live under some (possibly renamed) spelling in that file — checked per block, not per file.** This split three ways across this tranche's files: `display_level_start.m`'s disabled foveal branch was left completely verbatim (references a `targetDestination` that doesn't exist under any spelling); `fixation_interval.m`'s disabled blank-interval tail in the grouping tree had its renames propagated (`session_settings`, `target_destination`, `blank_interval_s` are all live); `load_current_session.m`'s disabled `settings_out.stimuli`/`.diffpair` lines in the grouping tree also had renames propagated (`settings_out`, `exp_settings`, `subject_file`, `current_level`, `current_session` are all live) but were **not reflowed**, since unlike a rename a reflow is a real edit and these lines are the ones licensing this step's two documented line-length exceptions.
6. **Header cross-references follow the actual call chain, confirmed by reading `run_experiment.m` in each tree, not assumed from directory listing.** Every hook file's `See also` points at `RUN_EXPERIMENT` (which wires it as a hook) and at the one or two sibling hooks it is most directly sequenced with (`fixation_interval`↔`stimulus_interval`↔`response_interval`↔`give_feedback` as the per-trial sequence; `load_current_session`↔`load_stimuli`↔`run_experiment`↔`save_current_level` as the per-session/level sequence) — not a blanket cross-reference to all 8 siblings, which would say nothing.
7. **The two trees' shared per-file rename map was applied identically wherever the underlying code is identical, and only diverged where the code itself already diverges** (`load_stimuli.m`'s `stim1`/`stim2` pair vs. single `stim`; `load_current_session.m`'s otherwise-identical map applies to both despite the `idx`/`response` field difference, B3.14, since that difference is a struct-field name, not a local variable this tranche renamed). This keeps the eventual S1.1/S2.1 merges (byte-identical and near-duplicate files respectively) clean, per this tranche's own dispatch instruction.

**Header-doc corrections made along the way.** All 18 files gained the full exemplar shape (summary, package-qualified signature, `Inputs`/`Output` with units where known, `See also`) — 4 of them (`give_feedback.m` both trees, `load_stimuli.m` both trees) had no header at all before this tranche; the rest had a one-line or partial header. The 5 stale shout-caps headers section 2/this tranche's dispatch flagged were all fixed: `display_level_start.m`'s `%% presenttargetonly` (not even a proper header line, a stray section marker) → `%DISPLAY_LEVEL_START`; `fixation_interval.m`'s `%PRESENTFIXATIONCROSS` → `%FIXATION_INTERVAL`; `response_interval.m`'s `%% waitforresponse` → `%RESPONSE_INTERVAL`; `save_current_level.m`'s `%LOADCURRENSESSIONS` (itself a typo for "current sessions") → `%SAVE_CURRENT_LEVEL`; `stimulus_interval.m`'s `%DRAWSTIMULUS` → `%STIMULUS_INTERVAL` — all ×2, one per tree. `response_interval.m`'s original header also mislabeled its own second output as absent (the signature `[response, RT]` was undocumented); the new header documents `rt` and flags B3.16 inline rather than silently describing a working two-output function.

**Commented-out code.** 6 rows triaged, all newly found (the survey's approximate entries for this directory undercounted and mislocated slightly — its `fixationInterval.m:24-27` reference is the grouping tree's blank-interval tail, correctly located; its `giveFeedback.m`/`displayLevelStart.m`/`loadCurrentSession.m` "1-4 lines each" bucket resolves into the other 5 rows below, with `give_feedback.m`'s three one-line sound sketches counted together as one row since they're the same disabled idea repeated per branch) — see section 3.1.4. Verdicts: 4 unfinished, 1 alternative, 1 superseded. **No commented-out code exists in `save_current_level.m` or `stimulus_interval.m` in either tree, or in `run_experiment.m`'s local functions** — a documented negative, checked file by file while reading.

**Bugs found — five new, none fixed, and this is where the tranche's "note anything suspicious about the two trees' `run_experiment.m`/`load_stimuli.m` diffs" instruction lands.** Reading `load_stimuli.m` side by side turned up more than copy-paste drift: 🟠 **B2.8** (the grouping tree's `load_stimuli.m` uses an undefined `bit_depth_out` because the whole gamma-correction/target-creation block present in the discriminate tree's copy is missing — errors immediately, on every call) and 🟠 **B2.9** (that same tree's `load_current_session.m`/`load_stimuli.m` never set `stim1PosPix`/`stim2PosPix`/`targetOutline`/`diffpair`, fields that `display_level_start.m`, `stimulus_interval.m`, `give_feedback.m`, `save_current_level.m`, and `run_experiment.m`'s `level_end` — all byte-identical or near-identical to the discriminate tree's copies — read by exactly those names; masked today only because B2.8 throws first in the same call chain). Both are reachable, both error rather than silently miscomputing, hence B2 not B1. Two lower-confidence tree-difference findings were also filed rather than resolved, per instruction: 🟡 **B3.14** (`load_current_session.m`'s `subject_file.idx` vs `subject_file.response`, the one remaining difference between the two trees' otherwise-identical session-selection logic) and 🟡 **B3.15** (`response_interval.m`'s missing ESCAPE-abort branch in the grouping tree). One bug found independent of the tree comparison: 🟡 **B3.16** (`response_interval.m`'s second output `rt` is declared but never assigned, in both trees — `checkcode`'s `STOUT` confirms it — reachable only if the unseen `vislab.psychframework.run_experiment` ever requests it).

**One line-number correction to a previously recorded item.** `save_current_level.m:17`'s `[NODEF]` hit (flagged in advance by this tranche's dispatch) is confirmed still present after the rename, now at line 32 (discriminate) / 33 (grouping) — `checkcode` output pasted above. It is `subject_file.levelCompleted(session_number)` on the right-hand side of its own increment, which `checkcode`'s static analysis can't see is actually initialized by the `load(...)`-without-named-outputs two lines above; not a real bug, a pre-existing false positive from the same `LOAD` pattern already noted elsewhere in this repo (section 2).

**No new optimizations or structural items found.** These 18 files are almost entirely Psychtoolbox display/IO calls and struct plumbing — no loop-invariant work, no repeated file I/O beyond the already-recorded pattern, no `inv`/`pinv`/`det`, no unpreallocated growth, no `parfor`. S2.1 (merging the near-duplicate files) and S1.1 (merging the byte-identical ones) both had their premises re-confirmed by reading every file side by side; no new merge candidate or vectorization opportunity turned up.

**Deliberately left alone.** `run_experiment.m`'s historical mention of "the old runExperiment + runLevel + runTrial" (both trees) — per tranche 1's decision 2, a reference to deleted legacy functions, not renamed. `mk_texseg_session.m`-style struct-field freezing logic doesn't apply here since this tranche's struct fields (`SessionSettings`'s etc.) were never proposed for renaming by section 3.1.1 in the first place — noted only because the reasoning is the same shape as tranche 4's. `load_stimuli.m`'s unused `bg_pix_val`/`bgPixVal` local in the grouping tree (used once, at `fixation_pixel_val = 0.5*bg_pix_val`) — not dead, just noted as read. Every `save`/`load` string literal (`'exp_files/...'`, `'subject_file'`) and the zone-3 frozen `exp_settings`/`subject_file` names: confirmed present but frozen, not a documented negative this time since they are genuinely there and untouched.

**`Contents.m` — created now, for `+run` only.** `+experiment/+discriminate/+run/Contents.m` and `+experiment/+grouping/+run/Contents.m` were both created, each listing its own 9 files. Chosen over deferring to tranche 7 (`+prep`) because `+run` is a complete, self-contained sub-package on its own — every file in it is either called by `run_experiment.m` or is `run_experiment.m` itself, none of them call into `+prep`, and `+prep`'s own files (session setup, subject registration) are a genuinely separate concern that will get its own `Contents.m` when tranche 7 runs. The grouping tree's `Contents.m` inline-flags every entry whose file is broken as written (B2.8/B2.9/B3.14/B3.15/B3.16), following tranche 4's precedent of not letting `help` silently claim a broken function works. The top-level `+experiment`, `+discriminate`, and `+grouping` package `Contents.m` files are left for whichever tranche finishes last between this one and tranche 7, per this tranche's dispatch note.

**Baseline note for later tranches.** The working tree is clean again after this step's commit; HEAD remains a valid `check_stage1` baseline, and no `+experiment/+run` path changed beyond the 18 files above and the two new `Contents.m` files.
