<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

Run 2026-09-21, **MATLAB R2024b**. Baseline: HEAD, working tree confirmed clean with `git status` before the first edit, so `git show HEAD:<path>` supplied the `old` copy of every file. Scope: exactly the 5 files section 0.1.7 names — `generate_stimuli.m`, `setup_experiment.m`, `setup_subject.m` in `+experiment/+discriminate/+prep/`, and `setup_experiment.m`, `setup_subject.m` in `+experiment/+grouping/+prep/` — re-checked on disk first (270 lines total, matching the plan's estimate exactly; an untracked, already-gitignored `generate_stimuli.asv` autosave also sits in the discriminate `+prep` folder and was left alone). File and function names were already settled by tranche 1 (both `setup_experiment.m`/`setup_subject.m` were already snake_case filenames; `generate_stimuli.m` was likewise already snake_case and not renamed) — this step's job was file-local content only: camelCase locals to snake_case, the `diff` builtin shadow, header shape, formatting, and commented-out-code triage.

**`check_stage1` over all 5 files, per-file rename maps — verbatim:**

```
PASS=1  discriminate/prep/generate_stimuli
PASS=1  discriminate/prep/setup_experiment
PASS=1  discriminate/prep/setup_subject
PASS=1  grouping/prep/setup_experiment
PASS=1  grouping/prep/setup_subject
check_stage1: 5 / 5 pass, 0 fail
```

Run with a **per-file rename map** (5 maps), following tranches 2-6's precedent. `assert_rename_bijection` passed for every map (embedded in `check_stage1`). Per-file maps, new→old: `generate_stimuli.m` — `n_tex`→`nTex`, `n_trials`→`nTrials`, `i_tex`→`iTex`, `i_trial`→`iTrial`, `patch1`→`ptch1`, `patch2`→`ptch2`, `i_tex2`→`iTex2`, `patch_joined`→`ptch` (the joined-patch local, renamed away from the vocabulary table's literal `ptch`→`patch` to avoid shadowing the `patch` graphics builtin — confirmed via `which('patch','-all')`, see Decisions below); discriminate `setup_experiment.m` — `n_tex`/`n_trials`/`n_levels`/`n_sessions`/`folder_out`→`nTex`/`nTrials`/`nLevels`/`nSessions`/`folderOut`; discriminate `setup_subject.m` — same four plus `i_session`→`i` (the session-index loop counter, avoiding the imaginary-unit shadow); grouping `setup_experiment.m` — `n_texs`/`n_patches`/`n_trials`/`n_levels`/`n_sessions`/`n_regions`/`is_diff_trial`/`i_session`/`n_tex`/`i_trial_flat`/`folder_out`→`nTexs`/`nPatches`/`nTrials`/`nLevels`/`nSessions`/`nRegions`/`diff`/`iSession`/`nTex`/`iTrial_flat`/`folderOut`; grouping `setup_subject.m` — the same four as discriminate's copy (`n_trials`/`n_levels`/`n_sessions`/`folder_out`), no `i_session` map entry needed since that rename only occurs inside a commented-out block canon strips anyway.

**Package-qualified call sites — present in 2 of 5 files, none needed a workaround.** `generate_stimuli.m` calls `lib.texture_patch(...)` 5 times; discriminate's `setup_experiment.m` calls `experiment.discriminate.prep.generate_stimuli(...)`; grouping's `setup_experiment.m` calls `grouping.mk_texseg_session(...)`, `grouping.mk_trl_points(...)`, and `lib.gamma_correct(...)` (4 times). None of these callee names were renamed by this tranche (already renamed in tranche 1), so `check_stage1.m:30`'s lookbehind never had reason to fire. Confirmed by reading every diff (pasted above in the plan's dispatch context and re-verified here): the only tokens changed adjacent to a `.` are struct-field/array accesses on the *left* of the dot (`session.sz`, `session.pw`, etc.) or local-variable arguments, never a callee name on the right.

**Golden-harness replay: passes, and covers none of this tranche's files.** `golden_harness('replay', 'tools/golden_ref.mat')` returns without throwing (verbatim checksum table matches the harness's own reference dump). Per section 1's coverage note, `+experiment` needs Psychtoolbox and a live display, and these 5 files additionally need either the Brodatz/texture data or the natural-image set — none of the harness's entry points touch them, a documented negative, not a gap this tranche introduced.

**`checkcode`: 1 message total, identical in kind, count, and line (modulo the rename) to the pre-edit baseline.** `+experiment/+grouping/+prep/setup_experiment.m` carries the one pre-existing `NOPRT` hit (missing suppressing semicolon on the debug `[i_session i_trial_flat]` display, now at line 84, was line 56 before this tranche's edits) — confirmed present, unchanged, and deliberately not fixed (Stage 2 territory). The other 4 files are clean, before and after (the "function name known by file name" messages `checkcode` reported against this tranche's own HEAD-backup copies are an artifact of the backup files' own renamed filenames, not a real pre-existing message — confirmed by re-running `checkcode` against the actual HEAD paths).

**Line-length exceptions — 0 lines over 100 characters after edits.** The survey/dispatch's count of "4 lines over 100, worst at line 58 (160 chars)" undercounted slightly on re-measure: 5 lines in grouping's `setup_experiment.m` exceeded 100 characters before this tranche's spacing/renames (lines 22, 29, 35, 58, 61 — worst at line 58, 160 chars, matching the dispatch note), and renaming pushed the total from 5 lines to a slightly different 5-line set (lines 70, 86, 87, 88 after the first pass of fixes) before all were resolved. Every trailing-comment line was fixed by moving the comment to its own line above the code (invisible to `canon`, since comments are stripped); every long *code* line (the `grouping.mk_trl_points(...)` call and its `lib.gamma_correct(...)` companions) was wrapped with `...` continuation instead, since these are genuine multi-argument calls, not string literals. No line in any of the 5 files exceeds 100 characters after the edits (re-verified with `awk`).

**Decisions made here.**

1. **The zone-1 vocabulary table's literal `ptch`→`patch` mapping does not apply everywhere it looks like it should.** `generate_stimuli.m`'s `ptch1`/`ptch2` locals became `patch1`/`patch2` per the table, but the bare `ptch` local in the `'joined'` branch (the full 128x64 patch before it's split top/bottom into the pair) could not become bare `patch`, because `patch` is a live MATLAB graphics builtin (`which('patch','-all')` resolves to `toolbox/matlab/graphics/graphics/primitive/patch`) — renaming into it would be a new builtin shadow, the exact class of mistake this plan's builtin-shadow sweep exists to catch. Named it `patch_joined` instead.
2. **The `diff` builtin shadow (section 2's finding, `+experiment/+grouping/+prep/setup_experiment.m:24`) is fixed by renaming the local to `is_diff_trial`**, matching the file's own comment ("condition of each trial. diff=1, same=0") — a boolean per-trial flag, not a difference operator. The `struct(...)` field name `'diff'` (dot-qualified-equivalent — a string literal field name, cross-file vocabulary shared with the `+run` package's `exp_settings.diff` reads) was left exactly as spelled; only the local variable feeding it was renamed. Same struct-field-vs-local-container split tranche 6 used throughout.
3. **Struct field name strings inside every `struct(...)` call are frozen/cross-file vocabulary, not touched, even though several are camelCase or contain the same word as a renamed local.** Both `setup_experiment.m` files build `exp_settings` via `struct('nTex', n_tex, ...)`-style calls; the field-name string (`'nTex'`, `'nTrials'`, `'bgPixVal'`, `'loadSessionStimuli'`, `'stimulusIntervalMs'`, etc.) is read by the sibling `+run` package's files (confirmed by grep — `load_stimuli.m` in both trees reads `exp_settings.nTrials`/`.nLevels` by exactly these spellings) and by `exp_settings`/`subject_file`'s own zone-3 frozen-`.mat` status; only the local variable supplying each field's *value* was renamed to snake_case. This produces the same half-renamed-by-design struct that tranche 6 established for `SessionSettings`-style containers, just via `struct(...)` calls rather than dot-assignment.
4. **`exp_settings`/`subject_file` (the loaded/saved variable names) are zone-3 frozen and were left untouched in all 5 files**, per section 3.1.1 zone 3 — stated explicitly in each file's new header's "Note" line, together with which struct fields are frozen alongside them. Every `save`/`load` string literal (`'exp_settings.mat'`, `'subject_file'`, the `exp_files/<exp_type>/...` path strings) is likewise untouched.
5. **`folderOut`→`folder_out` in all 4 `setup_*.m` files** — a plain local, free to rename, no cross-file vocabulary involved.
6. **The lone `i` loop counter (discriminate's `setup_subject.m`, session-index loop) became `i_session`**, matching the descriptive-index convention tranche 6 used for camelCase `iSession`-style counters elsewhere in `+experiment`, and avoiding the imaginary-unit shadow section 2 flagged as a repo-wide pattern to fix wherever a tranche touches the file.
7. **Renames were propagated into the one commented-out block that would still run if uncommented** (grouping's `setup_subject.m:37-42`, the disabled `idx`-via-`randperm` block — see the triage below): every name it references (`n_trials`, `n_sessions`, `n_levels`, `subject_file`) is still live in the file under its renamed spelling, so tranche 6's rule 5/8 precedent applied. The one-line `% seeds = nan(...)` sketch in grouping's `setup_experiment.m` got the same treatment for the same reason.

**Header-doc corrections made along the way.** All 5 files gained the full exemplar shape (summary, package-qualified signature, `Inputs`/`Output` with units, `See also`, plus a `Note` line naming every frozen/cross-file name left alone and why) — `generate_stimuli.m` had **no header at all** before this tranche (section 2's finding, confirmed), and the other 4 had only a one-line `% Create all ...` comment.

**Commented-out code — 2 blocks triaged, both newly found (the survey's single "small block, `+experiment/+grouping/+prep/setup_subject.m:10-15`" entry undercounted the directory by one block; `generate_stimuli.m` and discriminate's two files carry no commented-out code at all — confirmed while reading, a documented negative).** See section 3.1.4 for both rows in full. Verdicts: 2 unfinished/alternative-leaning (one *alternative*, one *unfinished*) — grouping's `setup_subject.m:37-42` disables the exact `idx`-via-`randperm` block the discriminate tree keeps live (an **alternative**, and the direct cause of B3.14), and grouping's `setup_experiment.m:47` sketches an unwired `seeds` field by analogy with the discriminate tree's live one (**unfinished**, since nothing in the grouping pipeline produces a seed to track).

**Bugs found — two new B2s and one new B3, none fixed.** The dispatch's specific ask — whether the two `+prep` trees populate `exp_settings` with everything the two `+run` trees (already found broken in tranche 6) actually read — turned up something bigger than a `+prep`-only issue: 🟠 **B2.10** (`exp_settings.monitorSizePix`, read as the very first field access in *both* trees' `load_stimuli.m`, is never assigned anywhere in the repo — not by either `+prep/setup_experiment.m`, not by either `load_current_session.m` — so both trees error immediately on this, before B2.8/B2.9 (previously thought grouping-only) ever get reached; this means the *discriminate* tree, which tranche 6 had no reason to flag as broken, is equally unable to reach `load_stimuli.m`'s body today) and 🟠 **B2.11** (grouping's `load_stimuli.m:47` also reads `exp_settings.ecc`, which only the discriminate tree's `+prep/setup_experiment.m` sets — masked by B2.10 throwing first in the same file, same shape as B2.8/B2.9's masking relationship). One latent finding independent of the tree-divergence check: 🟡 **B3.17** (`moniter_brightness`, an unread, typo'd field saved by both trees, harmless today but a hazard for a future reader expecting `monitor_brightness`).

**No new optimizations or structural items found beyond a line-number update.** `generate_stimuli.m`'s two loops were reread as the dispatch asked: the first (same-texture pairs) remains cleanly parfor-sliceable (disjoint writes, no cross-iteration reads), confirming O3.4's existing claim; the second (different-texture pairs) remains genuinely sequential, since each outer iteration's `prev_tex` lookup reads every prior iteration's `tex` entries to avoid resampling a pair — not a new finding, but O3.4's line reference was stale (`:13-46`) and has been corrected to `:32-66`/`:68-93` in section 0.5. No loop-invariant work, repeated file I/O beyond O1.2's already-recorded pattern, `inv`/`pinv`/`det`, unpreallocated growth, or `parfor` candidate turned up in the other 4 files — mostly struct plumbing and one `save`/`load` pair each.

**Deliberately left alone.** `+experiment/+grouping/+prep/setup_experiment.m:84`'s unsuppressed `[i_session i_trial_flat]` debug display (the `NOPRT` lint hit) — Stage 2 territory, not fixed here. `patch_sz`/`stim_sz` and other already-snake_case abbreviations in grouping's `setup_experiment.m` — left exactly as spelled, since this tranche's mandate was casing conversion, not vocabulary expansion (no wording changes, per the dispatch instruction), and these aren't camelCase.

**`Contents.m` — created for both `+prep` sub-packages, and for the three top-level packages tranche 6 deferred.** `+experiment/+discriminate/+prep/Contents.m` and `+experiment/+grouping/+prep/Contents.m` were created, each listing that tree's 2-3 files. Tranche 6's own `+run` `Contents.m` files were already on disk (confirmed via `find ... -iname Contents.m` before starting), and tranche 6's log explicitly deferred the top-level `+experiment`, `+discriminate`, and `+grouping` package `Contents.m` files to "whichever of 6 or 7 finishes last" — since tranche 6's commit is already on `HEAD` and this tranche is finishing second, those three were created now: `+experiment/Contents.m` (naming both experiment types and noting the S1.1/S2.1 merge candidates), `+experiment/+discriminate/Contents.m`, and `+experiment/+grouping/Contents.m` (the last noting the tree is not currently runnable end to end, per B2.8-B2.11).

**Baseline note for later tranches.** The working tree is clean again after this step's commit; HEAD remains a valid `check_stage1` baseline, and no `+experiment/+prep` path changed beyond the 5 files above and the 5 new `Contents.m` files (2 `+prep`, 3 top-level).

<summary><h5 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:0.92em">Step 8 — `+general`</h5></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

Run 2026-09-21, **MATLAB R2024b**. Baseline: HEAD, working tree confirmed clean with `git status` before the first edit, so `git show HEAD:<path>` supplied the `old` copy of every file. Scope: exactly the 3 files section 0.1.8 names, re-checked on disk first — `+general/` holds exactly `compute_exp_error_mat.m` (63 lines), `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m` (269) and `simulate_discrimination.m` (368), 700 lines total, matching section 2 exactly, with no fourth file and no stray autosave. File names were already settled by tranche 1 (none of the three was renamed); this step's job was file-local content only.

**`check_stage1` over all 3 files, per-file rename maps — verbatim:**

```
PASS  +general/compute_exp_error_mat.m
PASS  +general/nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m
PASS  +general/simulate_discrimination.m

check_stage1: 3 / 3 pass, 0 fail
```

Run with a **per-file rename map**, following tranches 2-7's precedent (`image_size` and `patch_size` are new names in two of the three files and would collide with each other's old names across a combined map). `assert_rename_bijection` passed for every map.

**Package-qualified call sites — present in two files, neither needed a workaround.** `simulate_discrimination.m` calls `vislab.lib.otf_filter`, `vislab.nat_stat_bayes.dv_power`, `lib.hist_dv` and `lib.edge_props_stim`; `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m` calls none (its three helper calls are bare — B2.12). None of those callee names were renamed by this tranche, so `check_stage1.m:30`'s lookbehind never had reason to fire, and all 3 files pass on the unmodified checker. Confirmed by reading the diffs: the only renamed tokens adjacent to a `.` are struct accesses on the *left* of the dot (`exp_settings.nTex`, `tx_discrim_bd.grad_m_bd`, `lms_file.lms`), never a callee name on the right.

**Golden-harness replay: passes, and covers none of this tranche's 3 files.** `golden_harness('replay', 'tools/golden_ref.mat')` returns without throwing. Per section 1's coverage note, all of `+general` is data-dependent — these three files need the natural-image set, the Brodatz sheets, or `exp_files` subject data — so none of the harness's 15 entry points reach them. A documented negative, not a gap this tranche introduced; the replay's only job here is confirming nothing the harness *does* cover moved, which it didn't (nothing outside `+general/` was touched). None of the three was executed end to end, per this tranche's scope: Stage 1 verification for `+general` is pattern-check-only.

**`checkcode`: 4 messages total, identical in id, count and file to the pre-edit baseline** (measured on the HEAD copies and again on the edited files; only line numbers moved). `compute_exp_error_mat.m` 1 (`ISCL`, "use isscalar instead of length comparison", now line 56, was 21), `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m` 0, `simulate_discrimination.m` 3 (`NOPTS` ×2 on the unsuppressed `[i_tex i_tex i_samp]` / `[i_tex j_tex i_samp]` progress displays, now lines 131 and 183, were 69 and 114; `NASGU` on the first `results = classify_normals(...)`, whose value is overwritten before being read, now line 247, was 173). All four are pre-existing and deliberately untouched: the two `NOPTS` are Stage 2 (section 1 already argues they are accidental, not deliberate interactive output, since both sit three loops deep), the `NASGU` is Stage 2, and `ISCL` is a real AST change and so neither Stage 1 nor one of Stage 2's four patterns.

Worth recording as a correction to section 2: it named `simulate_discrimination.m` the repo's worst-linting file in spirit, but `checkcode` gives it only 3 messages — `+lib/edge_props_stim.m`'s 18 remain the worst, and the 15 `STOUT`s there are the counterpart of *this* file's 29-output call (B2.4), not of anything `checkcode` flags here.

**Line-length exceptions — 1 line over 100 characters, inside a commented-out block.** `simulate_discrimination.m:77` (142 chars) is the disabled `% load('vislab-common/data/eff_coding_bins.mat') % load the efficient-coding histogram bins ...` line, whose length is entirely its trailing comment; reflowing it would mean rewriting dead code, which step 2c forbids until its Stage 3 verdict lands. **No live line in any of the three files exceeds 100 characters.** This tranche started from the repo's worst offender — section 2's `simulate_discrimination.m:229` at **330 characters**, re-verified at that exact line before editing (the nine-cue `classify_normals` call for the combined decision variable) — and 23 other lines over 100 in that file. Every one was wrapped with `...`: the six `classify_normals` calls whose two long bracketed cue lists are now one continuation line each, the two 29-output `lib.edge_props_stim` calls (one continuation per five-scale group), the three `reshape(permute(...))` session-concatenation lines, and the four `plot(...)` calls in the experimental-overlay cell. In `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m` twelve lines crossed 100 *after* renaming (the abbreviations were shorter than their expansions) — the `patches_near`/`patches_far` four-subscript assignments, at 101-102 characters — and all twelve were wrapped. `compute_exp_error_mat.m` needed two wraps (the two `reshape(permute(...))` lines) and now has none over 100. No tabs and no trailing whitespace in any of the three files, before or after.

**Decisions made here.** This tranche sets no new vocabulary — it inherits cluster B's from tranche 2 and cluster C's from tranche 3, and the `i_`-prefix loop-counter convention that `+general` already used was kept rather than forced into cluster C's shapes, per this tranche's dispatch instruction. What it did have to decide:

1. **A script's header invocation line names the file, not a package path.** Tranche 3 gave its two callerless scripts a `grouping.<name>` invocation line. That is misleading: MATLAB's package dispatch covers functions and classes, not scripts, so `general.simulate_discrimination` does not and cannot work. All three headers here therefore read `%   run('+general/<name>.m')`, with an explicit sentence saying the file is a script and pointing at S2.5. **This amends tranche 3's decision 10 for any later tranche that documents a script** — and the two `+grouping` scripts' headers should be corrected to match when S2.5 touches them (not a new Stage 3 item; S2.5 already owns those files).
2. **`ptch` → `patch_second`, not bare `patch`, and not `patch1`/`patch2`.** Tranche 2's rule B-4 forbids a bare `patch` (Graphics builtin). In `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m` the variable is always "the second patch of the pair" (the reference patch is `ptchr` → `patch_ref`), so `patch_second` names the role rather than inventing an index. Tranche 7 solved the same collision differently (`patch_joined`), for the same reason — role name over index — so the two are consistent in method even though the words differ.
3. **A frozen `save`-string variable keeps its original spelling; the local that feeds it is renamed freely.** `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m`'s nine zone-3 names (`ptchn9`/`ptchf9`/`pcnt9` and the 10 and 12 triples) appear as string literals inside `save(...)`, so the *variable* names are the serialised names and cannot change. The three working arrays that feed them are not in any `save` string and were renamed (`ptchn`→`patches_near`, `ptchf`→`patches_far`, `pcnt`→`patch_count`), producing a deliberate mismatch at nine lines like `ptchn9 = patches_near(...)`. Same pattern as tranche 2's rule B-1 and tranche 4's session-struct-field note; each file's header says which names are frozen and why.
4. **`filter` → `apply_filter`, following tranche 2's decision 3 exactly.** `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m`'s `filter = 1` toggle shadows the Signal Processing Toolbox builtin — the third and last site of section 2's `filter` shadow finding, after `downsample`/`downsample_old`. Unlike those two it is a plain local, not an `inputParser` mirror, so nothing here is deferred to S2.7.
5. **A name that would be reverse-renamed inside a command-syntax string literal cannot be used, and `canon` catches it.** The obvious rename for `acc_subj` was `accuracy`, and `check_stage1` **failed** on it: `tree2str` renders `ylabel 'accuracy'` (command syntax, three lines below) with the word bare, so the reverse-rename rewrote the *string* to `'acc_subj'` and the comparison diverged. Renamed to `subject_accuracy` instead, which the `(?![A-Za-z0-9_])` lookahead cannot match inside `'accuracy'`. Worth remembering for tranche 9, which touches `texture_grouping.m`: command-syntax arguments (`xlabel foo`, `hold on`, `colormap winter`, `axis normal`) are string literals that *look* like identifiers to the rename regex, so no new variable name may equal a word used that way anywhere in the same file.
6. **Splitting a `;`-separated statement pair must preserve each half's own semicolon.** `colormap winter; colorbar` and `figure; hold on` both became two lines, and the first attempt dropped the semicolon from `colormap winter` and `figure` — which `canon` sees, because `tree2str` preserves display-vs-suppress, and `check_stage1` duly failed. The correct split keeps `colormap winter;` and `figure;` suppressed and leaves the second half as it was. Two spacing-only splits, two real check failures: the checker earned its keep twice on this file.
7. **`itrial` and `iTrial` are two different variables in one script and could not both become `i_trial`.** `simulate_discrimination.m`'s first cell counts simulated trials in `itrial`; its second cell counts real experimental trials in `iTrial`. `assert_rename_bijection` requires a unique *range* as well as domain, so one of them had to take a different name: `itrial`→`i_trial` (the dominant use, 12 sites) and `iTrial`→`i_exp_trial` (4 sites, and the qualifier is genuinely informative since that cell is about the experiment, not the simulation). The mirror of rule C-6: there, one old name served several roles; here, two old names served one role each and collided on the obvious new name.
8. **`b` → `noise_const`, matching `+lib/power_dv.m` and `rp.m`.** `simulate_discrimination.m`'s `b = 10; % weak power suppression parameter` is passed straight into `vislab.nat_stat_bayes.dv_power(patch_a, patch_b, b, ...)` — the same role tranche 2 (decision 10) and tranche 5 (decision 6) already settled as `noise_const`, documented as dimensionless because it is added to a power spectrum normalized to unit mean. Likewise `epsl`→`eps_offset`, reusing the exact name tranche 2 gave the same constant inside `edge_props_stim`.
9. **`sz`/`psz` had to be read per file again, per rule C-2, and here both were pixel sizes.** `simulate_discrimination.m`'s `img_sz`/`patch_sz` are the Brodatz sheet and patch side lengths in pixels → `image_size`/`patch_size`. `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m` has four: `sz1`/`psz1` are the level-1 sizes → `base_image_size`/`base_patch_size`, `sz`/`psz` the level-adjusted ones → `image_size`/`patch_size`, and `psz2 = 2*psz` is the width of a patch *pair*, so it became `pair_width` rather than `patch_size2` — the arithmetic is a doubling but the quantity is a pair width, and every use is as the second dimension of a pair array. Neither file's `sz` was a patch-grid count, so `grid_size` appears nowhere in this tranche.

**Header-doc corrections made along the way.** All three files gained the full exemplar shape (summary, invocation, prose, labelled `Inputs`/`Outputs` with units, `Note`, `See also`); none had more than a one-line `%% ...` section marker before. `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m`'s opening comment named itself **`nat_near_far_patches.m`** — a stale filename that drops the `_bayes` suffix — replaced with a real summary. Two argument meanings were also stated properly for the first time: `pupil_diameter` in millimetres and `wavelength` in nanometres (from section 3.1.1's units glossary, which already fixed `pd`/`w`), and `pad_val` as a grey level on 0-255 rather than the unlabelled "mean greylevel value" the original comment gave. Each header additionally carries an explicit list of every data path the file reads and whether it resolves — see the path audit below, which is the substance of this tranche.

**The data-path audit — exactly what these two files read, and whether it resolves.** Checked on disk 2026-09-21 against the confirmed-present siblings `../vislab-common/data/CPS natural images` and `../vislab-common/data/textures`. Line numbers are post-edit.

| file:line | path or name as written | resolves? |
|---|---|---|
| `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m:50-51` | `addpath('C:\Users\Bill Geisler\Documents\Projects\Texture\Discrimination\Texture Discrimination Code')` | **no** — machine-specific (B2.3). Presumably where `aply_otf`/`rgb2lms`/`dsmp` lived (B2.12) |
| `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m:52-53` | `addpath('C:\Users\Bill Geisler\Documents\Projects\Texture\Images\CPS Set-9-10-12_16-bit linear')` | **no** — machine-specific (B2.3). This is the natural-image set, and it **is present locally**, at `../vislab-common/data/CPS natural images` = `cfg.paths.natural_images` |
| `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m:79-80` | `fullfile(fileparts(mfilename('fullpath')), '..', '..', 'vislab-common/data', 'cps_rgb2lms.mat')` | **yes** — the only correctly-built path in either file. From `+general/` the two `..` land on the `Geisler Lab` parent, and `vislab-common/data/cps_rgb2lms.mat` exists. Equivalent to `fullfile(cfg.paths.data_root, 'cps_rgb2lms.mat')` |

Every image is 2844x4284, 48-bit. So the only thing wrong is the directory, and `cfg.paths.natural_images` already names the right one |
| `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m:184-185,264-265,344-345` | `append('patch_pairs_9', num_str, '.mat')` etc., written to the **current folder** | writes wherever MATLAB happens to be; `cfg.paths.stimuli` is the intended home (`data/stimuli/`, which already holds a `textures/patch_pairs.mat`) |
| `simulate_discrimination.m:75` | `'vislab-common/data/nat_im_eff_coding_bins.mat'` | **no**, twice over (B2.14): missing `../`, and the real filename is `nat_im_eff_coding.mat`. That file **exists** and holds exactly the four variables this script then reads — `grad_m_bins`, `grad_o_bins`, `grad_p_bins`, `grey_hist_bins` (plus `grad_pca_*_bins`, `pca_coeffs`, `pca_coeffs_0`) |
| `simulate_discrimination.m:82` | `tex_dir = 'img_data/brodatz/'`, then `dir([tex_dir '*.gif'])` | **no** — no `img_data/` exists in this repo (S2.4). The 60 Brodatz sheets are at `../vislab-common/data/textures/brodatz/B<n>.gif` = `fullfile(cfg.paths.textures, 'brodatz')`, and the count matches `n_tex = 60` exactly |
| `simulate_discrimination.m:336-337` | `'exp_files/norm/exp_settings.mat'`, `'exp_files/norm/subject_out/neel.mat'` | **no** — `exp_files/` holds only `grouping`, `joined`, `sep` (B3.1) |
| `simulate_discrimination.m:383` | `'vislab-common/data/eff_coding_bins.mat'` | **no** — same two faults as line 75 (B2.14), and no file of that name exists under any prefix |
| `simulate_discrimination.m:386` | `'data/model/texture discrimination boundaries.mat'` | **yes**, from the repo root — the file exists and holds `tx_discrim_bd` with the four boundary fields the script reads (see B3.20 for their dimensions) |
| `simulate_discrimination.m:410,416` | `['img_data/brodatz/B', num2str(tex_a), '.gif']` | **no** — same as line 84 (S2.4) |

Three conclusions for the Stage 3 fix and the Stage 2 plan rewrite that follow this tranche. **First, the data is all here.** Every natural image, every Brodatz sheet, the RGB→LMS matrix and the efficient-coding bins exist locally, and `config()` already points at all of them (`cfg.paths.natural_images`, `cfg.paths.textures`, `cfg.paths.data_root`, `cfg.paths.model`). Section 0.2's Status line and section 1's harness-coverage note both assume the ~19 GB natural-image set "this machine doesn't have" — **that assumption is wrong and should be struck**; the blocker for a `+general` golden harness is not missing data but the three unresolvable functions (B2.12), two genuinely missing `.mat` files (B2.13), and the script-not-function shape (S2.5). **Second, only one of the twelve paths above is built correctly**, and it is the one that uses `fullfile(fileparts(mfilename('fullpath')), ...)` rather than a bare relative string — which is exactly what `config()` generalises. Routing all of these through `cfg.paths.*` is a single Stage 3 edit, not eleven. **Third, `setup.m` does not `addpath` the data store** (`setup.m:50-56` only warns if the folder is absent), so no choice of working directory makes a bare `'vislab-common/data/...'` string resolve — the relative-prefix half of B2.14 cannot be worked around by `cd`, it has to be fixed.

**Commented-out code.** 8 blocks triaged, 6 of them not in the survey's inventory (which listed only `simulate_discrimination.m:16-18,307-317` for this directory) — see section 3.1.4. Verdicts: 5 alternative, 1 superseded, 1 unfinished, 1 scaffolding. **`compute_exp_error_mat.m` carries no commented-out code at all**, and `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m` carries exactly one line of it (its `% rng('shuffle');`) despite being dense with `%` separator rules and prose — both checked line by line while reading, a documented negative. Nothing was deleted or converted. Per tranche 2's rule 8, renames were propagated into the two `% img_?=mean(img_?,3);` lines' enclosing scope only in the sense that nothing in them needed renaming (`img_a`/`img_b` were already so named), and **were not propagated** into the disabled bin-loading block (lines 77-79) or the disabled `edge_dv_diff` line (`:226`): the first references `n_bins`/`grey_hist_bins`, which are live only in the *third* cell and not at that point in the script, and the second references `edge_dv_diff` and `edge_length_dv_exp`, which exist nowhere in the file under any spelling. The frozen `save` string at line 333 (`"data/model/texture discrimination boundaries.mat"`, `"tx_discrim_bd"`) was left completely verbatim, per section 3.1.1's zone-3 rule.

**Bugs found — one B1, three B2s and four B3s, none fixed.** 🔴 **B1.3** (`nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m` reuses set 10's *count* as set 12's cumulative *offset*, so `ptchn12`/`ptchf12` are saved 6020 slices deep instead of 3940 with the first 2140 being other sets' data — silent, and the only arithmetic error in the file), 🟠 **B2.12** (`aply_otf`, `rgb2lms` and `dsmp` do not resolve from this repo; `aply_otf` errors on the first image since `apply_filter = 1` is the default), 🟠 **B2.13** (both `cdfs_abr_mo13_mo23_cs33*.mat` files are missing, and nothing in the script reads what they would have loaded anyway), 🟠 **B2.14** (`simulate_discrimination.m`'s two efficient-coding-bin loads have both a wrong relative prefix and a wrong filename, and this is the script's first executable statement), 🟡 **B3.18** (`compute_exp_error_mat.m` hardcodes `1:4` in both level loops against a `nLevels`-sized array), 🟡 **B3.19** (`nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m`'s sets 10 and 12 never recompute `size_x`/`size_y`/`dist_min` — harmless only because all 391 CPS images are the same size, verified), 🟡 **B3.20** (the first cell now fits 5-scale gradient boundaries while the third cell and the shipped `.mat` are both 4-scale — verified by loading the artifact: `q2` is 4x4 for all three gradient cues, 9x9 for `all_bd`), 🟡 **B3.21** (`compute_exp_error_mat.m` accumulates-and-normalizes the diagonal but assigns the off-diagonal, filed with low confidence since it only matters if a different-texture pair repeats).

**The three already-recorded items that live in these files were all re-read, and all three descriptions hold.** **B2.3** — confirmed at what are now lines 50-53 (was `:9,11`), both `addpath` calls verbatim and untouched; the second one is the natural-image directory, which per the audit above exists locally under a different name. **B3.1** — confirmed, now at `:334-335` (was `:245-246`); `exp_files/` really does hold only `grouping`, `joined`, `sep`. **S2.4** — confirmed and now precise: `simulate_discrimination.m` uses `'img_data/brodatz/'` at three sites (lines 82, 410, 416), no `img_data/` directory exists anywhere in this repo, and the 60 sheets it wants are the 60 `B<n>.gif` files in `../vislab-common/data/textures/brodatz`, i.e. `cfg.paths.textures`. **B2.4** was also re-read from the caller's side: `simulate_discrimination.m` asks `edge_props_stim` for all 29 outputs by position at three sites (now `:158-171`, `:208-221`, `:437-447`), of which it names 19 and discards 10 with `~`; the 14 it names beyond output 15 are the ones `edge_props_stim` never assigns, so every one of those three calls errors — confirming B2.4's reachability claim from this end. **B1.1** untouched, and its cost is now quantified for O2.3: it sits on a call made once per simulated trial *and once more per rejected draw*.

**Optimizations — one new, plus two confirmations.** 🟠 **O2.3** (`simulate_discrimination.m` computes the expensive `edge_props_stim` *before* the rejection test that only reads the two cheap decision variables, so every rejected draw pays for a result that is immediately overwritten — a pure reorder numerically, but RNG-perturbing while B1.1's stray `randi` stands, hence 🟠 and Unprofiled). O3.4's two claims about these files were both re-verified and its line numbers and reasoning updated: the image-load loop is cleanly sliced and RNG-free, and `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m`'s blocker is specifically the running `patch_count` index, which is computable from the loop variables but only as part of S2.8's restructuring. O1.4's premise (the unconditional `target_mask()` default) gains weight from this side too: `simulate_discrimination.m` is the caller that makes `edge_props_stim` a per-trial cost at all. Nothing else turned up — no `inv`/`pinv`/`det`, no unpreallocated growth (every storage array is preallocated at the top), no `parfor`, and no loop-invariant work beyond what O3.4/O2.3 already name.

**Structural items — one new.** 🟠 **S2.8** (collapse `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m`'s three near-identical ~60-line per-image-set blocks into one loop over a set table — ~120 duplicated lines, and doing so dissolves B1.3 and B3.19 by construction). S2.5's premise was re-confirmed for all three files (none has a `function` line; all three are scripts) and S3.2's from the caller's side (the 29-output positional call, three times over, with ten `~` placeholders each — the clearest argument in the repo for returning one struct).

**Deliberately left alone.** `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m`'s four dead normalization constants (`cnorm`, `m0`, `c0`, `ntype`, lines 59-62) and its dead `show_img`, `base_image_size` and `image_size` — assigned and never read anywhere in the file; `checkcode` does not flag them (it will not call an assignment dead in a script, since a later cell or the caller's workspace could read it), and deleting an assignment is Stage 2's fourth pattern, not Stage 1's. Their headers document only the constants that are actually used, so the dead ones are visible by their absence. `simulate_discrimination.m:92`'s `imgs = nan(640, 640, n_tex)`, which hardcodes 640 where `image_size = 640` is in scope two lines up — a real (if harmless) redundancy, and changing the literal to the variable is a real AST change, so not Stage 1; too marginal for an item, noted here so the next reader knows it was seen. The inconsistent `+ eps_offset` inside `log(lib.hist_dv(...))` — present at lines 154-155 (same trials) and absent at 205, 357 and 433 — **checked and cleared, not filed**: for different-texture trials a histogram decision variable of exactly zero gives `-Inf`, which the `> -10` rejection test at line 223 then rejects, so the guard is genuinely unnecessary on that path; the asymmetry is coherent rather than an oversight. Every `save`/`load` string literal and every zone-3 frozen name in all three files: confirmed present (nine in `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m`, plus `tx_discrim_bd`, `exp_settings`, `subject_file` and `lms` across the other two) and untouched — not a documented negative this time, since they are genuinely there.

**`+general/Contents.m` created**, listing all three scripts in two groups (simulation and model fitting; subject-data analysis), each entry the file's own header summary, with an opening note that none of the three can be called as `general.<name>` because they are scripts (S2.5) and an inline bug id on each file that is broken as written — following tranche 4's and 6's precedent of not letting `help general` silently claim a broken script works.

**Baseline note for later tranches.** The working tree is clean again after this step's commit; HEAD remains a valid `check_stage1` baseline, and no `+general` path changed beyond the 3 files above and the new `Contents.m`. Tranche 9 (`config.m`, `setup.m`, `texture_grouping.m`) is the last Stage 1 tranche, and this tranche's path audit is directly relevant to both of its first two files.

<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h5 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:0.92em">Step 9 — `config.m`, `setup.m`, `texture_grouping.m`</h5></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

Run 2026-09-21, **MATLAB R2024b**. Baseline: HEAD, working tree confirmed clean with `git status` before the first edit, so `git show HEAD:<path>` supplied the `old` copy of all three files. Scope: exactly the 3 root-level files section 0.1.9 names. The root listing was re-checked on disk first — it holds these three plus the 10 cluster-C files tranche 5 already cleaned, and nothing else. **This is the last Stage 1 tranche; 0.1 is now closed.**

**`check_stage1` over all 3 files, per-file rename maps — verbatim:**

```
PASS  config.m  (0 renames)
PASS  setup.m  (12 renames)
PASS  texture_grouping.m  (56 renames)

check_stage1: 3 / 3 pass, 0 fail
```

Per-file maps, following tranches 2-8's precedent. `assert_rename_bijection` passed for both non-empty maps — but only after one fix, see decision 5 below. `config.m` renamed **nothing**: every identifier in it was already snake_case and already clear (`cfg`, `repo_root`, and the dot-qualified field paths, which are zone 2 and deferred to Stage 3 regardless), so its entire diff is header, comments and reflow. That is a real check, not an omission: a zero-rename `check_stage1` still compares the two canonical forms in full.

**Golden-harness replay: passes, and covers 1 of this tranche's 3 files.** `golden_harness('replay', 'tools/golden_ref.mat')` returns without throwing (its `isequal` assert at `golden_harness.m:243` did not fire; exit status 0). `config()` is covered, by `config_path_fields` (8 field names, sorted), `config_optics` `[60 4 550]`, `config_rgb_to_lms_checksum` `24.3120` / size `[3 3]`, `config_norm` `[128 0.25]` and `config_seed` `0` — all unchanged. Step 0's decision 2 was respected: **paths are still recorded by field name only, never by value**, and nothing in this tranche's edit gives the harness an absolute path to checksum. The reference was **not** recaptured. `setup.m` and `texture_grouping.m` have **no harness coverage at all** — a documented negative, consistent with section 1: `setup.m` modifies the MATLAB path and may hit the network, and `texture_grouping.m` is a full driver needing real Brodatz data, so both are pattern-check-only for Stage 2. Neither was executed.

**`config()`'s extra before/after check — the one the skill requires of a config file — passes exactly.** The HEAD copy was placed beside the edited one under a temporary filename (so that `mfilename('fullpath')` resolved to the same repo root for both, which is the only way the comparison means anything) and both were called: `isequal(config_HEAD(), config())` returns **1**, and the field order is identical at both levels too. The temporary file was deleted immediately; `git status` confirms only the three intended files are modified.

**`checkcode`: 1 message total, identical in id, count and file to the pre-edit baseline** (measured on the HEAD copies and again on the edited files). `config.m` 0, `setup.m` 0, `texture_grouping.m` 1 — `CLALL` on the `clear all` at what is now line 58 (was line 3). That is the repo's only `CLALL` and this tranche's only lint message; it is untouched by instruction, and recorded as O3.7 rather than fixed.

**Line-length exceptions — 2 lines, both unbreakable string literals** (`setup.m:80-81`, 111 and 110 characters, the two middle pieces of the `noData` warning's message). They are already a `[...]` concatenation of three literals; breaking a literal into more pieces is a real `horzcat` change that `canon` would see, and rewording the message changes the literal itself, so neither is available in Stage 1. The nine other over-100 lines the survey counted for these two files (5 in `config.m`, 6 in `setup.m`) are all gone: `config.m`'s were code lines carrying long trailing comments, and the comments' content now lives in the header's Output block; `setup.m`'s were fixed by re-wrapping the `ensure_addon_on_path` call and by pulling two trailing comments onto their own lines above the statement. `texture_grouping.m` had no line over 100 before and has none after (one line, the region crop, would have crossed 100 after renaming and was wrapped with `...`). No tabs anywhere; the one trailing-whitespace line in the repo (`texture_grouping.m:21`) is gone.

**Decisions made here.** This tranche binds no later Stage 1 tranche, because there is none — the decisions below are recorded for Stage 2/3 and for the next repo.

1. **The header's self-name keeps a space after the `%`, matching the exemplar.** `vislab-common/+vislab/+lib/watson_otf.m` opens `% WATSON_OTF  Summary.`, and so does `config.m`, both before and after this tranche. Counted across the repo as it now stands: 53 files use `% NAME  ` and 31 use `%NAME  ` (6 of those indented inside the function body, cluster C's variant). So the exemplar's form is also the majority form, and all three files here use it. Tranche 2's decision 1 wrote the convention as `%NAME`, which is how `+lib` ended up; that is a cosmetic split not worth a sweep now, but the exemplar is the tiebreaker and this is recorded so Stage 6 does not read the 53/31 split as drift introduced by the cleanup.
2. **A zero-argument function still gets the full labelled block, with `Inputs` stated as `none`.** `config.m` and `setup.m` both read `%   Inputs` / `%     none.` rather than omitting the section. An absent section is ambiguous (was there nothing to say, or did nobody write it?); an explicit `none` is a documented negative, the same discipline this plan uses everywhere else. `setup.m`'s `Output` says `none` too, and then says what it *does* — modifies the path for this session only, nothing saved.
3. **`config.m`'s header carries the units in the `Output` block, one line per field path.** This is the zero-argument case of the exemplar's `Inputs`/`Output` shape: the struct *is* the output, so its 15 field paths are listed with their units from section 3.1.1's glossary — ppd as pixels per degree, `pupil_diameter` in mm, `wavelength` in nm, `target_mean` in gray levels 0-255, `target_contrast` and `rgb_to_lms` as dimensionless. Two header claims were verified rather than copied: the matrix maps a **row-vector** RGB pixel to `[L M S]` (stated by `vislab.lib.rgb2lms`'s own header), and the optics triple is exactly `vislab.lib.otf_filter`'s last three arguments (read off its signature). That is also what licenses the `See also SETUP, vislab.lib.OTF_FILTER, vislab.lib.RGB2LMS.` — a cross-reference to the two functions that consume these constants, since **nothing in this repo consumes `cfg` at all yet** (grep: the only reader of `config()` anywhere is `tools/golden_harness.m`). The header says so in a `Note`, pointing at S2.4, rather than leaving a reader to wonder why the config looks unused.
4. **Two stale facts in `config.m`'s comments were corrected against disk, not preserved.** The old inline comment described `data/stimuli/` as holding `nat/, brodatz/`; it holds `textures/` and nothing else (checked 2026-09-21), and the header now says that with the date attached. `data/model/` holds exactly the fitted discrimination boundaries, so the header names them instead of saying "boundaries, etc.".
5. **`setup.m`'s two loop counters had to collapse onto one name, across two different local functions.** `locate_folder`'s `for i = 1:numel(candidates)` and `ensure_addon_on_path`'s `for i = 1:numel(hits)` are unrelated loops in unrelated functions, so the obvious edit is `i_candidate` and `i_hit` — and `assert_rename_bijection` rejects it, because the rename map is textual and per *file*, not per function, so `i` cannot appear twice in the range. Both became the neutral `ii`, per rule C-6, with a comment at the first one saying why. This is a new wrinkle on C-6 worth recording: **the bijection constraint crosses local-function boundaries**, so a file with several local functions can be forced into neutral counter names even where no single function is ambiguous. Splitting them is S3.5.
6. **`fetch_commons` → `fetch_vislab`, and `commons` → `vislab_dir`: a local subfunction rename, done in Stage 1 even though the repo-wide rename tranche (0.1.1) has long closed.** A *local* function is invisible outside its file, so renaming it is exactly as safe and exactly as checkable as renaming a variable — `check_stage1` covers it as a bare identifier, and it passed. Nothing else in the repo mentions "commons" (grepped). The word named nothing real: the sibling repo is `vislab-common` and the package is `+vislab`, so "commons" was a third word for a thing that already had two. **One thing this could not fix:** the warning identifier string `'texture_segmentation:setup:noCommons'` still says `noCommons`. It is a string literal, therefore `canon`-visible, therefore out of Stage 1's reach — and it is also the kind of identifier external code may match on. Left exactly as written; worth a glance in Stage 3, not worth an item of its own.
7. **`toolboxes_dir` and `toolboxes_path` are two names for one value, deliberately.** `addons_toolboxes_dir`'s output (old `d`) and `ensure_addon_on_path`'s local holding that output (old `tb_dir`) are the same path, and the bijection rule above means they cannot share a new name. Rather than leave one abbreviated, both were expanded to distinct accurate names — the callee's own output is `toolboxes_dir`, the caller's local is `toolboxes_path`. This is tranche 3's decision C-8 ("a function's name and its output variable are allowed to disagree") applied one level out, to a function and its caller, and it costs nothing because neither name is visible from the other's body.
8. **Continuation lines were re-indented to the 4-space hanging indent even where the original aligned under the delimiter** — tranche 2's decision 6, applied to `setup.m`'s three multi-piece warning strings (13 spaces → 12) and its `candidates` cell array (18 → 8). **One exception, stated as such: a numeric matrix literal keeps its rows aligned under the opening bracket**, because the alignment is what makes a matrix readable as a matrix. `config.m`'s 3x3 `rgb_to_lms` therefore keeps its leading space after `[` (a sign column, so that `-1.096` lines up under `6.984`) and `texture_grouping.m`'s 2x2 `q2` keeps its second row aligned under its first. The `config.m` matrix also gained the missing space after two commas; its sign columns were made consistent across all three rows in the process.
9. **`texture_grouping.m`'s bare `%` separator lines became blank lines.** The file used a content-free `%` line between logical blocks, 12 of them — a motif that appears nowhere else in the repo as a *body* separator (the `%` lines elsewhere are the blank lines inside a header comment block, which is the exemplar's own style and is kept). One blank line between logical blocks is this plan's rule, and it reads as one; `%` on its own line reads as a comment someone forgot to write.
10. **The driver's vocabulary is cluster C's, read per file per rule C-2 — and this file is where `grid_size`/`n_patches` finally land on their defined meanings.** `ni` (patches per scene side, 16) → `grid_size` and `nij = ni^2` (256) → `n_patches`, exactly C-1's definitions; confirmed from the call site, since `thresh(dv, nij, critl)` passes `nij` into the parameter tranche 5 named `n_patches` for precisely this reason. The scene's coarser 4x4 region grid needed a name C-1 does not cover, and took `region_grid_size`. Pixel sizes went to `scene_size` (1024), `image_size` (640, the Brodatz sheet — matching tranche 8's name for the same quantity), `region_size` (256) and `patch_size` (64). `b` → `noise_const`, the fourth file to settle on that name (tranche 2's decision 10, tranche 5's 6, tranche 8's 8). `nlnka`/`lnka`/`nlnkb`/`lnkb`/`nls` → `n_links_a`/`links_a`/`n_links_b`/`links_b`/`n_common`, which are `nlsame.m`'s own parameter and output names — the call now reads `nlsame(links_a, n_links_a, links_b, n_links_b)`, a line that documents itself. Likewise `r`/`shp` → `radius`/`shape` to match `mk_win`'s signature.
11. **`ii`/`jj`/`xx`/`yy` in the driver, for the same C-6 reason, and this file is the worst case of it yet.** `i` serves four distinct roles here (region row, histogram bin index, and the first patch index of a pair in three separate double loops), `j` three, and `x` two (a 2-element vector of log responses in the quadratic, then a patch's pixel row origin in the map-drawing loop). One old name cannot map to two new ones, so all four took neutral names, with a comment at the first use pointing at C-6. Added to S3.5. Everything with a single role got a real name: `row1`/`col1`/`row2`/`col2` for the two patches' grid coordinates, `region_row_lo`/`_hi` and `patch_row_lo`/`_hi` (deliberately different prefixes — the old `ilo`/`klo` pair could not both become `row_lo`), `patch_coords`, `link_ratio`, `group_num`, `group_img`, `patch_block`.
12. **`patch1`/`patch2` for the two patches, per rule B-4.** `ptchi`/`ptchj` hold actual image arrays and are passed straight to `rp(patch1, patch2, ...)` and `Rh(patch1, patch2, edges)`, so cluster B's names are the right ones; a bare `patch` would shadow the Graphics builtin, and cluster C's `patch_index1`/`patch_index2` (rule C-4) mean integers, which these are not.
13. **`q2`/`q1`/`q0` were left exactly as they are.** They are the field names of the quadratic-boundary structs this repo ships (`fabric_bd.mat`, `data/model/texture discrimination boundaries.mat`) and the shape `classify_normals` works in, so they are effectively external vocabulary. Renaming them would make the driver disagree with every artifact it could load.

**Header-doc corrections made along the way.** `config.m` already had the repo's best prose header and was extended, not rewritten: the summary line, the "EDIT this if..." note and the sibling-repo note all survive, with a signature line, `Inputs`/`Output`, two `Note`s and a `See also` added around them. `setup.m` was treated the same way — its detailed prose about what `+lib` contains and which add-ons are required is unchanged; what is new is the signature line, `Inputs`/`Output`, the `Note` on the data store (below) and `See also CONFIG`. `texture_grouping.m` had **no header at all** beyond `% texture_grouping.m` — a filename and nothing else — and got the full script shape per tranche 8's decision 1: summary, `run('texture_grouping.m')` (**not** a package-qualified invocation; it is a script, and `general.<name>`-style dispatch does not exist for scripts), what it does, an explicit statement that it is a script, an "Expects, in the current folder or on the path" section for the Brodatz sheets, `Workspace outputs` with sizes and units, two `Note`s (the dangling `Rh`, and the seed/quadratic constants) and a `See also`.

**One header claim worth flagging, because it reverses the sign convention the rest of this cluster uses.** Every other decision variable in the cluster is documented "larger means more different" (`rp`, `re`, `rs_new`). This script's combined `dv` is the opposite: `thresh(dv, n_patches, link_criterion)` keeps pairs **above** the criterion as links, links are what build groups, and `thresh.m` forces the diagonal to link unconditionally — a patch is always in its own group. Since `dv`'s diagonal is left at 0, well below the criterion of 15, the forcing is not decorative: it is there precisely because 0 is a *low* score and a patch versus itself must still link. So on this script's scale, larger means more evidence the two patches share a texture. Stated in the header with that reasoning, since it is the sort of thing a reader will otherwise get backwards.

**The data-path question this tranche was sent to settle: tranche 8's finding is confirmed, and sharpened.** `setup.m` was read line by line and then probed empirically (R2024b, a read-only `exist` check in a throwaway `-batch` process — `setup.m` itself was **not** run, per this tranche's scope).

- `setup.m` puts exactly **two** folders on the path: `fileparts(vislab_dir)`, i.e. `../vislab-common`, as the parent of the `+vislab` package, and `repo_root`. That is all. The shared data store is only `isfolder`-tested and warned about; it is never added.
- `addpath` is not recursive, so having `vislab-common` on the path does not make `vislab-common/data` reachable, and MATLAB's partial-pathname lookup needs the folder *containing* the file to be on the path. Measured, with cwd at the repo root and both of `setup.m`'s folders added: `exist('vislab-common/data/cps_rgb2lms.mat', 'file')` → `0`, `exist('data/cps_rgb2lms.mat', 'file')` → `0`, `exist('cps_rgb2lms.mat', 'file')` → `0`. Adding `../vislab-common/data` explicitly turns the last two into `2`. `exist('B1.gif', 'file')` stays `0` even then, since the Brodatz sheets are one level further down, in `data/textures/brodatz`.
- **One refinement to B2.14's wording, same id, no renumbering.** "No cwd makes the bare form resolve" is very slightly too strong: `'vislab-common/data/...'` *would* resolve against the working directory if cwd were the `Geisler Lab` parent folder. But the same script also reads `'data/model/...'` and `'exp_files/...'`, which need cwd to be the repo root, so the two requirements are mutually exclusive — no single working directory makes that file's path set resolve. The corrected sentence is in B2.14. The bug is unchanged in substance and in tier, and fault (b) (the filename itself is wrong) makes it unfixable by `cd` regardless.
- `setup.m`'s header now carries this as a `Note`, so the next reader learns what setup does *not* do from setup itself rather than from this document.

**Commented-out code.** Two blocks, both in `texture_grouping.m`, both triaged in section 3.1.4: the `% image_num = randi(n_images);` random-texture-selection line (**alternative**, and the only thing in the file that reads the otherwise-dead `n_images = 60`) and the file's trailing `% figure; ... image(rthres*255); ...` display line (**scaffolding**; renames not propagated, since `rthres` exists nowhere in the file — it is a stale caller-side spelling of `thresh`'s `rthresh` output, which the live code assigns to `links`). **`config.m` and `setup.m` carry no commented-out code at all** — checked line by line while reading, a documented negative. The survey had located no blocks in any of these three files, so both rows are new. Nothing was deleted or converted. While here, section 3.1.4's one remaining `not yet triaged` cell (the survey's approximate `simulate_discrimination.m:16-18,307-317` row) was marked as superseded by the three real rows tranche 8 wrote for those exact blocks — a bookkeeping fix, no verdict changed.

**Bugs — none new, which for these three files is a real negative and not a thin read.** `config.m` is 30 lines of assignments and was checked against disk (every path it builds exists, and `config()`'s output is byte-identical before and after). `setup.m`'s four local functions were each traced: the `locate_folder` fallback order, the clone's `status == 0 && isfolder(.../+lib)` success test, the add-on probe-then-`genpath` self-heal, and the `settings()`-then-`which` two-route directory lookup all do what their comments say, with the one gap above — which is a documented behaviour, not a defect in the code as written, and is already B2.14's. `texture_grouping.m`'s two known items were re-read and both hold: **B2.2** — the bare `Rh(...)` call is still there, untouched, and `Rh` still resolves to nothing (the other half of B2.2, the bare `Rp`, has resolved to `rp.m` since tranche 1 and the call now matches `rp`'s five-argument signature exactly); and the driver's `imread` of a bare `B<n>.gif`, which is **not** filed as a new bug but appended to **S2.4** as its third path spelling, following tranche 8's treatment of the identical `img_data/brodatz/` case — with the right working directory it does run, and the fix is S2.4's for all three sites at once.

**Optimizations — two new, neither made.** 🟡 **O3.7** (`clear all`, the repo's only `CLALL`, left untouched by instruction: dropping `all` is a behaviour change, not a style one) and 🟡 **O3.8** (the response loop computes both decision variables for all 65536 *ordered* pairs including self-pairs, where both measures look symmetric in their two patches and the diagonal is discarded — potentially close to 2x on the script's dominant loop, but the symmetry has not been proved against `rp`'s windowing and nothing has been timed). **One correction to an existing item:** 🔴 **O1.3** listed `texture_grouping.m:30` among the sites that re-`imread` the same Brodatz files every trial. That is wrong and has been struck — the loop advances a running counter and reads 16 *distinct* sheets exactly once each, so there is no repetition to hoist here. The item's other six sites are unaffected.

**Structural items — four new.** 🟠 **S2.9** (`config.m`'s `rgb_to_lms` is element-for-element identical to the `lms` variable in the shared `cps_rgb2lms.mat`, which `vislab.lib.rgb2lms` loads and whose header explicitly tells callers not to load it themselves — two copies of one lab-global calibration, verified by `isequal`), 🟡 **S3.6** (`cfg.paths.data` vs `cfg.paths.data_root` name two unrelated folders and the shared suffix implies one contains the other), 🟡 **S3.7** (four seeding conventions, none routed through `cfg.seed`, including this driver's live `rng(2)` — which no section 3.1.4 row owns, since it is live code), 🟡 **S3.8** (the driver hardcodes a 2-cue quadratic boundary of exactly `fabric_bd.mat`'s shape but with different values, from an unrecorded fit). S2.4's premise was re-confirmed and its site list corrected as above; S2.5's script-to-function premise holds for `texture_grouping.m` too, though the item's file list does not name it (it enumerates `+grouping`/`+general` scripts) — this driver is the user-facing one and is arguably meant to stay a script, so it is deliberately **not** added to S2.5.

**Deliberately left alone.** The `clear all` (O3.7) and the bare `Rh` call (B2.2), both by instruction. `config.m`'s field *paths*, which are zone 2 and dot-qualified — S3.6 records the one worth renaming. The `'texture_segmentation:setup:noCommons'` warning identifier, a string literal (decision 6). `texture_grouping.m`'s `n_images = 60`, live but read only by a commented-out line: deleting an assignment is Stage 2's fourth pattern, and the triage row ties its fate to that block's. The `nimg`-adjacent `first_image_num = 1` (old `mnk`), which is genuinely used. `q2`/`q1`/`q0` (decision 13). Every `save`/`load` string literal and zone-3 frozen name: **none of these three files contains one** — no `save` call, no `load` call, no frozen variable, a documented negative. (The word "load" does appear twice in `texture_grouping.m`, both times as an English verb in a section comment — left alone, per step 1's decision 3.) The README's stale layout section (`edgecode/`, `+stats/`, `CLEANUP.md`), which section 2 suggested this tranche might absorb: **not done**, because `README.md` is outside this tranche's three-file scope and the standing rule is that nothing outside the file list may be edited. It remains a standalone doc fix, and is the one thing section 2 flagged for Stage 1 that Stage 1 did not do.

**`Contents.m`.** Not applicable — these are root-level loose files, not a package, the same as tranche 5.

**Baseline note.** The working tree is clean again after this step's commit, and no path changed. **Stage 1 is complete: all of 0.1.0 through 0.1.9 are ticked**, `check_stage1` has passed on every in-scope file in the tranche that touched it, and section 0.6.1 will re-run it repo-wide at the close.

</div>
