<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h3 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:1.05em">3.2 · Stage 2 · Tidying</h3></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

**Coverage gaps, resolved where the data question turned out to be the blocker, still open where a code problem is:**

- All of `+experiment` (tranches 6-7) requires Psychtoolbox and a live display — no headless golden harness is possible; every file in these tranches gets pattern-check-only verification for Stage 2. Unchanged by the rewrite.
- `+general` (tranche 8): the data (natural images, Brodatz) **is** present and reachable via `cfg.paths.*` since S2.4 — not the blocker anymore. The remaining blockers are B2.12 (unresolvable legacy functions), B2.13 (two `.mat` files absent from the store), and S2.5 (script, not function — no entry point exists to call). Pattern-check-only until those are settled; revisit once they are.
- `+lib/texture_patch.m` and the root decision-variable functions `re.m`/`rs.m`/`rp.m` — **coverage extended** (0.2.0, done 2026-09-21): these only ever needed the small, git-tracked Brodatz set, which is present, and `run_demo.m` has proven the calling pattern works end to end. Golden-tested directly with a real patch and an explicit seed, never through `texture_grouping.m`.
- `+lib/edge_props_stim.m` and `rs_new.m` — **covered by the identity of the error each raises, not by an output checksum**, because neither has a runnable path: `edge_props_stim` dies at `imshow` on the `'tex'` path (B2.15) and at a bad subscript on the `'camo'` default (B3.6), and `rs_new` dies inside `rp` on a missing argument (B3.13). That pins *where* each one fails, so a Stage 2 edit cannot move it; their numbers stay unverified until Stage 4.
- `edge_dv.m`, `contour_blur_estimation.m`, `texture_grouping.m` — pattern-check-only. The open question about the first two was **closed 2026-09-21 while doing 0.2.0**, ahead of tranche 5: `edge_dv.m` is not a usable entry point at all (its first executable statement calls the nonexistent `lib.edge_contour_props`, B2.1), and `contour_blur_estimation.m` has no `function` line, takes no input, reads no Brodatz data and only draws an annulus into a figure. Neither was added. The third is the full driver script, excluded on principle regardless of what it calls.
- `setup.m` — pattern-check-only, path-modifying script, unchanged.
- Everything else — `config()`, and the pure `+grouping`/`+lib` functions listed in section 1's harness-coverage note — gets both pattern-check and golden-output verification, unchanged.

**Per-tranche work log:**

<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h5 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:0.92em">0.2.0 — extending the golden harness to real Brodatz patches</h5></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

Run 2026-09-21, **MATLAB R2024b**. Only `tools/golden_harness.m` and `tools/golden_ref.mat` were touched; no repo source file was edited in this step. Six new entry points, 20 new reference fields, all reached through `cfg.paths.textures` — two Brodatz sheets, `B1.gif` and `B2.gif`, 640x640 each — and nothing else on disk.

**What was added, and what each one is reduced to:**

| entry point | how it is called | reduced to |
|---|---|---|
| `lib.texture_patch` | `('tex_num', 3, 'patch_sz', [64 64], 'seed', 1, 'cont', 0.12)` — fully explicit, and a second call taking the `'rand'` defaults for both texture number and seed under `rng(cfg.seed)` | plain, position-weighted and squared checksums, size, plus the returned `seed`/`tex_num`/`coords` |
| `rp` | two gray-level 64x64 crops + `mk_win(64, 16, 1)`, `noise_const = 10` | the scalar, for a different-sheet pair and a same-sheet pair |
| `rs` | same two pairs, `rs(patch, patch, 64)` | the scalar, both pairs |
| `re` | same two pairs, `re(patch, patch, 64, 2, 8, 50)`, gated on the Image Processing Toolbox | the scalar, both pairs |
| `rs_new` | `rs_new(patch_a1, patch_b1, 64, 8)` | **the identifier of the error it raises** (`MATLAB:minrhs`, B3.13) |
| `lib.edge_props_stim` | exactly as `+general/simulate_discrimination.m:171-174` calls it — `cat(3, patch_a, patch_b)`, `'stim_type', 'tex'`, `'pad_val', 128`, the three bin matrices from `nat_im_eff_coding.mat` — and once more on the `'camo'` default | **the identifier of the error each path raises** (`images:imageDisplayValidateParams:unsupportedDimension`, B2.15; `MATLAB:badsubscript`, B3.6) |

**Captured values** (R2024b, `cfg.seed = 0`): `texture_patch_checksum` 2047.99999999997, `texture_patch_weighted_checksum` 4210157.0833194, `texture_patch_sq_checksum` 1038.742, `size` [64 64], `seed` 1, `tex_num` 3, `coords` [241 415]; `texture_patch_rand_weighted_checksum` 256906.528893091, `rand_seed` 1749605794, `rand_tex_num` 21, `rand_coords` [503 417]; `rp_diff` 0.00106532403629094, `rp_same` 0.000366094805718233; `rs_diff` 5.84403479782818, `rs_same` 1.68854133686158; `re_diff` 25.5909709687289, `re_same` 11.2846101098096; `rs_new_error_id` `'MATLAB:minrhs'`; `edge_props_stim_tex_error_id` `'images:imageDisplayValidateParams:unsupportedDimension'`; `edge_props_stim_camo_error_id` `'MATLAB:badsubscript'`.

**Five decisions, each of which binds any later harness extension:**

- **H-1. A function with no runnable path is covered by the identity of the error it raises, not skipped.** `edge_props_stim` (both paths) and `rs_new` all die before assigning an output. Recording `err.identifier` pins *where* each one fails, so a Stage 2 edit to those files cannot silently move the failure — which is precisely what Stage 2's runtime check is for. The `error_id` helper also records `''` if the call unexpectedly *succeeds*, so a later fix makes replay fail loudly rather than pass quietly. This is not output coverage and is not claimed as such: the numbers those functions would return stay unverified until Stage 4.
- **H-2. Where a plain checksum is fixed by construction, add a position-weighted one.** `texture_patch` imposes its own mean (`lum`) and SD (`cont`) on the patch it returns, so `sum(patch, 'all')` is 2048 for *any* 64x64 patch at `lum = 0.5` — it would pass whatever pixels came back. The reduction therefore also carries `sum(patch .* reshape(1:numel(patch), size(patch)), 'all')`, which no reshuffle, wrong crop or wrong sheet survives. Use this wherever an entry point normalizes its own output.
- **H-3. Real-data patches are cut at fixed offsets, in raw gray levels, for everything except `texture_patch` itself.** `re`/`rs`/`rp`/`edge_props_stim` are all documented as taking gray levels, and the efficient-coding histogram bins `edge_props_stim` needs were fitted on them, so the harness reads the sheet with `imread` and slices `(1:64, 1:64)` / `(301:364, 201:264)` — deterministic, no RNG draw, and the same shape of input `simulate_discrimination.m` builds. `texture_patch`'s own normalized `[0, 1]` output is only ever used to test `texture_patch`. One sheet gives the same-texture pair and a second sheet the different-texture pair, so each decision variable is exercised on both conditions rather than one arbitrary call.
- **H-4. Parameters are chosen so the call cannot trip a bug that is already filed.** `re`'s gradient-magnitude threshold is 50 on purpose: at 20 these patches give patch 1 more contours than patch 2, and `re.m`'s patch-2 loop runs to *patch 1's* contour count (B1.2), so the call dies on an out-of-range index. Verified by trying both. Same precaution the existing `find_bin` sweep takes around B3.2 — the harness records current behaviour, so it must not be sized to sit on top of a known crash.
- **H-5. The OTF prefilter is deliberately not applied.** `simulate_discrimination.m` runs each sheet through `vislab.lib.otf_filter` before cropping. The harness skips that: it would add a cross-repo dependency to every replay without changing what is being pinned, since the entry point under test is `edge_props_stim`, not the filter. Stated here so a later reader does not "fix" the discrepancy.

**Verification.** A dry run was done first, into a scratch `.mat`, and every pre-existing reference field compared field-by-field against the committed `tools/golden_ref.mat` — all unchanged, so the extension adds coverage without disturbing any. Then `golden_harness('capture', 'tools/golden_ref.mat')` was run once (the specific authorization for this step), followed immediately by two consecutive replays to confirm the new entries are deterministic and not merely self-consistent:

```
captured
replay 1 OK
replay 2 OK
```

**Two questions closed while here.** `edge_dv.m` and `contour_blur_estimation.m` were read in full to answer 0.2.4's retroactive question: neither is a viable entry point. `edge_dv.m`'s first executable statement is `lib.edge_contour_props(patch_a)`, and that function exists nowhere in `+lib` (B2.1), so the function cannot run at all, on Brodatz data or anything else — it is not even an error-identity candidate worth recording, since the error is a missing dependency rather than anything Stage 2 could move. `contour_blur_estimation.m` has no `function` line, takes no input, reads no data, and only renders a hardcoded 64x64 annulus into a figure after a `close all`; there is nothing to check a checksum against. Both stay pattern-check-only, permanently, not "not checked yet".

**One bug found and not fixed: B2.15** (`imshow` on a 3-plane non-RGB array kills every `'tex'` call). Filed in section 0.4. It was found by this step rather than by tranche 2's Stage 1 read because it only shows up when the function is actually called.

</div>

</details>

<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h5 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:0.92em">0.2.1 — <code>+lib</code></h5></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

Run 2026-09-21, **MATLAB R2024b**, immediately after the harness extension above. Baseline: HEAD (working tree clean for these files at the start). Scope: the 14 code files in `+lib/` plus `Contents.m`, which is a documentation listing and carries none of the four patterns. Golden-covered: `steerable_filter`, `steerable_grad`, `local_sd`, `texture_patch` by output checksum, and `edge_props_stim` by error identity (0.2.0).

**Pattern 1 — missing suppressing semicolons. Found and fixed: 17 lines in two files.** `texture_patch.m:47,48,77` (`rng('default')`, `rng(seed)`, `warning(...)`) and `edge_props_stim.m:214,215` and `:252-255,283-286,314-317` (the `figure`/`imshow`/`subplot`/`bar`/`title` calls in the plotting block). None of these actually prints anything — each is a call with no requested output, so there is no `ans` to display — but `tree2str` records the display/suppress bit either way, which is exactly why Stage 1 could not add them and Stage 2 can: the canonical diff is the whitelisted `X` → `X;` and nothing else. `git diff` on both files is 17 lines changed by one character each, and no new `checkcode` message appears (a `VUNUS` would have, had any of these produced a value).

**One missing semicolon deliberately NOT added, and this is a decision later tranches should follow: `edge_props_stim.m:89`'s bare `randi(10)`.** Section 1 flagged it in advance as an accidental debug print, and it is — but it is also filed as **B1.1**, a 🔴 bug, precisely because the draw it makes shifts the RNG stream for the rest of any run that calls it, and the fix recorded there is to delete the line, not to quiet it. Adding a `;` would remove the one visible symptom (the `ans = 9` this tranche watched it print on every harness call) while leaving the actual defect in place, making the bug strictly harder to notice before Stage 4 gets to it. **Rule: where a missing semicolon is itself part of a filed bug whose fix is deletion, leave it and cite the bug id.** The general form of this rule — a Stage 2 pattern hit that overlaps a filed bug belongs to the bug, not to Stage 2 — is applied three more times below.

**Pattern 2 — `find(...)` that could be logical indexing. None found.** `find` does not appear anywhere in `+lib` — grep over all 14 files returns no match at all, so there was nothing to judge.

**Pattern 3 — unpreallocated growth. None found.** No `end+1` anywhere in `+lib`, and `checkcode` reports no `AGROW`. Every array in these files is either preallocated to its final size or assigned whole.

**Pattern 4 — unused assignments `checkcode` flags. Eight hits; five deleted, three left with reasons.**

- **Deleted:** `downsample.m:43-47`, the five locals `ppd`/`pd`/`w`/`apply_filter`/`ncolr` pulled out of `parser.Results` and never read — the function's body is six lines and uses none of them. The `addParameter` declarations stay, so the documented call-signature compatibility with `downsample_old` (which does use all five) is untouched, and so is the header paragraph describing them. Verified beyond the pattern check, since `downsample` has no harness entry: HEAD's version was copied out under a temporary name and both were run on the same random 64x64 image at every supported `down_level` with `'filter', 1, 'ncolr', 1` — `isequal` true at 1, 2, 4, 8 and 16.
- **Left — `edge_props_stim.m:194`, `grads_pca`.** This is **B3.5** itself: the live PCA branch computes `grads_pca` and never assigns it back to `grads`, unlike both commented-out variants beside it which end in `grads = grads_pca`. The assignment is not dead code, it is a bug's missing second half. Deleting it would delete the evidence.
- **Left — `edge_props_stim.m:81`, `bd_strip_rep`.** Read only by the commented-out old gradient loop (`grad_bd(~bd_strip_rep) = nan`), whose Stage 1 triage verdict is *alternative* (section 3.1.4) — i.e. Stage 3 is to convert it, not drop it. Deleting the variable now would quietly break the block Stage 3 has been told to revive, and B3.6 names that same block as the fix for the `'camo'` path.
- **Left — `edge_props_stim.m:109`, `grad_hist_thresh`.** Same shape: it is a documented name-value input whose only reader is the commented-out line `% grad_mag(grad_mag<grad_hist_thresh)=0;` at `:221`. The parameter is documented in the header as parsed but currently unused, so the local mirrors a deliberate state of affairs, not an oversight.

**Golden-harness replay after the edits, verbatim:**

```
REPLAY PASS
```

(`golden_harness('replay', 'tools/golden_ref.mat')` returned without throwing; the `ans = 9` lines and the docked-figure warning in the console around it are B1.1 and the plotting block doing their usual thing inside `edge_props_stim`, not harness output.) This is the first tranche where replay covered files the tranche actually edited — `texture_patch` by four checksums and `edge_props_stim` by its two error identifiers — so the semicolon edits are verified at runtime, not only by pattern.

**`checkcode` before → after, whole package:** `compute_p_clipped` LOGSUM x2 → unchanged (not one of the four patterns — a readability suggestion about `sum` on a logical, which is a Stage 3 question at most); `downsample` NASGU x5 → **clean**; `edge_props_stim` STOUT x15 + NASGU x3 → unchanged (STOUT is B2.4, NASGU as discussed); all 11 other files clean before and after. No message was introduced.

**Nothing else touched.** No structural or speed findings beyond what sections 0.3/0.5 already record for these files (S2.2, S2.3, S2.7, S3.2, O1.1, O1.4, O2.1, O3.5 all sit in `+lib` and were left alone). One new bug came out of this tranche's half of the work, B2.15, but it was found by the harness extension calling the function rather than by the pattern check — it is logged with 0.2.0 above.

</div>

</details>

<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h5 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:0.92em">0.2.2 — <code>+grouping</code> pure helpers</h5></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

Run 2026-09-21, **MATLAB R2024b**. Baseline: HEAD. Scope: the 12 files that were tranche 3's Stage 1 boundary — `mk_dist.m`, `mk_decc.m`, `mk_mecc.m`, `mk_bindex.m`, `mk_masks.m`, `mk_texs.m`, `find_bin.m`, `find_xy.m`, `check_xy.m`, `check_tlst.m`, `effective_distance.m`, `find_tex_regions.m`. Eight of the twelve are golden-covered by the original harness; `mk_texs`, `find_xy`, `effective_distance` and `find_tex_regions` are not (the last two are scripts).

**Outcome: a complete documented negative — nothing was changed in any of the twelve files.** All four patterns were checked file by file:

**Pattern 1 — missing suppressing semicolons. None found.** Every non-comment, non-continuation line in all twelve files already ends in `;` — checked mechanically (a scan for statements ending in neither `;`, `,` nor `...`, discounting block keywords) and confirmed by reading each file. Worth recording because two of the twelve, `effective_distance.m` and `find_tex_regions.m`, *are* scripts, which is where the skill warns a bare display line is most likely to be deliberate: neither has one, so the "ask before fixing" case never arose here.

**Pattern 2 — `find(...)` that could be logical indexing. None found.** `find` does not appear in any of the twelve files. (`find_bin`, `find_xy` and `find_tex_regions` are named for what they look up, not for the builtin.)

**Pattern 3 — unpreallocated growth. None found.** No `end+1` in any of the twelve, and no `AGROW` from `checkcode`. Two related things were looked at and are deliberately *not* targets: `mk_masks.m:39`'s `masks` third dimension is preallocated wrong, but that is **B3.3**, a sizing bug, not unpreallocated growth; and `mk_masks.m:44`'s `patch_list` is preallocated to a guessed width, `round(3*grid_size^2/n_regions) + 1`, which MATLAB would silently grow past if a region ever exceeded it. Growth-by-implicit-resize inside a `while` is real, but it is not the `x(end+1)` form this pattern covers, and fixing it needs a size that is only knowable after the fact — that makes it a Speed/Structure question, and it is already inside O3.3's `mk_masks.m:75-88` entry, so no new item is filed.

**Pattern 4 — unused assignments `checkcode` flags. One hit, left in place.** `find_bin.m:67`'s `err = 1;` inside `if i_dist == 0`. It is genuinely never read — `err` appears nowhere else in the file and is not an output — so by the letter of the pattern it is deletable. It is **not** deleted, for the same reason `response_interval.m`'s `response = -1;` was not deleted in tranche 6/7 and `edge_props_stim.m`'s `grads_pca` was not deleted in 0.2.1: it is already half of a filed bug. **B3.2** names this exact line — the `i_dist == 0` branch sets a dead flag and then falls through to `bin_index(0, ...)`, which errors on the zero index — and its fix is to make that branch actually report the bin-lookup failure, not to remove the only trace that the author meant to handle it. Deleting the assignment would leave a branch with an empty body, which is a worse thing to hand Stage 4 than what is there now.

Two further genuinely-unused assignments exist in the scripts and are **outside** this pattern because `checkcode` does not flag them: `effective_distance.m:24`'s `dist_max` (already recorded as part of B3.9's item (d)) and `find_tex_regions.m:23`'s `image_width`. `checkcode` suppresses `NASGU` in scripts, since a script's variables can legitimately be read by whatever runs after it. Recorded here so the gap is visible rather than silently inherited; neither is touched, and both files' fate is S3.3's question anyway.

**Golden-harness replay, verbatim:**

```
REPLAY PASS
```

Run after 0.2.1's edits, covering `mk_dist`, `mk_mecc`, `mk_decc`, `mk_bindex`, `mk_masks`, `find_bin`, `check_xy` and `check_tlst` — unchanged, as expected for a tranche that changed nothing.

<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h5 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:0.92em">0.2.3/0.2.4 — <code>+grouping</code> stimulus scripts + root cluster-C files</h5></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

Run 2026-09-21, **MATLAB R2024b**. Baseline: HEAD, working tree confirmed clean with `git status` before starting. Scope: the 9 files that were tranche 4's Stage 1 boundary — `mk_texseg_session.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_points.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m`, `mk_trl_points.m`, `tex_regions.m`, `s_gtr_img.m`, `demo_mk_trl_points.m`, `shape_cue.m` (per this tranche's dispatch, pattern-check-only — most are scripts (S2.5) and several are known-broken, B2.5-B2.7/B3.11) — plus the 8 files that were tranche 5's boundary — `re.m`, `rs.m`, `rs_new.m`, `rp.m`, `thresh.m`, `nlsame.m`, `mk_contour.m`, `mk_win.m`. `re`/`rs`/`rs_new`/`rp` are golden-covered as of 0.2.0 (the latter by error identity, B3.13); `thresh`/`nlsame`/`mk_win`/`mk_contour` were already golden-covered since Stage 1.

**Pattern 1 — missing suppressing semicolons. Found and fixed: 18 lines in three files, all display-only calls with no return value.** `mk_texseg_stim_points.m:227,229,278,280,292,294`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape.m:78,80,141,143,158,160`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m:226,228,276,278,294,296` — a bare `pause` and a bare `set(gcf, 'position', [x0, y0, width, height])` once per trial-display block, three blocks per file. Neither call produces a value, so nothing is actually printed either way — same class as tranche 2's `rng(...)`/`warning(...)` fix in `edge_props_stim.m`: `tree2str` still records the display/suppress bit, so this is Stage 2's whitelisted `X` → `X;` regardless of visible effect. Confirmed by a mechanical scan (every line ending in a bare identifier or `)` with no trailing `;`, `,` or `...`, discounting control-flow headers and continuations) over all 17 files, cross-checked against `checkcode` before and after: none of these 18 lines showed up as a `checkcode` message (there is no value to flag as unset), matching the precedent that this class of hit is found by reading, not by `checkcode`. No other candidate exists in any of the other 14 files — a documented negative, and worth recording explicitly because 6 of these 17 files are scripts, exactly the case the skill flags as the likeliest place for a *deliberate* unsuppressed display; none of the 18 hits found here prints anything at all, so the "ask before fixing" case never arose.

**Pattern 2 — `find(...)` that could be logical indexing. None found.** `find` does not appear in any of the 17 files (grep over all of them returns no match) — the two names in this file set that sound like the builtin, `find_bin`/`find_xy`/`find_tex_regions`/`s_gtr_img`, are named for what they look up, not for the builtin, same as tranche 3's precedent.

**Pattern 3 — unpreallocated growth. None found.** No `end+1`/`end + 1` anywhere in the 17 files, and `checkcode` reports no `AGROW`. Every array in this file set is either preallocated to its final size or assigned whole.

**Pattern 4 — unused assignments `checkcode` flags. Four hits, all genuinely unused and all fixed; none overlapped a filed bug.**

- **`mk_texseg_session.m:186`, `n_pixels`** (`NASGU`). `n_pixels = image_width*image_width;` computed just before the contrast-assignment loop and never read again; `image_width` (the line above it) turned out to have no other reader either, so both lines were deleted together. Grepped the whole file for both names first to confirm neither is read anywhere else. Not a symptom of any filed bug — nothing in section 0.4 names this line.
- **`s_gtr_img.m:24`, `image_width`** (`NASGU`). Same shape: `image_width = patch_width*grid_size;` is never read in this function's body (confirmed by grep). Deleting it exposed a second-order effect worth recording carefully: `patch_width` was previously used *only* to build this dead `image_width`, so removing the line made `patch_width` itself unused, and `checkcode` picked that up as a new `INUSD` (input-argument-unused) message. That is not this pattern's business to fix — pattern 4 covers local-variable assignments (`NASGU`), not function inputs, and turning `patch_width` into `~` would be a signature change, Stage 3 territory. Instead, the header's `Inputs` block and its opening paragraph were updated to note `patch_width` as unused, the same way `patch_img` and `n_channels` were already documented — a documentation fix, not a behavior change. `s_gtr_img` has no caller anywhere in the repo (B3.11), so nothing downstream reads any of these inputs regardless.
- **`re.m:89,128`, `links`** (`ASGLU`, "value assigned to variable might be unused, consider replacing with ~"). Both patch-1 and patch-2 contour blocks call `[contour_pts, links, n_contour_pts] = mk_contour(...)`, but `links` — MK_CONTOUR's zeroed-out adjacency map — is never read afterward in either block (confirmed by grep for `links` in the whole file: only these two assignment sites). Replaced with `[contour_pts, ~, n_contour_pts] = ...` at both sites, per `checkcode`'s own suggested fix for `ASGLU`. Checked against section 0.4 first: B1.2 and B3.12 are this file's two filed bugs, and neither touches `links` or these two lines, so there is no overlap to protect.
- **`rs.m:34,38`, `dxmx`/`dymx`/`p1s`** (`NASGU` on line 38's `p1s`). The post-loop line `p1s = circshift(p1, [dxmx, dymx]);` recomputes the best-aligned shift of `p1` and never uses the result — `dv` (already holding the winning `sm` from inside the loop) is the only thing returned, at the very next line. Deleting just that one line would have left `dxmx`/`dymx` (assigned at line 34, `dxmx = dx; dymx = dy;`) with no remaining reader either, so both were removed together as one unit, the same "cluster of interrelated dead code" call tranche 2 made for `downsample.m`'s five dead locals. Verified by rereading the whole function: `dv`'s value is unaffected, since the comparison that sets it (`if sm > dv, dv = sm; ...`) is untouched. No filed bug names this line.

**`checkcode` before → after, whole file set (ids):** `mk_texseg_session.m` NASGU → clean; `s_gtr_img.m` STOUT + 2×INUSD + NASGU → STOUT + 3×INUSD (the new `patch_width` INUSD is the documented side effect above, not a new defect — `patch_img`/`n_channels` were already INUSD and undocumented-fix-worthy for the same reason, B3.11's uncalled-function territory); `re.m` 2×ASGLU → clean; `rs.m` NASGU → clean; the other 13 files clean before and after. No new message appeared that isn't accounted for above.

No `golden_harness('capture', ...)` was run. None of the 9 `+grouping` stimulus-script files are golden-covered (per 0.2.3's own pattern-check-only status) — the replay's job for those 9 is only to confirm this tranche's edits elsewhere didn't disturb anything the harness does cover, which they didn't.

<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h5 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:0.92em">0.2.5/0.2.6 — <code>+experiment/+run</code> + <code>+experiment/+prep</code></h5></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

Run 2026-09-21, **MATLAB R2024b**. Baseline: HEAD, working tree confirmed clean with `git status` before starting. Scope: all 23 files under `+experiment` — the 9+9 files in `+experiment/+discriminate/+run` and `+experiment/+grouping/+run` (tranche 6 boundary), and the 3+2 files in `+experiment/+discriminate/+prep` and `+experiment/+grouping/+prep` (tranche 7 boundary); the 4 `Contents.m` files are documentation listings, not code, and carry none of the four patterns, so they were read but not separately checked. Pattern-check-only per section 0.2's Status line and section 1's coverage note — Psychtoolbox/a live display, not data, is the blocker, and no golden-harness entry point exists or is being added for these files.

**`checkcode` over all 23 files, verbatim (ids), matching tranches 6/7's recorded baseline exactly, message for message, before this tranche's one edit:**

```
display_level_start.m, fixation_interval.m, give_feedback.m, load_stimuli.m, run_experiment.m  (clean, both trees)
load_current_session.m (both trees): LOAD, USENS, LOAD
response_interval.m (both trees): STOUT (rt, B3.16), NASGU (response = -1;)
save_current_level.m (both trees): LOAD, NODEF (false positive, tranche 6 log)
stimulus_interval.m (both trees): ASGLU (stimulus_onset_time, tranche 6 log)
generate_stimuli.m, setup_subject.m (both discriminate/grouping): clean
discriminate/+prep/setup_experiment.m: clean
grouping/+prep/setup_experiment.m: NOPRT (line 84, `[i_session i_trial_flat]`)
```

**Pattern 1 — missing suppressing semicolons.** One hit, and it is the one this tranche's dispatch named in advance: `+experiment/+grouping/+prep/setup_experiment.m:84`'s `[i_session i_trial_flat]` inside the `i_trial_flat` loop. Section 3.1's tranche-7 findings-log entry (Step 7, "Deliberately left alone") explicitly deferred this to "Stage 2 territory, not fixed here" rather than filing it as a bug — so, per this tranche's instructions, it was fixed here rather than left again. Added the suppressing `;`. Re-running `checkcode` afterward shows the `NOPRT` is gone, replaced by a `VUNUS` ("`[` produces a value that might be unused") — the expected residual lint signature of exactly this whitelisted pattern (`tree2str`'s canonical diff for a semicolon insertion is `X` → `X;`; the statement itself is unchanged, only its display/suppress bit flips). `git diff` on the file is exactly that one character. No other missing-semicolon candidate exists in any of the other 22 files — a documented negative, confirmed both by the `checkcode` sweep above (no other file reports a display-related message) and by rereading every file's non-comment lines directly.

**Pattern 2 — `find(...)` that could be logical indexing.** `find` appears in exactly one file (both trees, since it's a near-duplicate pair): `+experiment/+{discriminate,grouping}/+run/load_current_session.m`, three calls. None are targets: `[not_completed_session, not_completed_bin] = find(subject_file.levelCompleted < n_levels)` (lines 43-44/52-53) uses `find`'s two-output subscript form to get row/column pairs into a 2-D array — logical indexing gives values, not subscripts, so this genuinely needs `find`. `s_index = find(min(not_completed_session) == not_completed_session)` (line 51/60) looks convertible at a glance, but `s_index(1)` is then used to index two *other* arrays (`not_completed_bin(s_index(1))`, `not_completed_session(s_index(1))`) — exactly the "indexes a second array" exclusion the pattern check calls out. `current_bin = find(ismember(subject_file.binIndex, condition, 'rows') == 1)` (line 61/70) returns the numeric row index itself, which is stored as `settings_out.currentBin` and used downstream as a bin number, not filtered as a logical mask. None found — a documented negative for the other 22 files, which contain no `find` call at all.

**Pattern 3 — unpreallocated growth.** None found, in any of the 23 files. Grep for `end+1)`/`end+1}` over the whole `+experiment` tree returns no matches, and `checkcode` reports no `AGROW` anywhere in the sweep above. A documented negative.

**Pattern 4 — unused assignments `checkcode` flags.** One real `NASGU` candidate, in both trees of `response_interval.m` (`response = -1;`, line 40 discriminate / 41 grouping) — investigated and **not deleted**. The initializer is provably never read given the current control flow: the `while true` loop immediately below only exits via `break` (after `response` is reassigned to `0` or `1`) or, in the discriminate tree only, an `error(...)` on ESC — there is no timeout branch, so no execution path reaches the function's `end` with `response` still `-1`. But the docstring documents `-1` as the "no response" outcome the function is supposed to be able to return, and reading the code shows that outcome is actually unreachable — the timeout the docstring promises was never wired up. Deleting the assignment would be a mechanically "safe" `NASGU` cleanup, but it would also delete the only surviving trace of a real missing-feature bug, which is not this stage's business to paper over. Filed as **B3.23** in section 0.4 instead, and the line left exactly as it is. The other three `checkcode`-flagged messages in this sweep are not this pattern and were left alone as already accounted for: `save_current_level.m`'s `NODEF` is a known static-analysis false positive (tranche 6 log, section 3.1 Step 6, "One line-number correction..." paragraph) — the variable *is* initialized, by the `load(...)`-without-named-outputs two lines above, which `checkcode` can't see; `stimulus_interval.m`'s `ASGLU` is the already-recorded overwritten `stimulus_onset_time` (tranche 6 log, "checkcode: 16 messages..." paragraph); `response_interval.m`'s `STOUT` on `rt` is B3.16, an unassigned *output*, explicitly excluded from this pattern by this tranche's own dispatch instructions.

**Golden-harness replay: passes, and covers none of this tranche's files, before or after the edit.** `golden_harness('replay', 'tools/golden_ref.mat')` returns the reference struct without throwing (checked once at the end, after the one edit). Per section 1's coverage note, every file under `+experiment` needs Psychtoolbox and a live display, so none of the harness's entry points touch any of these 23 files — the replay's only job here is confirming the tranche's one edit didn't disturb anything the harness *does* cover, which it didn't (nothing outside `+experiment` was touched). No `golden_harness('capture', ...)` was run.

**Verdict per section 0.2.5/0.2.6.** `+experiment/+run` (0.2.5, 18 files): a full documented negative — none of the four patterns found anything to fix. `+experiment/+prep` (0.2.6, 5 files, discounting the 4 `Contents.m`): one genuine fix, the deferred `NOPRT` in `grouping/+prep/setup_experiment.m`; the other 4 files are a documented negative. No new commented-out-code, structural, or speed findings — those were tranches 6/7's job (Stage 1), not this one's, and were not re-scanned.

</div>

<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h5 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:0.92em">0.2.7 — <code>+general</code></h5></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

Run 2026-09-21, **MATLAB R2024b**. Baseline: HEAD, working tree confirmed clean with `git status` before the first edit. Scope: the 3 code files of tranche 8's boundary — `compute_exp_error_mat.m`, `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m`, `simulate_discrimination.m` (`+general/Contents.m` is a documentation listing and carries none of the four patterns). **Pattern-check-only**, and the reason is *not* missing data: since S2.4 every path in these files resolves. The blockers are **B2.12** (`aply_otf`/`rgb2lms`/`dsmp` resolve nowhere), **B2.13** (`cdfs_abr_mo13_mo23_cs33*.mat`), and **S2.5** (all three are scripts with no `function` line, so there is no entry point to call). None of the three was executed end to end, and nothing was added to the golden harness.

**Pattern 1 — missing suppressing semicolons. Found and fixed: 56 lines in two files** (13 in `compute_exp_error_mat.m`, 43 in `simulate_discrimination.m`). `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m` has none — every statement in it already ends in `;`, a documented negative. Two groups:

- **54 graphics calls** — `axis`, `colorbar`, `set(gca, ...)`, `xlabel`/`ylabel`/`zlabel`/`title` (including the command-syntax forms `axis image`, `xlabel 'accuracy'`), `hold on`, `xlim`/`ylim`, `imshow`, the `errorbar`/`plot` continuation tails — plus the five `load(...)` calls with no requested output. None of these prints anything, exactly as in 0.2.1's `+lib` plotting block, but `tree2str` records the display/suppress bit regardless, which is why Stage 1 could not touch them. **What makes these unambiguously accidental rather than deliberate is that they sit interleaved with already-suppressed neighbours in the same block:** `compute_exp_error_mat.m:82-94` reads `figure;` / `h_pcolor = pcolor(...);` / `set(h_pcolor, ...);` / `axis image` / `colormap winter;` / `colorbar`, and `simulate_discrimination.m:430-437` alternates `subplot(1, 2, 1);` with a bare `imshow(patch_a, [])`. The "ask before fixing" case the skill warns about needs a line whose display is the author's only output; here the neighbouring line one row up is already suppressed.
- **The two bare progress displays**, `simulate_discrimination.m:133` (`[i_tex i_tex i_samp]`) and `:185` (`[i_tex j_tex i_samp]`) — the only two lines here that genuinely printed. Section 1 named both in advance (as `:69,114`, pre-tranche-8 numbering) as accidental debug output rather than deliberate interactive output, on the grounds that both sit three loops deep. **Checked against section 0.4 before touching them, per this tranche's instruction: neither is a filed bug**, so 0.2.1's rule ("where a missing semicolon is itself part of a filed bug whose fix is deletion, leave it and cite the bug id") does not apply — that rule covers `+lib/edge_props_stim.m:89`'s `randi(10)` (B1.1) and nothing in these three files. Suppressed, following tranche 0.2.5/0.2.6's handling of the identical `[i_session i_trial_flat]` display. `checkcode` confirms the expected residual signature of exactly this whitelisted edit: `NOPTS` ×2 → `VUNUS` ×2, the statement unchanged and only its display bit flipped.

One consequence worth stating rather than acting on: suppressed, `[i_tex i_tex i_samp]` is now a statement with no effect at all. Deleting it is not one of Stage 2's four patterns (it is not an *assignment*), and neither is replacing it with a real `fprintf` progress line. Left as-is, consistent with what 0.2.5/0.2.6 did to the same shape of line; no new item filed, since the script's whole shape is already S2.5's.

**Pattern 2 — `find(...)` that could be logical indexing. None found.** `find` does not appear as a call anywhere in the three files (`grep -w find` over all six of this tranche's files returns only two English-prose comments in `texture_grouping.m` and one inside a `setup.m` warning string). Nothing to judge.

**Pattern 3 — unpreallocated growth. None found.** No `end+1` in any of the three, and `checkcode` reports no `AGROW`. Tranche 8's Stage 1 read already recorded that every storage array in these files is preallocated at the top; re-confirmed here.

**Pattern 4 — unused assignments `checkcode` flags. One hit, left in place with reasons.** `simulate_discrimination.m:249`, the first `results = classify_normals([pow_dv_same hist_dv_same], ...)`, whose value is overwritten at `:257` with no read in between (lines 250-254 are `axis`/`xlabel`/`ylabel` only). It is genuinely unused, but it is **not deleted**, for three reasons, and this is a decision a later pass should not silently reverse:

1. **The whitelisted form of this pattern is deleting a whole statement, and that is not available here.** `classify_normals` is the call that *draws the figure* those next three lines then label, so the statement has to stay; the only available edit is rewriting `results = f(...)` into `f(...)`, which deletes the assignment node rather than the statement. That changes the `nargout` the callee sees, and `classify_normals` is external code (the sibling `IntClassNorm` repo), out of scope and unverifiable from here.
2. **This file has no golden coverage**, so a change of that kind could not be checked at runtime at all — only by pattern, which is exactly the situation the coverage note says to be conservative in.
3. The assignment is structurally parallel to the five sibling `classify_normals` calls below it that *do* read `results`; the odd one out is that this cue pair's boundary is never extracted, which is a question about the script, not about a dead variable.

Two related `checkcode` non-hits are recorded so the gap is visible: `results` at `:308` and `results_grad` at `:301` are *also* never read, but `checkcode` does not flag them because they are the final values of those names in a script, which a later cell or the caller's workspace could legitimately read (the same script-`NASGU` suppression 0.2.2 recorded for `effective_distance.m`). `compute_exp_error_mat.m`'s one message, `ISCL` at `:56`, is a real AST change and so is none of the four patterns — unchanged, as in tranche 8.

**`checkcode` before → after, all three files:** `compute_exp_error_mat.m` ISCL ×1 → unchanged; `nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m` clean → clean; `simulate_discrimination.m` NOPTS ×2 + NASGU ×1 → VUNUS ×2 + NASGU ×1. No message introduced beyond the expected `NOPTS`→`VUNUS` flip.

REPLAY PASS

Per section 1's coverage note the harness reaches none of these three files, so the replay's job here is only to confirm the edits disturbed nothing it *does* cover — they didn't; nothing outside `+general/` was touched in this half. No `golden_harness('capture', ...)` was run.

**Nothing else touched.** No new bug, structural or speed finding: the four patterns are a closed list and this tranche re-read only what they ask about. Every item already recorded against these files (B1.3, B2.12, B2.13, B3.1, B3.18-B3.21, S2.5, S2.8, S3.2, O2.3, O3.4) was left alone.

Worth recording because `texture_grouping.m` is a script, which is where the skill warns a bare display line is most likely to be deliberate: it has none, so the "ask before fixing" case never arose. `config.m` and `setup.m` are functions and are `checkcode`-clean outright.

**Pattern 2 — `find(...)` that could be logical indexing. None found.** No `find` call in any of the three. The three textual matches are not calls: `texture_grouping.m:168,201` are the section comments `% find links` and `% find groups`, and `setup.m:168` is the word inside a warning message's string literal.

**Pattern 3 — unpreallocated growth. None found.** No `end+1` anywhere in the three files and no `AGROW` from `checkcode`. `setup.m`'s `candidates` cell is a literal built whole; `texture_grouping.m` preallocates `dv`, `links` and the map arrays to their final sizes before its loops.

**Pattern 4 — unused assignments `checkcode` flags. None.** `config.m` and `setup.m` are `checkcode`-clean, and `texture_grouping.m`'s single message is `CLALL` on `clear all` at `:56`, which is O3.7 and not this pattern. One genuinely-unused assignment does exist and is deliberately **not** touched: `texture_grouping.m`'s `n_images = 60`, whose only reader is the commented-out `% image_num = randi(n_images);`. `checkcode` does not flag it, because it suppresses `NASGU` in scripts — the same gap 0.2.2 recorded for `effective_distance.m` and `find_tex_regions.m` — so it falls outside this pattern by its own definition. It is also tied to that block's *alternative* triage verdict (section 3.1.4): Stage 3 is to convert the block, not drop it, and deleting the variable now would break what Stage 3 has been told to revive. Same shape of reasoning as 0.2.1's `bd_strip_rep`.

**Golden-harness replay, verbatim:**

```
REPLAY PASS
```

Run once at the end, after 0.2.7's edits. `config()` is covered by `config_path_fields` (8 field names, sorted), `config_optics`, `config_rgb_to_lms_checksum`/`_size`, `config_norm` and `config_seed` — all unchanged, which is the expected and required result: nothing in Stage 2 should be able to move `config()`, and this tranche did not edit it at all. Step 0's decision 2 still holds, paths recorded by field *name* only. No separate before/after `isequal(config_HEAD(), config())` run was needed this time, unlike tranche 9's Stage 1 pass: `config.m`'s diff against HEAD is empty. `setup.m` and `texture_grouping.m` have no harness coverage at all, as recorded above.

**Nothing else.** No new bug, structural or speed finding; every item already recorded against these three files (B2.2, B3.22, S2.4, S2.9, S3.5-S3.8, O1.3, O3.7, O3.8) was left untouched.

**Stage 2 status at the close of this tranche.** 0.2.0, 0.2.1, 0.2.2, 0.2.5, 0.2.6, 0.2.7 and 0.2.8 are ticked; **0.2.3 (`+grouping` stimulus scripts) and 0.2.4 (root cluster-C files) are still open**, so section 0.2 stays 🚧 rather than closing. Recorded explicitly because this tranche was dispatched as "the last piece of Stage 2", which the checklist does not bear out.

<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h5 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:0.92em">S2.4 — route every data path through <code>cfg.paths.*</code></h5></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

Run 2026-09-21, **MATLAB R2024b**. Working tree clean at the start (`git status`). Done out of tier order, at the user's request, before the Stage 2 plan is rewritten. **`config.m` was not touched**, so S2.9 and S3.6 are untouched too; the problem was never `config()`, which already built correct absolute paths to both halves of the shared store — it was that nothing called it.

**The item as recorded named three Brodatz path spellings in two files. The exhaustive grep found the same problem in nine files, across roughly two dozen path expressions, so the fix is wider than the item's text.** The search was `grep -rn "vislab-common/data\|img_data\|'data/\|\"data/\|exp_files/" --include=*.m .` plus a second sweep over every `imread(`, `load(`, `save(` and `dir(` call in the repo, both run over the whole tree including `tools/` — there are no excluded ancestral-code directories inside this repo to miss. Four sites the item did not know about turned up that way: `+grouping/mk_texseg_stim_points.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m` and `tex_regions.m` each build the same bare `append('B', num_str, '.gif')` that tranche 9 had recorded for `texture_grouping.m` alone. Leaving them would have made a ticked S2.4 false, so they are in.

**Every path changed, old → new.** `cfg = config();` was added to each file that did not already have it — at the top for a script (after its `clearvars`/`clear all`, which would otherwise wipe it), just above the read for the two functions.

| file | old | new |
|---|---|---|
| `+general/nat_near_far_patches_bayes.m` | `addpath('C:\Users\Bill Geisler\...\Texture Discrimination Code')` | deleted; replaced by a comment naming `aply_otf`/`rgb2lms`/`dsmp` and pointing at B2.12 |
| " | `addpath('C:\Users\Bill Geisler\...\CPS Set-9-10-12_16-bit linear')` | deleted; the three `imread`s below now build absolute paths instead |
| " | `fullfile(fileparts(mfilename('fullpath')), '..', '..', 'vislab-common/data', 'cps_rgb2lms.mat')` | `fullfile(cfg.paths.data_root, 'cps_rgb2lms.mat')` |
| " | `append('Set9_16_', num_str, '.png')` (and `Set10_`, `Set12_`) | `fullfile(cfg.paths.natural_images, append(...))` |
| " | `append('patch_pairs_9', num_str, '.mat')` (and `_10`, `_12`), written to the current folder | `fullfile(cfg.paths.stimuli, append(...))` |
| " | `load("cdfs_abr_mo13_mo23_cs33[_otf].mat")` | **unchanged**, see below |
| `+general/simulate_discrimination.m` | `load('vislab-common/data/nat_im_eff_coding_bins.mat')` | `load(fullfile(cfg.paths.data_root, 'nat_im_eff_coding.mat'))` |
| " | `tex_dir = 'img_data/brodatz/'`, `dir([tex_dir '*.' file_type])`, `[tex_dir files(i).name]` | `fullfile(cfg.paths.textures, 'brodatz')`, `dir(fullfile(tex_dir, ...))`, `fullfile(tex_dir, ...)` |
| " | `load('vislab-common/data/eff_coding_bins.mat')` | `load(fullfile(cfg.paths.data_root, 'nat_im_eff_coding.mat'))` |
| " | `load('data/model/texture discrimination boundaries.mat')` | `load(fullfile(cfg.paths.model, 'texture discrimination boundaries.mat'))` |
| " | `['img_data/brodatz/B', num2str(tex_a), '.gif']` ×2 | `fullfile(cfg.paths.textures, 'brodatz', ['B', num2str(tex_a), '.gif'])` |
| `texture_grouping.m` | `append('B', num_str, '.gif')` | `fullfile(cfg.paths.textures, 'brodatz', append(...))` |
| `+lib/texture_patch.m` | `['vislab-common/data/textures/brodatz/B' num2str(tex_num) '.gif']` | `fullfile(cfg.paths.textures, 'brodatz', ['B' num2str(tex_num) '.gif'])` |
| `+grouping/mk_trl_points.m` | `'vislab-common/data/textures/{brodatz,fabric,pertex}/...'`, three branches | `fullfile(cfg.paths.textures, '<set>', ...)`, three branches |
| `+grouping/mk_texseg_stim_points.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m`, `tex_regions.m` | `append('B', num_str, '.gif')`, one site each | `fullfile(cfg.paths.textures, 'brodatz', append(...))` |

`+general/Contents.m` was updated too, since its entries stated that these paths do not resolve.

**Verification — every new path was resolved on disk, read-only, in a headless R2024b batch** (`isfolder`/`isfile`, no pipeline run, no writes). Verbatim:

```
config resolved to: ...\texture-segmentation\config.m

isfolder=1  natural_images  ...\texture-segmentation\..\vislab-common\data\CPS natural images
isfolder=1  textures        ...\texture-segmentation\..\vislab-common\data\textures

isfile=1  ...\vislab-common\data\textures\brodatz\B60.gif

isfile=1  ...\vislab-common\data\CPS natural images\Set10_16_90.png

Every path the fix builds resolves. The three CPS counts match the script's hardcoded `n_img9 = 104; n_img10 = 90; n_img12 = 197` exactly, and the 60 Brodatz sheets match `n_tex = 60`, so the destinations are the right ones and not merely existing folders. The only `isfile=0` rows are the two B2.13 cdfs files, which were deliberately not repointed.

**The two files the fix did not repoint, and why.**

- **B2.13's cdfs loads — left exactly as written, with a comment.** Tranche 8 said neither file exists anywhere; searching the whole `Geisler Lab` tree rather than just `vislab-common` shows `cdfs_abr_mo13_mo23_cs33_otf.mat` **does** exist, in `../texture-learning/Bill's old code/` and `.../Bill's old code - revised/`. It is not in the shared store, so there is no `cfg.paths.*` field that names it, and inventing one — or copying the artifact into the store — is a decision about where lab data lives, not a path-routing fix. `whos -file` on it shows 25 variables (`Na`, `Nb`, `Nm`, `No`, `Nmo`, `Ncs1/2/4`, `ea`, `eb`, ...), **none** of which the script reads, so the load looks vestigial and deleting it may be the real answer. All of this is written into B2.13 and into a comment at the load itself; the decision is left open for the user.
- **B3.1's `exp_files/norm/...` loads — untouched by instruction**, and correctly so: they are not a shared-store problem but a nonexistent subdirectory. The eight *other* `exp_files` strings in `+experiment` are the same convention gap for the repo's own store and are now recorded as **S3.9** rather than folded in here.

**`setup.m` — confirmed obsolete to change, and not changed.** Tranche 9 established that `setup.m` puts only `../vislab-common` and the repo root on the path, never the data store. The question this item had to answer was whether anything still needs the store literally on the path after the fix. It does not: every remaining data access in the repo is now either an absolute path built from `cfg.paths.*`, or a repo-relative `exp_files/...` string that needs the repo root as the working directory and never touched the store (S3.9), or B2.13's two cdfs loads — and adding `../vislab-common/data` to the path would not help those, since neither file is there. So `setup.m` stays as it is, and the `Note` its header already carries about not adding the data store is now a statement about something nothing depends on.

**Golden-harness replay: passes.** `golden_harness('replay', 'tools/golden_ref.mat')` returns without throwing. Expected, and it is the point: the harness covers `config()` and the pure helpers, none of the ten edited files, so a pass confirms nothing it *does* cover moved. The reference was **not** recaptured; `golden_harness('capture', ...)` was not run.

**`checkcode`: unchanged from the pre-edit baseline, message for message** — `simulate_discrimination.m` 3 (`NOPTS` ×2 at 133/185, `NASGU` at 249), `texture_grouping.m` 1 (`CLALL`), and 0 in the other eight files, which is exactly what tranches 8 and 9 recorded. `mtree` parses all ten files without error. Three lines over 100 characters remain, all pre-existing and none introduced here: `simulate_discrimination.m:79` (the commented-out `eff_coding_bins` line, 142 chars, awaiting its own triage verdict) and `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m:215-216` (111 each).

**Judgement calls worth recording.**

1. **`cfg = config()` inside the function, rather than a new argument.** `+lib/texture_patch.m` and `+grouping/mk_trl_points.m` are the only two functions in the set; the rest are scripts. Adding a `tex_dir` parameter would have widened `texture_patch`'s `inputParser` (which S2.7 is already going to rewrite) and `mk_trl_points`'s nine-argument signature, and would have pushed the path decision onto callers that currently pass none. A zero-argument `config()` call inside the function keeps the change to one line and matches what the sibling repos do. In `mk_trl_points` the call sits outside the per-region loop, so it happens once per trial, not once per region.
2. **Cell 3 of `simulate_discrimination.m` calls `config()` a second time, deliberately.** The file's three cells are written to be run independently — cell 3 re-declares every constant cell 1 sets — so relying on cell 1 to have left `cfg` in the workspace would break the one cell that is most likely to be run alone.
3. **Generated patch pairs go to `cfg.paths.stimuli`.** `config.m`'s own header calls that field "derived patch sets, under `data/`", and `data/stimuli/textures/patch_pairs.mat` is already the texture-side equivalent of what this script writes for natural images. `cfg.paths.data` is the whole `data/` folder and `cfg.paths.model` is for fitted boundaries, so neither fits. No new subfolder was invented.
4. **The dead `addpath` to `Texture Discrimination Code` was deleted, not left or commented out.** It is B2.3's own subject — a path that exists on nobody's machine — and the thing it was reaching for (B2.12's three functions) is a separate item that a stale `addpath` does nothing to advance. A comment in its place names the three functions and cites B2.12, so the information survives the deletion.

**Found while doing this, recorded and not acted on.** 🟡 **B3.22** — `../camouflage_detection/config.m` is a second zero-argument `config()` on this machine's saved MATLAB path, so a bare `config()` resolves by path order; this item's own verification script tripped over it when `run()` changed the working directory. Ten files now depend on that resolution where none did before. 🟡 **S3.9** — the eight bare `exp_files/...` strings in `+experiment`, the same convention gap for the repo's own store.

**Items closed or refined by this work.** **S2.4** ticked. **B2.3** and **B2.14** ticked in section 0.4 as fixed here, each with a note saying so rather than a Stage 4 entry of their own. **B2.13** refined, not closed — one of its two files was found, in ancestral code, and what it contains is now recorded. Section 0.2's Status line, section 1's harness-coverage note and section 3.2's `+general` bullet all said the natural-image set may be absent from this machine; all three are corrected — it is present, and verified.

</div>

| file | old parameter | new parameter | old local | new local | call sites updated |

| `texture_patch.m` | `'cont'` | `'contrast'` | `cont` | `contrast` | `run_demo.m` (5 sites: lines 91/93/96/98/191), `tools/golden_harness.m` (2 sites: lines 274/285) |

`'ppd'` was deliberately **not** expanded anywhere — the item's own guidance says to check precedent, and `config.m`'s `cfg.optics.ppd` field plus every other `'ppd'` name-value pair in the repo (`+experiment/+discriminate/+prep/setup_experiment.m:49`, `+experiment/+grouping/+prep/setup_experiment.m:122` — both unrelated structs, not `+lib` callers, confirmed by reading) already treat it as an established term.

`steerable_grad.m`'s `'filter'` did not become `'apply_filter'` like the other two, because its value is a prebuilt kernel pair from `LIB.STEERABLE_FILTER`, not a boolean toggle — `'apply_filter'` would misdescribe it. `'filter_kernel'` matches what the header already called it ("a prebuilt kernel pair") and lets the local go back to mirroring the parameter's full spelling now that the parameter itself no longer shadows the builtin.

**Caller sweep — whole-repo, both by identifier and by quoted string.** Ran `grep -rn "'<old_name>'"` for all eight names, including `'filter'` and `'ppd'` (which needed the check even though `'ppd'` wasn't renamed, to make sure nothing outside `+experiment`'s unrelated structs was affected), across every `.m` file and every non-`.m` file (`docs/`, no `.mlx`/README hits). Confirmed via 3.3's S2.4 entry, re-verified directly here, that `lib.downsample`/`lib.downsample_old` have zero callers in the repo, so their five renamed parameters needed no call-site update at all. `tools/golden_harness.m`'s own calls to `lib.texture_patch` counted as call sites and were updated (`'patch_sz'`→`'patch_size'`, `'cont'`→`'contrast'`, plus one prose comment on line 265 that named `'lum'`). No dynamic references (`feval`/`str2func`/`exist`/`which`) to any of the eight names exist anywhere in the repo.

<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h5 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:0.92em">S2.6 — one shared setup call for the patch-pair geometry</h5></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

Run 2026-09-21, **MATLAB R2024b**. Re-located the item by name, not by line number, as instructed: the recorded lines had drifted again (`mk_texseg_session.m:75-80`, `mk_texseg_stim_points.m:68-73`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m:57-62`), but the finding itself was still exactly as recorded.

**The premise was re-checked before anything was changed, and the three sites really are identical.** All three set `grid_size = 16`, `bin_bounds_dist = [1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32]`, `bin_bounds_min_ecc = [0, 1, 2, 4, 8, 12]`, `bin_bounds_delta_ecc = [0, 2, 4, 6, 8, 12]` — the same literals, in the same order — and then make the same four calls, in the same order, with those arguments and nothing else. No extra argument, no differing `grid_size`, so there is no discrepancy to report and nothing that a merge could silently change.

**What was built: `+grouping/mk_pair_geometry.m`, a four-output function.**

```matlab
[dist, min_ecc, delta_ecc, bin_index] = grouping.mk_pair_geometry(grid_size, ...
    bin_bounds_dist, bin_bounds_min_ecc, bin_bounds_delta_ecc)
```

Its whole body is the four calls the three consumers used to make, in their original order. Each consumer's seven-line block (four calls, a blank line and two comments) becomes one call plus its comment. `+grouping/Contents.m` gained the matching entry, so `help grouping` still lists every file in the package.

**Why this shape, over the alternatives.**

1. **Four outputs, not a struct.** A struct would have been the tidier-looking return, but every consumer immediately passes these four into `grouping.find_bin(patch_index1, patch_index2, dist, min_ecc, delta_ecc, ...)` positionally — so a struct either forces `geom.dist`-style spelling into the nine-argument `find_bin` calls, or forces four unpacking lines at each site, which would re-add exactly the lines this item exists to remove. Four outputs keeps every downstream variable name and every `find_bin` call byte-identical, which is also what makes the diff reviewable. `grouping.mk_masks` already returns `[masks, maps]`, so multiple outputs are the local idiom.
2. **A new shared helper, not one consumer made canonical.** Option (b) — have the other two call `mk_texseg_session` — cannot work here: the two consumers are scripts that are *known broken* (B2.6), they need the four matrices in their own workspace rather than the session struct `mk_texseg_session` returns, and routing them through it would change what they do, not just where the setup lives.
3. **No signature change to anything that already had callers.** `mk_dist`, `mk_mecc`, `mk_decc` and `mk_bindex` are untouched — same files, same signatures, same golden-harness coverage. The only new call-site surface is the one new name.
4. **The name.** `mk_pair_geometry` follows the package's `mk_*` convention and tranche 3's rule C-3 (spell the vocabulary out rather than half-abbreviate); everything it returns is indexed by patch pair or by pair-geometry bin. `which('mk_pair_geometry', '-all')` and `which('grouping.mk_pair_geometry', '-all')` were both empty before the file was created, so it shadows nothing in this repo, a sibling repo, or MATLAB itself.

**Call sites found, and how the search was made exhaustive.** `grep -rn "mk_dist\|mk_mecc\|mk_decc\|mk_bindex"` over the *whole* repo, not just `.m` files and not just `+grouping/` (there are no excluded ancestral-code directories inside this repo). That found **five** places, not three, and two of them were deliberately left alone:

- The three the item names — all three converted.
- `run_demo.m:226-229` — the same four calls with the same values, **left as they are, deliberately.** `run_demo.m` is the repo's teaching script: its section 4 narrates the binning pipeline helper by helper, names all four in its own header and in `README.md:84`, and its value is precisely that a reader sees the four separate calls. Collapsing it into one call would make the demo worse at the only job it has. Recorded here rather than silently skipped.
- `tools/golden_harness.m:86-103` — **must not be converted.** It calls each of the four separately in order to checksum each one's output separately; routing it through the new helper would fold four independent reference fields into one code path and weaken exactly the coverage those fields exist to give.

`mk_texseg_session` itself has one caller (`+experiment/+grouping/+prep/setup_experiment.m:62`), unaffected — its signature did not change. The two scripts have no callers at all. Grepped for dynamic references too (`feval`/`str2func`/`exist`/`which` with any of the four names quoted): none anywhere.

**B2.6 and B2.7 are still present and unchanged — this was an explicit constraint, and here is how it was kept.**

- **B2.6.** The two scripts' calls to the new helper are written **unqualified**, exactly like every other helper call in those files: `[dist, min_ecc, delta_ecc, bin_index] = mk_pair_geometry(grid_size, ...)`, with no `grouping.` prefix, and a comment at the site saying so. Verified empirically the same way B2.6 originally was: in R2024b from the repo root, `which('mk_pair_geometry')` is **empty** and `which('mk_dist')` is **empty**, while `which('grouping.mk_pair_geometry')` resolves — so the new call fails to resolve for precisely the same reason the four old ones did. Both scripts still error on their *first* helper call (`mk_texs`, several lines above the geometry block), so the geometry block is unreachable either way and the observable behaviour of running either script is byte-identical to before: the same error, on the same line. B2.6's entry in section 0.4 was updated to name `mk_pair_geometry` in place of the four, so the bug's record stays accurate; the bug itself is untouched and still open.
- **B2.7.** Lives in `mk_texseg_stim_points.m`'s contrast-block loop (`for i_subblock = i_group:block_size:contrast_block_size-1`), ~130 lines below the geometry block. Not touched, not adjacent to anything touched, and re-read after the edit to confirm the missing `i_group+` offset is still missing.

**Verification — four checks, since none of the three consumers is golden-covered.**

1. **Side-by-side `isequal`, old block vs new call**, run before the call sites were converted, on the real `grid_size = 16` case and on a synthetic `grid_size = 7` with different bin bounds. Verbatim:

```
grid_size=16 : dist 1  min_ecc 1  delta_ecc 1  bin_index 1  (1 = isequal)
  classes: double double double double ; sizes [256 256] [256 256] [256 256] [5 5 5]
grid_size=7 : dist 1  min_ecc 1  delta_ecc 1  bin_index 1  (1 = isequal)
  classes: double double double double ; sizes [49 49] [49 49] [49 49] [3 2 3]
RNG state unchanged: 1
S2.6 SIDE-BY-SIDE PASSED
```

2. **End-to-end on the one consumer that actually runs.** The pre-edit `mk_texseg_session.m` was taken from HEAD, renamed to `mk_texseg_session_before`, and run beside the edited one in the same session (both seed `rng(0)` internally, so the comparison is deterministic). Every field of the returned struct — including `cuelocs` and `tperm`, which depend on the whole RNG draw order — came back `isequal`:

```
before 1.4 s, after 0.9 s
same field names in same order: 1
  cntrst   isequal=1
  cuelocs  isequal=1
  m0       isequal=1
  maps     isequal=1
  pw       isequal=1
  sz       isequal=1
  texs     isequal=1
  tex_set  isequal=1
  tperm    isequal=1
S2.6 END-TO-END mk_texseg_session PASSED (isequal on the whole struct)
```

This is the strongest evidence available for this item: the four pure calls are made in the same order and draw no random numbers, so the session's RNG stream is untouched, and the struct equality confirms it rather than assuming it. (The timing difference is run-to-run noise on a first-call JIT, not a speed claim — S2.6 is not a Speed item.)

3. **Golden-harness replay: passes, unchanged.** `golden_harness('replay', 'tools/golden_ref.mat')` returned with no assertion thrown, exit status 0. Expected: the harness covers `mk_dist`/`mk_mecc`/`mk_decc`/`mk_bindex` individually and none of their signatures changed, so a pass confirms this item did not disturb the coverage it inherits. The reference was **not** recaptured; `golden_harness('capture', ...)` was not run.

```
golden_harness replay: PASSED (isequal assert did not throw)
```

4. **`checkcode` clean** on all five touched files (`mk_pair_geometry.m`, `mk_texseg_session.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_points.m`, `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m`, `Contents.m`) — zero messages, same as before the edit — and `canon` parses all four `.m` files without error. No new line exceeds 100 characters (longest new line: 80, the new file's `function` line) and no trailing whitespace was introduced. `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m:213`'s pre-existing 111-character commented line is untouched, as recorded in section 3.3's S2.4 entry.

**Items touched in the plan, other than this one.** **O3.1** and **O3.2** both said they were "tangled with S2.6, since the same rebuild happens at three call sites"; both write-ups were corrected to the current state without acting on either — O3.1's loops now have one driver instead of three, while O3.2 is unaffected, because S2.6 merged only the *setup* calls and each consumer still calls `find_bin` from its own bin-table loop. The vectorization those two items describe was deliberately left alone for Stage 5. **S3.1** (whether `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m` should be deleted at all) is unchanged and still open; if it is later deleted, one of this item's three call sites goes with it, which costs nothing.

</div>

</details>

<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h5 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:0.92em">S1.2 — <code>find_xy</code> calls <code>check_xy</code> instead of inlining it</h5></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

Run 2026-10-05, **MATLAB R2024b**. Baseline: HEAD for `+grouping/find_xy.m`. The edit itself was made by an earlier, interrupted session and left uncommitted; this entry re-verifies it rather than taking it on trust. The rest of the working tree carried unrelated S2.1 work-in-progress, which is not part of this commit.

**The change.** The 40-line inline boundary test inside `find_xy`'s `while` loop became one line, `is_boundary = grouping.check_xy(x, y, trial, maps, grid_size);`. The header sentence that said the test was "written out inline here" now says it is delegated to `GROUPING.CHECK_XY`. Nothing else in the file changed; the signature is the same.

**Why it is output-identical.** The removed block was compared line by line against `+grouping/check_xy.m`'s body: the same `region = maps(x, y, trial)` read, the same `is_boundary = 0` reset, and the same eight neighbour tests in the same order with the same edge guards. The only difference is indentation. `check_xy` draws no random numbers, and the two `randi(grid_size)` calls stay in the loop exactly where they were, so the RNG stream is untouched. The inline copy's `region` local is gone from `find_xy`'s workspace, but it was never returned or read after the test.

**Call sites.** `grep -rn "find_xy"` over the whole repo (there are no excluded ancestral-code directories inside it): `+grouping/mk_texseg_session.m:106,112` (package-qualified, the one live path), and the unqualified calls in `mk_texseg_stim_points.m` and `mk_texseg_stim_shape_old.m` (still broken by B2.6, unchanged). None needed editing, since the signature did not change.

**Golden-harness replay: passes.** The harness does not cover `find_xy` (its header says so), but it does cover `check_xy`, which is untouched. Verbatim tail of the run:

```
golden_harness replay: PASSED (isequal assert did not throw)
```

The reference was **not** recaptured. `checkcode` on `find_xy.m`: 0 messages, and `mtree` parses it.

</div>

</details>

<details style="margin:0.7em 0 0.7em 1.5em">
<summary><h5 style="display:inline; margin:0; font-size:0.92em">S2.1 — merge the near-duplicate <code>+experiment</code> files, or decide not to</h5></summary>

<div style="margin-left:1.5em">

Run 2026-10-05, **MATLAB R2024b**. Started by an earlier, interrupted session, which merged `fixation_interval.m` and `response_interval.m` and left that uncommitted; finished and committed here.

**Baseline.** HEAD for all twelve files (six pairs), taken with `git show HEAD:<path>`. The three pairs this session merged or judged (`load_current_session.m`, `load_stimuli.m`, `+prep/setup_subject.m`) were clean at HEAD. The other three were not: both `fixation_interval.m`/`response_interval.m` copies had already been moved or deleted, and both `run_experiment.m` files edited. For those, every comparison below is against the HEAD copies, not against the uncommitted versions.

**The rule used to decide each pair.** Merge only where the two copies are the same code apart from a difference that one explicit argument can carry, and keep each tree's behaviour exactly, bugs included. Where a merge would need a flag whose only job is to keep a recorded bug alive, or a branch around most of the body, do not merge.

| file pair | what differs at HEAD (code, not headers) | decision |
|---|---|---|
| `+run/fixation_interval.m` | The grouping copy has the trailing blank commented out: four lines that read `blankIntervalS`, fill the fixation rect with `bgPixValGamma`, flip, and wait. Stage 1 triage verdict: *alternative*. | **Merged** into `+experiment/+run/fixation_interval.m`, with a new `blank_after` argument. The discriminate tree passes `true`, the grouping tree `false`. |
| `+run/response_interval.m` | The grouping copy has no ESC-abort branch (B3.15). | **Merged** into `+experiment/+run/response_interval.m`, with a new `allow_abort` argument. The discriminate tree passes `true`, the grouping tree `false`. |
| `+run/run_experiment.m` | Only the package prefix on each call (`experiment.discriminate.run.*` vs `experiment.grouping.run.*`). | **Not merged.** Each file is that tree's wiring: which loader and which hooks to use. A shared copy would take the tree as a parameter and rebuild these same names, which is the same code in a harder-to-read form. Both now call the two shared hooks. |
| `+run/load_stimuli.m` | About 30 of its ~70 code lines: one stimulus position instead of two, no `'joined'` layout branch, no gamma correction of the stimuli, no target outline, a different fixation-cross gray (0.5 vs 0.7 of background), and a different output struct. The grouping copy also uses an undefined `bit_depth_out` (B2.8). | **Not merged.** The two build different display structs, so a merge would be a branch around half the body, and the grouping half would have to keep an undefined variable to preserve B2.8. The choice of loader is also already carried by the data: each tree's `setup_experiment.m` stores a handle to its own `load_stimuli` in `exp_settings.loadSessionStimuli`, which is saved into `exp_files/<type>/exp_settings.mat`, and `run_experiment.m` calls through it. Moving either file would change the name those saved handles need. (I did not open the shipped `.mat` files to see which name they hold.) |
| `+run/load_current_session.m` | `n_levels` from `subject_file.response` instead of `subject_file.idx` (B3.14), and the `stimuli`/`diffpair` slicing commented out (triage verdict *unfinished*, the cause of B2.9). | **Not merged; filed as S3.10.** Both differences come from the grouping subject file having no `idx`, so a merge would need a flag that exists only to keep B2.9. |
| `+prep/setup_subject.m` | The `idx` build commented out (triage verdict *alternative*), and `response` made `false(...)` instead of `zeros(..., 'int8')`. | **Not merged; part of S3.10**, for the same reason. |

**Why an argument, not a setting, for the two merged hooks.** The interrupted session had merged both files as the discriminate tree's code, unchanged, reasoning that the blank's length is already a setting (`blankIntervalMs`: 0 for discriminate, 50 for grouping). That silently changed the grouping tree twice. It gained a screen blank and a 50 ms wait on every trial, and it gained the ESC branch, which fixes B3.15. Neither is allowed in a structure item. A setting cannot carry the blank difference either: with `blankIntervalMs` at 0 the blank still fills the rect and flips the screen. So each hook takes one logical, passed explicitly by each tree's `run_experiment.m`. For the blank, this is also exactly what an *alternative* triage verdict asks for: the disabled code becomes a documented option instead of dead code. Both arguments are temporary in spirit. Once someone decides whether the grouping experiment should blank and abort, each argument can be dropped by making the two trees pass the same value.

**Why each tree's behaviour is unchanged.**

- Discriminate, `fixation_interval(s, true)`: `if true` around the same four lines.
- Discriminate, `response_interval(s, true)`: `elseif true && key_code(KbName('ESCAPE'))`, which equals HEAD's `elseif (key_code(KbName('ESCAPE')))` for the scalar `key_code` element.
- Grouping, `fixation_interval(s, false)`: the four lines are skipped, matching HEAD where they were commented out. `blankIntervalS` and `bgPixValGamma` are not read, also matching HEAD.
- Grouping, `response_interval(s, false)`: `&&` short-circuits, so the ESC test is never made, matching HEAD where the branch did not exist.

Apart from the two guards, the code in both shared files is the discriminate tree's HEAD code line for line (compared with all comment lines removed).

**Call sites, and how the search was made exhaustive.** `grep -rn "fixation_interval\|response_interval\|load_current_session\|load_stimuli\|setup_subject"` over the whole repo, all file types, excluding only `.git/` and this plan. The two shared hooks are called only from the two `run_experiment.m` files, both updated. The four files left in place keep all their callers: both `run_experiment.m` (`load_current_session`), the two `exp_settings.loadSessionStimuli` handles (`load_stimuli`), and no code caller at all for `setup_subject`, which is run by hand. Nothing calls the hooks by string (`feval`, `str2func`). The vislab harness calls them only through the `hooks.*` closures built in `run_experiment.m`. `README.md` already describes the new package as "a shared +run for the hooks both experiments use", which still holds.

**Open bugs the merged and unmerged files still contain, unchanged.** In the shared hooks: B3.15 (preserved via `allow_abort = false`), B3.16 and B3.23. In the files left per tree: B2.8, B2.9, B2.10, B2.11, B3.14. All of their entries in section 0.4 are still accurate. B3.15, B3.16 and B3.23 got a sentence giving the new file location.

**Found while doing this, recorded and not acted on.** 🟡 **B3.24**: `setup_subject.m`'s `idx` loop indexes level columns by session number. Harmless now, since discriminate has 4 levels and 4 sessions, but the grouping tree's disabled copy would break if re-enabled. 🟡 **S3.10**: the deferred merge of `load_current_session.m` and `setup_subject.m`.

**Verification.** There is no runnable path: all of `+experiment` needs Psychtoolbox and a display, and both trees die at B2.10 before any hook runs. So the check is the line-by-line reading above, plus:

- `checkcode` on all four edited `.m` files: `fixation_interval.m` 0, both `run_experiment.m` 0, `response_interval.m` 2 (`STOUT` on `rt`, which is B3.16, and `NASGU` on `response = -1`, which is B3.23). These are the same two messages both HEAD copies carried, and nothing new. `mtree` parses all four.
- No line over 100 characters in any edited file.
- `golden_harness('replay', 'tools/golden_ref.mat')` passes. It covers none of `+experiment` (its header says so), so this only confirms nothing it covers moved. Verbatim tail:

```
golden_harness replay: PASSED (isequal assert did not throw)
```

The reference was **not** recaptured.

**Docs updated.** `+experiment/Contents.m` (new `+run` entry, and why the other files stay per tree), new `+experiment/+run/Contents.m`, both trees' `Contents.m` and `+run/Contents.m` (the two hooks moved out), and `README.md` (layout). The interrupted session's note in S1.1 about the shared package is kept.
